# Introduction: Welcome to the Shattered Expanse

*The world did not end in fire. It ended in a flood of magic.*

Welcome to the **Wayfinder Sourcebook**, a comprehensive campaign setting and rules expansion for the Pathfinder Roleplaying Game (1st Edition). This book transforms your tabletop experience from traditional high-fantasy dungeon crawling into a high-stakes, nautical, magic-punk adventure. 

## The Shattered Expanse
Centuries ago, the precursor civilization of Aethelgard pushed magical industrialization too far. Their relentless harvesting of Aetherâ€”the raw, physical manifestation of magicâ€”destabilized the earth's crust. Continents sank, oceans boiled, and the world was flooded. 

Today, the Shattered Expanse is an endless, violent ocean dotted with the volcanic peaks of drowned continents. It is a world where survival depends on the strength of your hull, the loyalty of your crew, and the firepower of your cannons.

## Themes of the Setting
When running a campaign in the Expanse, DMs and players should lean into the following themes:
1. **Nautical Magic-Punk:** Magic is not just whispered incantations; it is industry. Glowing blue Aether crystals power massive ironclad engines, replace lost limbs with mechanical prosthetics, and fuel devastating ship-to-ship weaponry.
2. **Deep-Sea Horror:** The depths are lightless and unforgiving. The magical radiation of the old world has twisted the local fauna into colossal Leviathans, and the darkness hides the insidious, flesh-warping Drow who rule the trenches.
3. **Ruthless Piracy & Politics:** There is no shining kingdom of good. The Crimson Dominion enforces its will through brutal inquisition, the Merchant Consortium values gold above human life, and the Free Captains are chaotic opportunists. Morality is a luxury on the high seas.

## How to Use This Book
This sourcebook is divided into five parts:
*   **Part I: Character Creation (Ch 1-10)** introduces the new Wayfinder class, thematic races, and the four unique magic systems (Aether-tech, Biomancy, Consortium Ledger-Magic, and Sea-Witchcraft).
*   **Part II: World & Lore (Ch 11-20)** details the history of the world and provides deep dives into the major factions.
*   **Part III: The Naval Rulebook (Ch 21-30)** contains robust, playtested rules for ship-to-ship combat, vessel upgrades, and specific Faction ship statblocks.
*   **Part IV: Threats & Treasures (Ch 31-40)** provides a massive bestiary of sea monsters, faction forces, and a vault of powerful magic items and cursed artifacts.
*   **Part V: The Gazetteer & Adventures (Ch 41-50)** maps out the major ports of call and provides four pre-written adventure modules to start your campaign immediately.

Hoist the sails, load the Aether-cannons, and prepare to chart the Unwritten Sea. The Expanse awaits.

# Chapter 1: Campaign Framework

## The World: The Shattered Expanse
Nearly 800 years ago came **The Sundering Tide**. No one knows exactly what happenedâ€”whether the gods went to war, an elder civilization awakened something beneath the crust, or the oceans became sentient. The world shattered into tens of thousands of islands. New islands emerge, entire nations drift, and maps are never permanent.

Only about 35% of the world's oceans have reliable charts. The rest is **The Unwritten Sea**.

## Core Campaign Blend
This campaign is an exploration sandbox blending "Age of Sail" themes with "High Fantasy" elements:
- **The Uncharted Ocean (40%)**: The world is not fully known. Maps are wrong. The primary gameplay loop is Rumor â†’ Expedition â†’ Discovery â†’ Map â†’ Route â†’ Trade â†’ Conflict.
- **The Great Magical Trade Network (35%)**: The economic engine. Discoveries matter because new resources (e.g., Moon Coral) create trade opportunities, drawing merchants, pirates, and governments.
- **The Pirate Renaissance (20%)**: Pirates are the first people willing to go where governments cannot. The era creates factionsâ€”traditional raiders, pirate nobility, independent explorers.
- **The Underdark Ocean (15%)**: A massive subterranean ecosystem containing ancient cities, drowned kingdoms, and alien ecosystems like the Aboleth Archives and Drow courts.
- **The Lost Civilization Mystery (100% Underlying Engine)**: Why are oceans unexplored? Because someone made them unexplorable. Everything connects back to **The First Captains**.

## The First Captains
The original explorers. The first people to bond with ships and spirits. They mastered the connection between Arcane, Psionics, Spirits, and the Ocean. At some point in history, they vanished. 

> [!NOTE] 
> **Level 20 Capstone Quest:** Upon reaching level 20, a Wayfinder is offered a legendary quest to "Restore the First Captains." This world-altering choice asks the player to restore, replace, hide, or release the greatest explorers in history.

## The Four Magic Systems
Magic enhances naval warfare rather than replacing it. Ships remain the kings of warfare.
1. **Arcane (The Pact)**: Traditional magic and warlock pacts. Power borrowed from sea devils, leviathans, living storms, or star beings. "I borrowed power from something older than the world."
2. **Psionics (The Mind)**: Power originating from consciousness itself. Some surface kingdoms fear it; the greatest psionic cities lie beneath the earth. "I discovered that my own consciousness is a weapon."
3. **Spirit (The Tide)**: Primal sea magic. Not worship, but alignment with the ocean's natural order. "I learned the hidden laws of the sea itself."
4. **Deep Magic**: Predates all three. It changes gravity, distance, probability, and time. It is the source of many impossible places and artifacts.

## Major Factions
The ocean is a deeply political ecosystem:
- **The Free Captains Alliance (Blackwake Harbor)**: A pirate frontier valuing exploration and independence.
- **The Grand Merchant Consortium (Port Meridian)**: A neutral trade power that doesn't own the city, just everything inside it.
- **The Crimson Dominion (The Crimson Citadel)**: A wealthy arcane empire built on slavery, magical superiority, and the exploitation of conquered peoples. They view Wayfinders and new routes as economic threats.
- **The Drowned Silk Houses (Nymorath Deep Harbor)**: An ancient Drow Underdark Ocean civilization. They raid the surface for resources, ancient claims, and political warfare. They remember the First Captains.
- **The Spirit Keepers (The Last Harbor)**: A hidden sanctuary of guardians preserving old traditions, Navigator Spirits, and First Captain lore.
- **Independent Salvagers (Salvager's Haven)**: A frontier port of wreck hunters, explorers, and forgotten discoveries.
# Chapter 2: Starting Origins

Every Wayfinder campaign begins with a shipwreck. The party does not start with a ship; they start with survival. 

## The Tide's Choice
There are two opening methods to determine where and how the players begin their adventure.

### Method A: The Captain's Choice
*â€œI chose where I wanted to go. The sea decided how I arrived.â€*

The player chooses their **Home Port** and starting faction environment. 
The sea (via random dice rolls or GM selection) determines:
- Shipwreck Cause
- Rescue Vessel
- Rescue Circumstances
- Initial Reputation

### Method B: The Sea's Choice
*â€œI surrendered my destination, but I wrote my legend.â€*

The player rolls randomly for their **Home Port**, letting fate decide their starting faction, local allies, and initial enemies. 
In return, the player chooses:
- Shipwreck Cause
- Rescue Vessel
- Arrival Circumstance

## Shipwreck Causes
The reason the ship went down shapes the player's initial mystery and backstory. Causes include:
- Leviathan attack
- Magical storm / Psionic distortion
- Drow naval assault / Crimson Dominion experiment
- Pirate battle / Mutiny / Betrayal
- Ancient defense system activated
- Underdark Ocean current breach / Planar ocean anomaly
- Ship vanished and reappeared damaged

## Rescue Vessels & Circumstances
The rescuers do not necessarily know they are rescuing someone important. The ship that pulls you from the water determines your first relationship and faction tie.

### Rescuer Options
- **Blackwake Connection:** A pirate exploration vessel hunting for treasure.
- **Port Meridian Connection:** A merchant expedition charting routes.
- **Crimson Dominion Connection:** A slaver/arcane vessel that recognizes something unusual about your survival.
- **Drow Connection:** Drow scouts drifting near an Underdark Ocean route.
- **Spirit Keeper Connection:** A vessel guided by spirits, deliberately looking for you.
- **Salvager Connection:** Wreck hunters where you are just another survivor.

### Arrival Circumstances
How you are treated on the rescue ship heavily influences your arrival at the Home Port:
- **Honored Guest:** Treated well.
- **Prisoner:** Rescued, but questioned and held under suspicion.
- **Debt Owed:** Saved, but expecting repayment.
- **Mystery Passenger:** The crew does not understand why you survived.
- **Prophecy/Recognition:** Someone on the ship knows exactly what you are.
# Chapter 3: Races & Transformations

While the campaign setting supports many races across the Shattered Expanse, the example base race for this specific Wayfinder is the **Tideborn Pixie**. However, characters do not stay "just" their starting race. As they explore the oceans, they undergo the **Fey Transformation System**, accumulating templates that reflect the magic, ruins, and pacts they encounter. 

## Base Race: Tideborn Pixie
"A Pixie born from ocean currents, ancient routes, and the forgotten magic between shores."

**Type:** Fey (Small)
**Speed:** 20 ft., fly 30 ft., swim 30 ft.
**Ability Adjustments:** Dex +2, Cha +1

**Racial Traits:**
- **Tideborn Amphibious Knowledge (Nature):** Ignores underwater movement penalties and natural ocean terrain penalties. Can breathe underwater 1 hr/level/day (permanent at level 5).
- **Fey Sight:** Darkvision 60 ft. **Spirit Sight:** Detect Fey/Spirits/Magical Residue at will (30 ft. range).
- **Fey Magic:** Innate Spellcasting (Pixie spells).
- **Polymorph:** Innate Polymorph ability (self only). Cannot gain spell-like, supernatural, or legendary abilities from the new form.
- **Psionic Spark:** Base Power Points = 1/2 Character Level + Cha Mod (min 1). Start with one power: *Detect Psionics, Mage Hand (Invisible)*, or *Minor Illusion*.

## The Fey Transformation System
Players may acquire transformations during their journeys. These are not simply chosen from a menu; they are earned or inflicted by the world.

**Transformation Capacity:** A character has a maximum of **4 Transformation Points**.
**Ability Score Stacking Rule:** Ability scores cannot exceed 20, or 24 for mythic transformations.

### Category A: Minor Marks
**Limit:** Max 2 | **Cost:** 1 Transformation Point
Minor changes caused by magical exposure, blessings, mutations, or small awakenings.
- **Fey-Touched (+1 Cha):** Pure fey awakening. Proficiency in Persuasion, Deception, or Performance. *Minor Glamour* (Silent Image 1/day). 
- **Stormborn Fey (+1 Dex):** Ocean storms. Lightning Resist. Wind Rider (flight speed +10 ft). Storm Sense (advantage on Perception and Survival in storms).
- **Coral-Blooded Fey (+1 Con):** Reef adaptation. Natural Armor (AC 13 + Dex). Improved underwater survival.
- **Moonlit Fey (+1 Wis):** Lunar tide magic. Darkvision 60 ft. Lunar Grace (+1 AC at night). Dream Whisper 1/day.
- **Shadowwake Fey (+1 Dex):** Dark ocean. Disadvantage on attacks against you in dim light. Darkvision 60 ft. Shadow Step (10 ft. teleport between shadows 1/day).
- **Psionic Spark Fey (+1 Int):** Mind awakening. +3 PP. Minor Psionic Talent (one power). Advantage on saves vs. charmed.
- **Spirit Marked Fey (+1 Wis):** Spirit connection. Gain a minor spirit companion. Spirit Awareness (30 ft.). +1 to all saving throws.
- **Draconic Spark Fey (+1 Con):** Dormant dragon ancestry. Resistance to one element. Blindsight 30 ft. Minor breath weapon (1/day, 1d4, 15ft cone).
- **Arcane Marked Fey (+1 Int):** Minor magical mutation. Advantage on saves against spells. Minor arcane trick (cantrip at will). +1 to spell attack rolls for one school.

### Category B: Awakening Templates
**Limit:** Max 1 | **Cost:** 2 Transformation Points
A fundamental evolution of the character.
- **Awakened Fey (+1 Int, +1 Wis, +1 Cha):** Psionic evolution. Gains Psionic subtype. PP = Level + Int + Cha. One Psionic Discipline & 1st level power. Advantage on saves vs. enchantments. Lifespan x5.
- **Arcane Ascendant Fey (+1 Int, +1 Cha):** Deep magic conduit. Gains Arcane Reservoir. Spellweaver Soul (advantage on checks for one school). Resistance to one element. Arcane Memory (recall witnessed spell 1/day).
- **Spirit-Bound Fey (+1 Wis, +1 Cha):** Spirit Shaman evolution. Navigator Spirit manifests physically. Advantage on spirit-related checks. Ancestral Memory (advantage on Knowledge (History) 1/day). Spirit Walk (ethereal 1 round/level, 1/day).
- **First Captain Legacy Fey (+1 Int, +1 Wis, +1 Cha):** Lost Wayfinder inheritance. Proficiency in Water Vehicles, Survival, Knowledge (Nature). Captain's Echo (visions from past Wayfinders). Improved Ship Bond progression. Lost Route Memory (recognize ancient routes 1/week).

### Category C: Heritage Templates
**Limit:** Max 1 | **Cost:** 3 Transformation Points
Changes what the character is fundamentally.
- **Draconic Fey (+2 Str, +2 Con):** Dragon lineage. Element Resistance. Blindsight 30 ft. Breath weapon (1d6/level, max 10d6, 1/day).
- **Celestial Tide Fey (+2 Wis, +2 Cha):** Divine blessing. Good alignment. Acid/Cold/Lightning Resistance. Divine Grace (Add Cha to saves). Radiant Presence (advantage on allies' saves).
- **Infernal Fey (+2 Int, +2 Cha):** Infernal pact. Lawful alignment. Fire Resistance. Contract Mind (advantage vs. charm/domination). Infernal Boon (Hellfire or Binding Word).
- **Xoriat-Touched Fey (+2 Int, +2 Con):** Reality mutation. Aberration subtype. Immune to confusion/insanity. Reality Fracture (reroll 1 failed d20 per day). Impossible Anatomy (attacks against you have disadvantage).
- **Chimera Fey (+2 Str, +2 Con):** Engineered evolution. Gain two physical mutations (Predator, Aquatic, Winged, Armored, etc.). Mutable Anatomy (swap one mutation 1/day). Advantage vs polymorph/transmutation.

### Category D: Mythic Transformations (Campaign Locked)
Not purchased. Not selected. Earned through world-altering campaign events.
- **Deep-Touched Fey (+2 Con, +2 Int, +2 Wis):** Altered by the Underdark Ocean. Aberration/Aquatic traits. Darkvision 120 ft., Water breathing, Pressure immunity, Cold Resistance. Abyssal Telepathy 60 ft. Deep Resonance (Bonus PP = Level + Cha). Unlocks an entire mythic evolution tree at levels 5, 10, 15, and 20.
- **Abyssal Fey (+2 Con, +2 Cha):** Corrupted ocean magic. Evil alignment. Dark Tide abilities. Abyssal Mark (gain power but attract outsider attention). Speak with dead sailors.
# Chapter 4: The Wayfinder Class

"A Wayfinder is not a swashbuckler with magic, a warlock with a boat, or a psion with a sword. The Wayfinder is the rediscovery of an ancient First Captain tradition where Arcane magic shapes reality, Psionics shapes perception, and Spirit magic shapes connection. At low levels, these are three separate disciplines. At high levels, the Wayfinder becomes someone who does not simply navigate the worldâ€”they alter the routes the world allows."

## Class Chassis
**Hit Die:** d8
**Alignment:** Any
**Proficiencies:** Choose four from Acrobatics, Knowledge (Arcana), Climb/Swim, Deception, Knowledge (History), Insight, Intimidation, Perception, Knowledge (Nature), Perception, Persuasion, Knowledge (Religion), Stealth, Survival, and Water Vehicles.
**Base Attack Bonus (BAB):** standard 5e progression (+2 to +6).
**Saving Throws:** Dexterity, Charisma.
**Weapon & Armor Proficiency:** Simple weapons, martial weapons with the finesse or light property, firearms. Light armor, medium armor (at level 5), shields.

## The Threefold Magic Systems
The Wayfinder progresses in three magical paths simultaneously.
1. **The Pact (Arcane):** Warlock chassis. Uses Charisma. Eldritch Armament (can manifest Eldritch Blast through a weapon, harpoon, pistol, or standard blast) and Invocations.
2. **The Mind (Psionic):** Psion chassis (half-progression, max 6th-level powers). Uses Charisma. Gains Power Points and discovers powers.
3. **The Tide (Spirit):** Custom Spirit Shaman+ chassis. Full 1-9 spell progression but spells must be discovered/learned via the Captain's Log instead of prepared from a deity.

### Lore-Caster (The Fusion Engine)
Instead of preparing spells, the Wayfinder uncovers **Wayfinding Lore**â€”an accumulated understanding of the world. 
- **Level 7 (Arcane-Psionic Fusion):** Combine a spell and a power into one standard action (e.g., Fireball + Telekinesis = Moving Flame Sphere).
- **Level 10 (Threefold Awakening - Triune Casting):** 1/day, combine a Spell, a Power, and a Spirit Ability to open new paths to unseen locations.
- **Level 17 (Reality Navigation):** You do not teleport; you discover a path through solid fog, magical barriers, or impassable storms.
- **Level 20 (Creator of Paths):** Create permanent routes, impossible trade lanes, and open new ocean passages.

## The Navigator Spirit
At level 1, the Wayfinder awakens a personal spirit companionâ€”the "mirror" of the captain.
1. **Origin Seed:** Choose the spirit's foundational nature (e.g., Ancient Navigator, Stormborn, Abyssal, Leviathan, Ancestor Captain, Ghost Fleet).
2. **Seed Benefit:** A deliberate expression of the spirit (e.g., Storm Sense, Lost Routes, Deep Survival).
3. **Random Traits:** Roll 2d100 for an Innate Trait and an Emergent Trait (e.g., Ancient Memory, Hates Calm Waters, Echo of Darkness).
4. **Evolution:** The spirit evolves at levels 5, 10, 15, and 20 based on the captain's standard actions (e.g., Guardian Aspect, Sage Aspect, Dominator Aspect, Pathfinder Aspect). At level 20, it becomes the embodiment of your legend.

## The Living Ship Bond
The ship is your second character. It advances through **Vessel Tiers** via Wayfinder level, Chronicle Achievements, and Physical Transformation.
- **Tier 0 (Mortal Ship):** Normal vessel with maintenance costs.
- **Tier 1 (Claimed Vessel - Lvl 3):** Ship recognizes you. You sense its condition.
- **Tier 2 (Awakened Vessel - Lvl 6):** Gains intelligence/personality. Becomes an Arcane, Psionic, or Tide vessel.
- **Tier 3 (Legendary Vessel - Lvl 10):** Threefold Keel. Can install Arcane, Psionic, and Tide upgrades (e.g., Eldritch Cannons, Psychic Helm, Storm Calling).
- **Tier 4 (Mythic Vessel - Lvl 15):** Requires rare materials (dragon bone, deep crystal). Gains traits like Ghost Ship or World Sailor.
- **Tier 5 (Path-Maker Vessel - Lvl 20):** Creates permanent passages through reality. The ship changes the map of the world.

## Captain's Techniques
In addition to their magical and vessel progression, a Wayfinder gains **Captain's Techniques** at levels 3, 6, 9, 12, 15, and 18. These represent their growing influence over the sea, their crew, and the world itself. 

*See **Chapter 5a: Captain's Techniques (Rules & Framework)** for acquisition rules and **Chapter 11: Progression Charts** for category and tier advancement summaries.*
# Chapter 6: Campaign Engines

The campaign loop is: **Explore â†’ Discover â†’ Record â†’ Understand â†’ Upgrade â†’ Explore Further**. Combat feeds discovery, and discovery changes the world.

## The Discovery System & Captain's Log
A Discovery is a verified, meaningful first-time revelation that expands the world. A rumor is not a discovery; it must be physically mapped, witnessed, or magically confirmed.
- **Wayfinding Lore:** Accumulated understanding. Never spent, only gained. Used to unlock Fusion Techniques, ship awakenings, and legendary status.
- **Discovery Points (DP):** Short-term resource spent on Fusion Techniques and special class abilities (max 3-10, refreshes during downtime).
- **The Captain's Log:** A magical, permanent record. It tracks discoveries across categories (Charts, Species, Trade, Magic, Spirits, Knowledge (History), Ships). The Log contains "Hidden Tags" (e.g., Arcane, Drow, First Captain) tracked by the DM that unlock future transformations or ship upgrades.

### Discovery Tiers
1. **Local:** Small area (e.g., hidden cove). Grants 1 Lore, normal XP.
2. **Regional:** Changes travel/trade for a region (e.g., safe shipping lane). Grants 2 Lore, ship/port benefit.
3. **Major:** Matters to a major faction (e.g., lost civilization outpost). Grants 4 Lore, strong faction reaction, unlock.
4. **World-Shaping:** Changes the map or economy (e.g., Underdark ocean route). Grants 8 Lore, major political shift.
5. **Mythic:** Happens only in legend (e.g., First Captain revelation). Grants 12 Lore, capstone narrative effects.

## The Passage System
The ocean is the dungeon. Passages are the "rooms." A voyage consists of multiple Passages.
1. **Captain's Orders:** Set the priority (Fast, Survey, Hunt, Hidden, Trade, Deep).
2. **The Sea Responds:** DM determines weather, encounters, ruins, or monsters.
3. **The Crew Acts:** Navigators, lookouts, and specialists report.
4. **Player Decisions:** Fight, negotiate, hide, trade, board.
5. **Resolution:** Update supplies, repairs, Lore, and morale.

### Boarding Combat (The Corsair Dungeon)
A hostile ship is a moving dungeon. Boarding has phases: Close distance, Cannon exchange, Hook, Swing across, Deck battle, Officer duel, Capture, Salvage.

## Ship Construction Rules Engine
A ship is built using **Ship Construction Points (SCP)**.
- **Size 1 (Small Craft):** 50 SCP, 1 Hull slot. (Scout/Smuggler).
- **Size 2 (Light Vessel):** 100 SCP, 2 Hull slots. (Piracy/Fast attack).
- **Size 3 (Medium Vessel):** 200 SCP, 3 Hull slots. (Professional pirate ship).
- **Size 4 (Heavy Vessel):** 350 SCP, 5 Hull slots. (Frigate/Galleon).
- **Size 5 (Capital Vessel):** 600 SCP, 8 Hull slots. (Man-o-war).

**Categories & Materials:**
SCP is spent on Structure, Propulsion, Combat, Utility, and Special Systems. Weapons take up slots (e.g., Light Cannon = 1 slot, 5 SCP; Dragonfire Cannon = 5 slots, 75 SCP).
Materials modify Hull Strength, Weight, and Magical Affinity. (e.g., Ironwood adds +40% Hull, +20% Weight; Darkwood adds +15% Hull, -30% Weight).

Ships can install Magical (teleportation anchors), Psionic (thought navigation), Divine (celestial sails), or Spirit (living figureheads) systems.
# Chapter 7: Dungeon Master's Guide

A Wayfinder campaign should feel like routes opening, rivals responding, nations adjusting, and legends forming. The DM's role is not to restrict the Wayfinder's influence, but to ensure that influence creates new challenges and opportunities.

## Running the Campaign
- **Naval Campaigns:** Track positioning, morale, supplies, and weather. Captain's Techniques create tactical advantages, not automatic victory.
- **Exploration Campaigns:** Exploration techniques should reveal clues and paths, but they do not eliminate the discovery process. Discovering a new route creates story (e.g., rival explorers, ancient guardians).
- **Political Campaigns:** First Captain techniques create influence and reputation, not mind control. The captain can gain audiences or secure trade agreements, but kings do not automatically surrender their thrones.
- **Fleet Warfare:** Fleet conflicts are strategic encounters. Ask: Who has superior intelligence? Who controls positioning? What are the objectives? 

## Handling Consequences
Every major achievement creates reactions.
- **Immediate Effect:** What changes now?
- **Local Response:** Who notices? (e.g., local pirates, merchants).
- **Regional Response:** Who reacts? (e.g., Crimson Dominion investigates, Drow launch a raid).
- **Long-Term Consequence:** How does the world change? (e.g., a new era of exploration begins).

### Tracking the World
- **Captain Reputation:** Fame, fear, and political influence.
- **Maritime Relations:** Allies, enemies, and neutral factions.
- **Known Routes:** Safe paths, dangers, and discoveries.

## Exploit Prevention & Rules Clarifications
To preserve the campaign sandbox and prevent the Wayfinder from bypassing all gameplay:

1. **Fleet + First Captain Interaction:** 
   - *Rule:* Fleet techniques affect tactical command during active operations. First Captain techniques affect strategic influence. A creature can benefit from only **one** Fleet technique command effect at a time.
2. **Mystic/Hunter Information Limits:**
   - *Rule:* Information gained through techniques provides direction, clues, and probabilities. It does not automatically reveal all hidden creatures, secret motives, or bypass perception. Perfect information destroys mystery.
3. **Corsair Siege Limits:**
   - *Rule:* Siege-related techniques create openings and tactical advantages. They do not automatically destroy structures, ships, or magical defenses. You create a breach; the fortress does not instantly collapse.
4. **Legendary World Effects:**
   - *Rule:* Permanent world-changing effects (e.g., discovering impossible routes, establishing sovereign domains) require time, resources, and narrative consequences. The captain can influence history, but they do not own it.
5. **Passive & Standard action Economy Limits:**
   - *Rule:* Bonuses from Captain's Techniques follow normal 5e advantage/disadvantage and bounded accuracy rules. A Wayfinder may use only **one** Reaction Captain's Technique per round.

## Final DM Principle
*The captain may change the world, but the world always responds. A Wayfinder's greatest victories should not close the campaign; they should create the next chapter.*

Captainâ€™s Techniques

Player Guide: Building Your Captain

Wayfinder Sourcebook - Character Development Chapter

Chapter Purpose

Captainâ€™s Techniques define how a Wayfinder grows from a capable traveler into a legendary captain.

A Wayfinder does not advance by simply collecting stronger attacks or larger numerical bonuses. The system is designed around identity:

What kind of captain are you?

How do you solve problems?

What does your crew believe you represent?

How does the world change because you are present?

Captainâ€™s Techniques use seven categories and four progression tiers:

Categories define identity.
Tiers define growth.

The approved categories are:

Seamanship

Duelist

Corsair

Fleet

Hunter

Mystic

First Captain

The approved tiers are:

Least

Lesser

Greater

Legendary

The subsystem is designed as an invocation-style modular advancement system comparable in structure to Warlock Invocations, but with a different identity: the Wayfinder masters opportunity, position, logistics, exploration, command, and influence. ChatGPT-Pirate Adventure Setup 2.txt

Section 1: Choosing Your Captain Identity

Before selecting individual techniques, determine what kind of captain your Wayfinder is becoming.

A captain identity answers:

What do you command?

What problems do you specialize in solving?

What kind of legend will people tell?

Your identity is shaped by:

Primary Category

Secondary Category

Technique Tier Choices

Campaign Role

A captain focused on exploration will make different choices than a captain building a fleet or ruling a pirate kingdom.

Section 2: Understanding Categories

Categories represent different philosophies of captaincy.

Choosing a category does not lock a character into one role forever. It establishes the foundation of advancement.

A captain may begin as a sailor, become a raider, and eventually become a ruler.

The important question is:

What is the core of your captainâ€™s legend?

Section 3: Understanding Tiers

Least Techniques

Identity:

"The captain begins."

Least techniques represent the foundation of your captain identity.

These abilities establish:

personal style,

basic mastery,

early specialization.

Expected Power:

Least techniques are practical abilities.

They should:

solve common problems,

define your role,

provide reliable advantages.

Gameplay Purpose:

Least techniques answer:

"What kind of captain are you becoming?"

Examples:

the explorer who never loses the trail,

the duelist who wins impossible fights,

the corsair who breaks enemy defenses,

the navigator who reads dangerous waters.

Lesser Techniques

Identity:

"The captain becomes recognized."

Lesser techniques represent specialization.

At this stage, the captain is no longer merely skilled.

They are known for something.

Expected Power:

Lesser techniques provide:

stronger specialization,

expanded capability,

improved control over situations.

Gameplay Purpose:

Lesser techniques answer:

"What kind of captain have you become?"

This is where characters begin strongly expressing their chosen path.

Greater Techniques

Identity:

"The captain becomes a major force."

Greater techniques represent advanced mastery.

The captain is no longer reacting to events.

They influence them.

Expected Power:

Greater techniques provide:

encounter-changing effects,

strategic advantages,

advanced command options.

Gameplay Purpose:

Greater techniques answer:

"What situations can you control?"

These abilities should shape battles, expeditions, and major conflicts without replacing the campaign itself. ChatGPT-Pirate Adventure Setup 2.txt

Legendary Techniques

Identity:

"The captain becomes history."

Legendary techniques represent campaign-scale influence.

These abilities belong to captains whose names are known beyond their own ship.

Expected Power:

Legendary techniques may influence:

fleets,

factions,

territories,

political situations,

major voyages.

Gameplay Purpose:

Legendary techniques answer:

"What changes because you exist?"

Legendary abilities create consequences. They do not remove the need for adventure.

The strongest captain is not the one who avoids consequences.

The strongest captain is the one whose victories create challenges worthy of their legend. ChatGPT-Pirate Adventure Setup 2.txt

Captainâ€™s Techniques

Dungeon Master Integration Guide

Wayfinder Sourcebook

DM Resource Chapter

Introduction: Running a Captainâ€™s Campaign

Captainâ€™s Techniques are designed around a different style of advancement than traditional class features.

A traditional adventurer asks:

â€œHow do I overcome this challenge?â€

A Wayfinder captain asks:

â€œHow does overcoming this challenge change the voyage?â€

Captainâ€™s Techniques are intended to influence:

naval campaigns,

exploration campaigns,

pirate conflicts,

political campaigns,

fleet warfare,

island kingdoms,

supernatural oceans.

The DMâ€™s role is not to limit Captainâ€™s Techniques. The role is to allow those abilities to create meaningful consequences.

A successful Captainâ€™s Techniques campaign should show:

routes opening,

rivals adapting,

factions responding,

territories changing,

legends forming.

The subsystemâ€™s core philosophy is that power creates opportunities and consequences rather than removing the need for adventure. ChatGPT-Pirate Adventure Setup 2.txt

Section 1: Managing Captain Abilities

1.1 Evaluating Powerful Abilities

Captainâ€™s Techniques differ from ordinary abilities because many affect the world around the character.

A DM should evaluate abilities using three questions:

1. What problem does the ability solve?

Examples:

A Seamanship technique may solve:

dangerous navigation,

difficult travel,

environmental hazards.

A Fleet technique may solve:

coordination problems,

command issues,

battlefield organization.

A First Captain technique may solve:

organizational limitations,

diplomatic barriers,

logistical challenges.

The question is not:

â€œDoes this ability bypass the adventure?â€

The question is:

â€œWhat new situation does this ability create?â€

1.2 Maintaining Player Agency

Captainâ€™s Techniques should increase player agency.

They should allow players to:

choose routes,

influence factions,

command resources,

establish reputations,

change strategic situations.

They should not remove:

meaningful choices,

consequences,

opposing interests,

campaign uncertainty.

Example:

A Mystic Navigator discovers a hidden sea route.

Incorrect interpretation:

â€œThe party automatically wins every voyage.â€

Correct interpretation:

â€œThe party gains access to a route others cannot use. Now merchants, pirates, kingdoms, and ancient guardians care about that route.â€

The ability creates story pressure.

It does not end the story.

1.3 Avoiding Removed Choices

The primary DM warning:

Do not convert influence into certainty.

Captainâ€™s Techniques should not become:

automatic diplomacy,

automatic victory,

perfect information,

unlimited resources.

The subsystem balance audit specifically identifies this concern:

Fleet should affect battle, not replace military campaigns.

First Captain should affect organizations, not create automatic obedience.

Mystic and Hunter should provide information, not absolute knowledge.

Corsair should create openings, not automatic destruction. ChatGPT-Pirate Adventure Setup 2.txt

Section 2: Legendary Techniques

Legendary Techniques represent the point where the Wayfinder becomes a historical figure.

They affect:

voyages,

fleets,

nations,

organizations,

maritime power.

They are not simply stronger combat abilities.

They are campaign tools.

2.1 Handling Legendary Effects

When a Legendary Technique is used, consider:

Immediate Effect

What changes right now?

Examples:

a route becomes accessible,

a fleet gains an advantage,

a faction recognizes the captain,

a supernatural boundary is crossed.

Secondary Effects

Who notices?

Possible reactions:

governments investigate,

pirates attempt exploitation,

merchants seek agreements,

enemies create countermeasures,

supernatural beings respond.

Long-Term Effects

How does the world change?

Track:

reputation,

territory,

alliances,

enemies,

economic changes,

political consequences.

Legendary abilities are designed to create lasting campaign developments. Permanent world-changing effects require time, resources, and campaign integration. ChatGPT-Pirate Adventure Setup 2.txt

2.2 Faction Responses

Factions should react according to their interests.

Merchants

Possible responses:

request access,

offer contracts,

compete,

attempt control.

Pirates

Possible responses:

challenge the captain,

recruit the captain,

imitate the captain,

sabotage the captain.

Governments

Possible responses:

negotiate,

monitor,

regulate,

attempt alliance.

Supernatural Powers

Possible responses:

curiosity,

hostility,

bargains,

ancient claims.

A Legendary captain becomes a strategic factor.

The world notices.

2.3 Reputation Changes

Reputation should evolve over time.

Recommended progression:

Local Reputation

The captain becomes known among:

crews,

ports,

local factions.

Regional Reputation

The captain becomes recognized by:

kingdoms,

major organizations,

rival captains.

Legendary Reputation

The captain becomes a figure of maritime history.

Possible results:

songs,

myths,

political influence,

historical records.

Section 3: Fleet-Level Play

3.1 Multiple Ships

Fleet-level campaigns require tracking more than individual combat.

Important factors:

objectives,

positioning,

supplies,

morale,

command structure,

weather,

enemy strategy.

Avoid reducing fleet warfare to:

â€œRoll attacks until ships explode.â€

Ships represent:

resources,

organizations,

strategic positions.

3.2 Organizations

Fleet techniques interact with organizations.

Track:

command hierarchy,

officers,

specialists,

resources,

loyalty,

reputation.

A fleet is not just a number.

It is a living structure.

3.3 Strategic Conflicts

Fleet conflicts should include objectives.

Examples:

Victory Conditions:

capture a harbor,

protect a convoy,

escape pursuit,

blockade an enemy,

establish dominance.

Not every naval conflict requires destruction.

A smarter captain wins the objective.

3.4 Territory Control

Territory should be tracked as a campaign resource.

Examples:

Controlled:

ports,

trade routes,

islands,

safe harbors,

supply chains.

Contested:

pirate waters,

disputed colonies,

supernatural zones.

Lost:

captured ports,

blocked routes,

hostile territory.

Section 4: Narrative Consequences

Captainâ€™s Techniques are designed to change campaign states.

The DM should maintain:

NPC reactions,

faction relationships,

reputation,

territory,

route control.

4.1 NPC Reactions

NPC reactions depend on:

the captainâ€™s reputation,

past standard actions,

current interests.

Examples:

A feared Corsair Captain may receive:

fear,

hostility,

reluctant respect.

A First Captain may receive:

diplomatic attention,

political requests,

strategic opposition.

4.2 Political Changes

Political effects should create negotiations, not automatic control.

A First Captain does not simply say:

â€œYou obey.â€

Instead:

â€œYou must now consider this captain a major political actor.â€

Possible outcomes:

treaties,

rival coalitions,

trade agreements,

political opposition.

The balance review specifically establishes:

First Captain creates influence, not automatic obedience. ChatGPT-Pirate Adventure Setup 2.txt

4.3 Reputation Systems

Track:

Allies

Who supports the captain?

Rivals

Who challenges the captain?

Enemies

Who benefits from the captain failing?

Dependents

Who relies on the captain?

Power creates relationships.

Relationships create stories.

4.4 Changing World States

When a captain succeeds, update the campaign.

Examples:

New Route Discovered

Changes:

trade,

piracy,

exploration,

political value.

Pirate Fleet Created

Changes:

naval balance,

rival responses,

territorial claims.

Supernatural Ocean Mastery

Changes:

magical interest,

ancient awakenings,

unusual threats.

The world should remember.

Campaign Integration Guidelines

Naval Campaigns

Emphasize:

ships,

crews,

routes,

battles,

maritime reputation.

Captainâ€™s Techniques should influence how naval situations are approached.

Exploration Campaigns

Emphasize:

unknown locations,

lost civilizations,

impossible terrain.

Exploration abilities should reveal opportunities, clues, and paths.

They should not remove discovery.

Pirate Campaigns

Emphasize:

reputation,

rival captains,

raids,

alliances,

territory.

Political Campaigns

Emphasize:

diplomacy,

organizations,

influence,

competing interests.

Supernatural Ocean Campaigns

Emphasize:

strange routes,

magical seas,

ancient powers,

impossible voyages.

Balance Considerations

Fleet + First Captain

Potential Risk:

Military and political authority combining into automatic victory.

Correction:

Fleet controls tactical operations.

First Captain controls influence and organization.

Neither creates automatic success. ChatGPT-Pirate Adventure Setup 2.txt

Mystic + Hunter

Potential Risk:

Perfect information.

Correction:

Information provides:

clues,

direction,

probabilities.

It does not reveal everything.

Corsair + Siege

Potential Risk:

Automatic destruction.

Correction:

Creates:

openings,

vulnerabilities,

advantages.

It does not instantly destroy defenses.

Legendary Influence

Potential Risk:

Permanent effects accumulating without limitation.

Correction:

Major changes require:

time,

resources,

consequences,

campaign tracking.

Exploit Review

Exploit Status

No fundamental mechanical exploits identified.

The primary risks are interpretation issues.

Resolved Issues

Issue
Resolution
Fleet stacking
Similar command effects do not stack unless stated
Perfect information
Information remains incomplete
Automatic siege victory
Siege creates openings only
Political domination
Influence does not equal control
Legendary bypass
Major effects require consequences

ChatGPT-Pirate Adventure Setup 2.txt

Final DM Assessment

Captainâ€™s Techniques are approved for:

naval campaigns,

exploration campaigns,

political campaigns,

pirate campaigns,

kingdom-building campaigns,

supernatural maritime adventures.

The subsystem is not designed to make the captain unstoppable.

It is designed to make the captain important.

The fighter defeats the enemy.

The wizard changes the battlefield.

The Wayfinder changes the voyage.

The captain now has a rulebook for changing the world without accidentally deleting the campaign. A surprisingly difficult standard for fictional people who insist on naming ships after storms and then being shocked when storms happen.


## NPC Captains and Villain Builds
To seamlessly integrate Captain's Techniques into your campaign, you can assign them to notable NPCs and recurring villains.

### Low-Level Rival: The Upstart Corsair (CR 4)
- **Role:** Aggressive privateer or early campaign antagonist.
- **Techniques:** Find the Weak Board (Least Corsair), Sea Legs (Least Seamanship).
- **Tactics:** Relies on aggressive boarding standard actions and environmental hazards to overwhelm the party's ship.

### Mid-Level Antagonist: The Fleet Commander (CR 10)
- **Role:** Leader of a regional naval force or pirate armada.
- **Techniques:** Crew Rotation (Least Fleet), Captain's Challenge (Least Duelist), plus Lesser Fleet techniques.
- **Tactics:** Engages at scale, using formations and coordinated bombardments before closing for a dramatic duel.

### Legendary Villain: The Immortal Pirate King (CR 19)
- **Role:** Campaign-ending threat with world-shaping influence.
- **Techniques:** Reserve the Hold (Least First Captain), Whispering Compass (Least Mystic), plus Greater and Legendary Corsair/First Captain techniques.
- **Tactics:** Operates on a strategic level, controlling factions and impossible routes. Rarely fights directly unless backed by overwhelming supernatural support.

## Treasure Considerations
When awarding treasure to a party with a Wayfinder, consider items that enhance their chosen techniques rather than just raw stats. For example, a spyglass that grants bonus uses of *Chart the Unknown* or an enchanted cutlass that augments the *Captain's Challenge* technique.

9. Visual Style Guide

Overall Style

Genre

Oceanic fantasy adventure.

Blend:

naval realism,

dark fantasy,

exploration,

mythic scale.

Color Direction

Primary palette:

deep ocean blues,

storm gray,

aged parchment,

bronze,

silver,

muted gold.

Lighting

Preferred:

dramatic skies,

lantern light,

moonlit seas,

magical glow effects.

Avoid:

bright cartoon colors,

clean modern aesthetics.

Character Design

Captains should appear:

experienced,

capable,

distinct.

Avoid:

generic pirates,

random sailors,

exaggerated comedy.

Ship Design

Ships should communicate progression.

Early:

practical vessels.

Advanced:

decorated command ships.

Legendary:

iconic vessels recognized across the world.

Typography Integration

Artwork should leave room for:

chapter titles,

rules boxes,

sidebars.

Avoid placing focal elements behind text areas.

Captainâ€™s Techniques
Category and Tier Chapter
Captainâ€™s Techniques are the Wayï¬nderâ€™s deï¬ning advancement system. They are not
just tricks, stunts, or combat riders. They are the means by which a Wayï¬nder
becomes a captain in the full sense of the word: a person who can shape voyages,
crews, battles, and the fortunes of the sea.

Category Chapter
1. Seamanship
Seamanship is the category of naval mastery. It covers ship operation, navigation,
environmental interaction, and voyage leadership.
A Wayï¬nder who leans into Seamanship is the captain who understands the sea as a
living route system. They know how to move a vessel, read conditions, survive hostile
waters, and keep a voyage on course when lesser sailors would be lost, wrecked, or
tempted to panic. Seamanship is the category for characters who want to make travel
itself matter.
In play, Seamanship supports:
navigation and routeï¬nding,
ship handling and movement,
survival in dangerous waters,
environmental adaptation,
leadership during voyages.
2. Duelist
Duelist is the category of personal combat mastery. It covers captain-versus-captain
conï¬‚icts, precision combat, and tactical superiority.
A Duelist Wayï¬nder is the captain who settles hard questions personally. This category
is for characters who want to dominate challenges through skill, timing, and presence
rather than through sheer brute force. Duelist does not replace battleï¬eld command.
It wins the knife-ï¬ght at the rail, the duel on the quarterdeck, and the decisive one-on-
one moment when a rival commander must be answered directly.
In play, Duelist supports:
personal combat,
oï¬ƒcer duels,
ðŸš€  Powered by ChatGPT Exporter 55 / 434

precision attacks,
counters and combat timing,
tactical superiority in close engagements.
3. Corsair
Corsair is the category of piracy, boarding actions, intimidation, and unconventional
warfare.
A Corsair Wayï¬nder ï¬ghts dirty in the most honorable way possible, which is to say,
the way that keeps the ship aï¬‚oat and the enemy regretting their life choices. This
category represents breaching, raiding, disruption, and the art of breaking an
opponentâ€™s conï¬dence and defensive structure without needing to play fair about it.
In play, Corsair supports:
boarding actions,
ship raids,
intimidation,
breaching and disruption,
unconventional tactics against fortiï¬ed targets.
4. Fleet
Fleet is the category of command structures, multiple vessels, strategic warfare, and
naval campaigns.
A Fleet Wayï¬nder is not primarily a lone hero. They are an organizer of coordinated
violence and disciplined action. Fleet is the category for captains who think in
formations, signal chains, squad roles, and operations that involve more than one ship
or more than one piece of the battleï¬eld.
In play, Fleet supports:
command structures,
allied coordination,
multiple vessels,
ðŸš€  Powered by ChatGPT Exporter 56 / 434

strategic warfare,
naval campaigns and battle planning.
5. Hunter
Hunter is the category of tracking, pursuit, monster hunting, and hostile-environment
survival.
A Hunter Wayï¬nder is the captain who does not let prey vanish just because it has the
good taste to run. This category covers the chase, the trail, the interception, and the
hard practical skill of surviving and operating in places that were not built to be
pleasant.
In play, Hunter supports:
tracking,
pursuit,
interception,
monster hunting,
survival in hostile terrain and dangerous waters.
6. Mystic
Mystic is the category of supernatural navigation, magical oceans, hidden knowledge,
and arcane inï¬‚uence.
A Mystic Wayï¬nder does not merely travel the sea. They negotiate with the seaâ€™s

omens, strange laws, symbolic insight, or direct supernatural interaction with the
world of water, weather, and hidden distance.

supernatural navigation,
magical and storm-driven environments,
hidden knowledge,
arcane or omen-based inï¬‚uence,

exploration that goes beyond ordinary cartography.
7. First Captain

other people and institutions respond to the captainâ€™s existence.

diplomacy,
reputation and authority.

DM Interpretation Notes
The DM should treat Captainâ€™s Techniques as campaign-facing, not just combat-facing.
The DM guidance established several important principles:
techniques provide advantages, not universal success,
exploration techniques provide information and direction, not perfect revelation,
naval abilities alter conditions and probabilities, not instant victory,
Legendary eï¬€ects create lasting consequences,
First Captain abilities create inï¬‚uence, not mind control. ChatGPT-Pirate Adventure

ðŸš€  Powered by ChatGPT Exporter 62 / 434

Use these principles when adjudicating the subsystem:
Seamanship should make routes possible or safer, not erase travel challenges.
Duelist should reward precision and tempo, not replace every combat option.
Corsair should create openings and vulnerabilities, not automatically destroy ships
or fortiï¬cations.
Fleet should improve coordination, not let one captain dictate the entire war by
existing near it.
Hunter should provide clues, pursuit, and pressure, not omniscience.
Mystic should provide supernatural direction, not perfect answers.
First Captain should move factions, organizations, and reputations, not overwrite
free will.
The published balance notes also support strict limits on information, command
stacking, and narrative scope. Fleet beneï¬ts should not stack freely, and information
techniques must remain directional rather than absolute. ChatGPT-Pirate Adventure

is becoming.

Categories represent diï¬€erent philosophies of captaincy.

# Chapter 15: Glossary and Master Index

## Glossary of Terms

- **Captain's Technique:** A magical or extraordinary ability granted by the Wayfinder class, representing the captain's mastery over their vessel, crew, and the elements.
- **Living Ship:** The Wayfinder's magical vessel, progressing in power alongside the captain. Upgrades via Tiers (Driftwood, Captain's, Legendary, Mythic, Path-Maker).
- **Navigator Spirit:** A spectral entity or manifestation of the sea's will that guides the Wayfinder.
- **Shattered Expanse:** The primary setting for the campaign, an ocean dotted with ruins, impossible geography, and shifting magical tides.
- **Tier:** The power scaling system for Captain's Techniques and Vessels. The four Technique Tiers are Least, Lesser, Greater, and Legendary.

## Master Technique Index

The following is an alphabetical index of every Captain's Technique available in the sourcebook.

- Abyssal Quiet
- Admiralâ€™s Oath
- Ambush Route
- Anchor the Line
- Banner of the Rising Tide
- Banners of Every Port
- Bite of the Chosen Range
- Black Flag Feint
- Blood in the Water
- Bloodline Reading
- Boarding Hook
- Boarding Partyâ€™s Wake
- Boarding Rush
- Boarding Signal
- Boarding Storm
- Brace the Spars
- Breach and Bind
- Break Their Guard

# Chapter 18: The World Atlas

*â€œThe world did not end. It merely shattered. What was once a continent of empires is now an ocean of possibilities.â€* â€” First Captain Alaric Thorne

The **Shattered Expanse** is the defining geographical reality of the setting. Roughly 800 years ago, a cataclysm known as the **Sundering Tide** obliterated the ancient supercontinent, fragmenting it into tens of thousands of islands scattered across an endless, turbulent ocean.

## Charted Waters vs. The Unwritten Sea

The ocean is roughly divided into two distinct logistical and magical zones.

### The Charted Waters (35% of the Expanse)
The Charted Waters represent the "civilized" or at least "survivable" regions of the world. These are the major trade routes, the secured archipelagoes, and the naval territories claimed by the dominant factions. 
- **Navigational Reality:** Standard compasses work here. The weather behaves according to predictable patterns, and the ley lines are stable enough to allow for reliable arcane travel.
- **The Great Magical Trade Network:** This is where the bulk of the Pirate Renaissance takes place. Armadas clash over taxation routes, and corsairs raid the fat merchant galleons of the empires.

### The Unwritten Sea (65% of the Expanse)
Beyond the edges of the faction maps lies The Unwritten Sea. This is the untamed, shifting, and wildly magical frontier. 
- **Navigational Reality:** Non-magical compasses spin wildly. Islands move with the tides, and the stars occasionally change position. Navigating the Unwritten Sea requires a Wayfinder, a Navigator Spirit, or suicidal luck.
- **The Discovery Loop:** This is the realm of ancient First Captain ruins, slumbering leviathans, and forgotten magic. Expeditions venture here seeking power, often returning with either legendary wealth or not at all.

## Major Magical Phenomena

The Sundering Tide did not just break the land; it broke the weave of magic itself, creating permanent geographical hazards.

### The Aether Streams
Massive, invisible rivers of magical energy that flow beneath the ocean's surface. Ships equipped with Arcane engines (such as a Tier 3 Legendary Vessel) can lock onto an Aether Stream, allowing them to sail at impossible speeds against the wind. However, these streams occasionally surge, threatening to tear apart hulls that aren't structurally reinforced.

### The Doldrums of Silence
Zones where the wind simply ceases to exist, and all sound is magically dampened. These regions can stretch for hundreds of miles. Ships caught in the Doldrums without magical propulsion or rowers will slowly starve to death. It is rumored that ghosts of the First Fleet haunt the deepest centers of the Silence.

### The Glass Storms
A localized weather event found near psionic ruins. Instead of rain, the clouds precipitate crystalline shards driven by gale-force winds. A Glass Storm can shred a mundane sail in minutes and severely damage a wooden hull. Navigators must either magically shield the ship or outrun the storm front.

## The Vertical Map: The Underdark Ocean
The Shattered Expanse is not limited to the surface. Beneath the waves lies the **Sunless Sea** (the Underdark Ocean). 
Accessible only through massive, whirlpool-like abysses known as "The Drown-Gates," the Sunless Sea is a subterranean ocean lit by bioluminescent fungi and magma vents. It is home to deep-dwelling empires, lost aberrations, and the most dangerous corsairs who use the subterranean waters to bypass surface blockades.

> [!NOTE]
> **DM Guidance on Geography:** The Shattered Expanse is designed to support the core campaign loop: Rumor -> Expedition -> Discovery -> Route -> Trade -> Conflict. By placing high-value ancient ruins in the dangerous Unwritten Sea, and the markets to sell those goods in the Charted Waters, you naturally force the players into conflict with both the environment and the rival factions!
# Chapter 19: Regional Gazetteer - The Crimson Dominion

*â€œMagic is not a gift. It is a weapon. The ocean is not a frontier. It is a territory. Those who disagree will row our galleys until they understand.â€* â€” High Admiral Vane

The Crimson Dominion is the most formidable, expansionist, and terrifying military power operating in the Charted Waters. They are a magocracy built upon a foundation of absolute subjugation, believing that those who wield arcane power possess the divine right to rule, and those who lack it exist solely to serve. 

## 1. Geography, Demographics & Hubs

The Dominion lays claim to the **Cinder Atolls**, a highly volcanic, resource-rich cluster of islands on the eastern edge of the Charted Waters. 

**Population Overview:**
- Total Estimated Population: ~2.5 Million
- The Arcane Admiralty & Elite: 5%
- The Iron Legion (Military) & Citizens: 35%
- The Bound (Enslaved Caste): 60%

### The Crimson Citadel (Capital Hub)
The capital is not merely a city; it is a fortress carved directly into the caldera of a dormant, massive volcano. The caldera is open to the sea, providing an impenetrable natural harbor.
- **The Obsidian Spire:** The central tower rising from the harbor water, housing the Arcane Admiralty. It glows with eternal, magical fire.
- **The Ash Markets:** The civilian and merchant districts, built in tiers along the interior walls of the volcano.
- **The Deep Pens:** The horrific slave quarters located near the magma vents, used for geothermal heating and psychological breaking.

### The Iron Shipyards
Located on a neighboring island stripped bare of all vegetation. Here, massive drydocks use bound fire and earth elementals to smelt steel and shape hull plating. The air is choked with smog, and the harbor is constantly churning with the launch of new warships.

### The Border Forts (The Crimson Line)
A perimeter of heavily fortified, magically warded island outposts. Any ship that crosses The Crimson Line without a Dominion writ is immediately fired upon by heavy arcane siege engines.

## 2. Magic Systems Integration

The Dominionâ€™s approach to magic is entirely utilitarian and weaponized.

- **Arcane Magic (The Backbone):** Evocation and Abjuration are the highest-honored schools. Wizards are drafted into the military at a young age. Dominion ships use wands of *Fireball* mounted on gimbals instead of black powder cannons, and project massive *Wall of Force* shields to deflect incoming fire.
- **Psionics (Internal Control):** Psions form the Dominion Inquisition. They are used for long-range, secure fleet communication (bypassing the need for flags or couriers) and for thought-policing the slave populations and lower military ranks. 
- **Spirit Magic (Banned/Exploited):** The Dominion considers Spirit Magic primitive and dangerous. When they encounter elementals or nature spirits, they use Arcane binding circles to trap them, turning them into living batteries to power ship engines and forges.
- **Deep Magic (Feared & Ignored):** The Dominion's arrogance blinds them to the deep ocean. They view the Underdark Ocean as a myth or a wasteland, making their heavily armored surface fleets highly vulnerable to attacks from below.

## 3. Faction Relations (Geopolitical Matrix)

The Dominion operates on a policy of eventual total conquest. 

- **Free Captains Alliance:** *Active Extermination.* The Dominion views pirates as vermin disrupting their divine right to rule the ocean. They hang captured Wayfinders from their rigging as warnings.
- **Grand Merchant Consortium:** *Cold War / Exploitative Trade.* The Dominion relies on the Consortium for rare spell components and raw materials. They tolerate themâ€”for nowâ€”but actively plant spies in Port Meridian, planning for the day they simply annex the trade network.
- **Drowned Silk Houses (Drow):** *Shadow War.* A secret, brutal conflict rages. The Dominion loses ships to "mysterious storms" that are actually Drow submarine ambushes. The Admiralty officially denies the Drow exist to avoid looking weak.
- **Spirit Keepers:** *Hostile Exploitation.* The Dominion actively hunts the ancient leviathans the Spirit Keepers protect, seeking to harvest their blood and bone for magical experimentation.
- **Independent Salvagers:** *Regulated Tolerance.* The Dominion desperately wants First Captain technology. They allow the Salvagers to operate in their waters, provided the Dominion gets the right of first refusal on all magical artifacts pulled from the Unwritten Sea.

## 4. Societal Hierarchy (The Caste System)

Social mobility in the Dominion is impossible without magical aptitude.

1. **The Arcane Admiralty:** The absolute rulers. Consists of twelve High Mages who hold the rank of Admiral. They dictate law, military strategy, and magical research.
2. **The Magi-Officers:** Commissioned ship captains, tacticians, and court wizards. To hold rank on a ship, one must be capable of casting at least 3rd-level spells.
3. **The Iron Legion:** The non-magical standing army. Highly disciplined, heavily armored marines and city guards. They are respected, but ultimately viewed as expendable shields for the Magi.
4. **The Merchant Class:** State-approved civilians. Bureaucrats, quartermasters, and sanctioned traders who manage the empire's logistics.
5. **The Bound:** The slave caste. Comprised of captured pirates, conquered islanders, and political dissidents. They handle all physical labor, from mining to rowing the massive galleys when the wind fails.

## 5. Internal Politics & Rival Factions

The Dominion projects absolute unity, but beneath the surface, it is a nest of vipers.

- **The Traditionalists:** High mages who believe the empire should rely strictly on pure Arcane superiority and look down on those who meddle with foreign magic.
- **The Artificers Guild:** Radicals within the shipyards who want to integrate salvaged, unstable First Captain technology with modern ship designs, often resulting in catastrophic explosions.
- **The Inquisition:** The terrifying psionic secret police. They are supposed to hunt traitors, but they have grown so powerful that even Magi-Officers fear them.
- **The Liberation Front:** A highly secretive, violently suppressed abolitionist movement operating in the deep mines and ship bilges. They communicate via smuggled thieves' cant and are desperate for outside help (a perfect hook for a Wayfinder crew).

## 6. The Military Machine

The Crimson Armada is not merely a navy; it is an industrialized magical war machine. Their tactical doctrine relies on overwhelming arcane superiority, unbreakable discipline, and heavily armored vessels that laugh at traditional cannon fire.

### 6.1 Chain of Command & Ranks
The Dominion military strictly divides arcane casters from mundane soldiers, creating a stark caste system even aboard ship.
- **High Admiral (The Council of Twelve):** The supreme commanders of the Dominion, operating from the Obsidian Spire. They are exclusively Archmages of the Evocation and Abjuration schools.
- **Fleet-Magus (Admiral):** Commands a flotilla of 5-10 ships. Usually a Wizard of at least 13th level.
- **Spell-Captain (Captain):** The commanding officer of a single vessel. They wear crimson robes lined with steel pauldrons. They lead the ship's casting batteries.
- **Iron Praetor (First Mate/Marine Commander):** The highest-ranking non-magical officer. They do not steer the ship; they command the Iron Legion marines in boarding actions and enforce discipline with brutal efficiency.
- **Aether-Helmsman (Quartermaster):** Specialized artificers or sorcerers who interface directly with the ship's bound elementals, steering the vessel via magical conduits rather than a traditional wheel.
- **Legionnaire (Marine):** The rank-and-file of the Iron Legion. Heavily armored in plate, wielding halberds and heavy crossbows.

### 6.2 Standard Naval Doctrines
- **The Aegis Phalanx:** Dominion ships never engage alone. They travel in perfectly spaced diamond formations. If one ship is targeted by heavy fire, neighboring ships cast *Wall of Force* or *Otiluke's Resilient Sphere* to shield it.
- **The Arcane Volley:** Instead of relying on black powder (which is susceptible to wet powder and backfires), Dominion ships use batteries of low-level mages (Evokers). When the Spell-Captain drops their baton, the battery casts synchronized *Magic Missiles* or *Lightning Bolts* directly at the enemy's waterline.
- **The Iron Boarding:** The Iron Legion never swings on ropes. Dominion ships use heavy, magically-propelled grappling chains that forcefully drag the enemy vessel hull-to-hull. Magi then cast *Hold Person*, *Slow*, or *Web* onto the enemy deck, while the heavily armored Iron Legion marches across wide gangplanks to methodically slaughter the paralyzed crew.

### 6.3 Specific Ship Classes
- **The *Ignis*-Class Frigate (The Hunter):** Fast, sleek interceptors. They have no sails. Instead, they are propelled by a bound water elemental pushing a jet stream beneath the hull. Armed with forward-facing Arcane Ballistae. Used for hunting pirates and blockading ports.
- **The *Aegis*-Class Galleon (The Line-Breaker):** The standard ship of the line. Plated in Crimson Iron (granting fire resistance). They house a battery of 20 battle-mages and carry a compliment of 100 Iron Legion marines.
- **The *Obsidian*-Class Dreadnought (The Flagship):** Massive, floating fortresses propelled by bound fire elementals turning colossal paddlewheels. They carry a massive central arcane cannon (The God-Spear) capable of casting a maximized *Disintegrate* beam once per hour. Only three exist in the Armada.
- **The *Inquisitor*-Class Sub-Chaser (The Silent Eye):** Small, stealthy vessels painted pitch black. They use Psionic scrying nets dragged through the water to detect the brainwaves of submerged Drow submarines or hiding Wayfinders.

### 6.4 Magical Ship Integration
- **Elemental Cores:** The heart of a Dominion ship is its Engine Room, where bound elementals are tortured and siphoned for raw locomotive power. If the containment runes are broken in combat, the elemental breaks free, usually destroying the ship from the inside out in a violent rage.
- **Scrying Nets:** Dominion rigging is often interwoven with divination magic. Climbing the rigging of a Dominion ship while invisible will often trigger magical alarms on the bridge.
- **Warded Hulls:** The hulls are inscribed with Abjuration runes, giving them exceptional Hardness against magical attacks, but making them ironically vulnerable to raw, overwhelming physical force (like a Leviathan ramming them).

## 7. Notable Figures

- **High Admiral Vane:** The ruthless, calculating leader of the Arcane Admiralty. A master Evoker who lost an arm in a pirate raid and replaced it with a prosthetic forged of living magma. He seeks to completely eradicate the Free Captains.
- **Inquisitor Kaelen:** The terrifying head of the Dominion's psionic secret police. Kaelen is rarely seen, preferring to project his consciousness into the minds of his agents. He is currently obsessed with rooting out the Liberation Front.
- **Arbiter Zyla:** A highly decorated Magi-Officer who secretly sympathizes with the abolitionists. She commands the frigate *Iron Resolution* and occasionally turns a blind eye to smuggler vessels carrying escaped slaves.

## 8. Player Character Integration

- **Best Fit Backgrounds:** Soldier, Sage, Acolyte, Noble, Prisoner (The Bound).
- **Favored Classes:** Wizard (Evocation, Abjuration), Fighter (Eldritch Knight, Battle Master), Artificer (Artillerist). 
- **Roleplay Hook:** A Dominion PC might be a loyalist trying to prove their worth, an exiled mage who grew a conscience, or a former slave (The Bound) seeking bloody vengeance against their former masters.

## 9. Unique Regional Assets

- **Arcane Ballistae:** Siege weapons that don't use ammunition. They draw on the operator's spell slots (or a bound elemental core) to fire bolts of raw force damage.
- **Crimson Iron:** A dense, magically treated steel that grants Resistance to Fire damage. It is incredibly heavy, requiring specialized ship designs to stay buoyant.
- **Wands of the Line:** Standard-issue evocation wands given to Magi-Officers, which can be slotted into shipboard amplification arrays to cast extended-range *Fireballs* during naval combat.
# Chapter 20: Regional Gazetteer - The Grand Merchant Consortium

*â€œWe do not conquer islands, Captain. We purchase them. It is infinitely cheaper than war, and the locals even thank us for it.â€* â€” High Factor Vance of the Gold Scale

The Grand Merchant Consortium is the economic heart of the Shattered Expanse. They are not a traditional nation with borders, but rather a hyper-capitalist coalition of trading companies, banking guilds, and mercenary navies. They believe that gold is the only true magic in the world, and they possess enough of it to buy or bury any threat.

## 1. Geography, Demographics & Hubs

The Consortium's power does not lie in a contiguous landmass, but in their absolute control over the Great Magical Trade Network.

**Population Overview:**
- Total Estimated Population: ~1.2 Million (Direct employees and citizens)
- The High Factors (Ruling Board): <1%
- Guild Members & Contracted Sailors: 40%
- Indentured Laborers & Debtors: 59%

### Port Meridian (Capital Hub)
Port Meridian is a massive, sprawling city-state built across a central, highly strategic archipelago. It is the wealthiest location in the known world. The city is a dizzying array of toll bridges, towering marble banks, and crowded, neon-lit arcane bazaars. The Consortium doesn't "own" the city in a royal senseâ€”they just own the deeds to every single building and ship inside it.
- **The Golden Spire:** The central banking and administration hub where the Board of Factors meets.
- **The Silk Wards:** Opulent districts for high-ranking merchants and visiting diplomats.
- **The Debt Docks:** Slums where those who have defaulted on Consortium loans are forced to work off their debt in the shipyards.

### The Waystations
The Consortium maintains dozens of heavily fortified, neutral trading posts scattered across the Charted Waters. These serve as safe havens, resupply points, and banking outposts.

## 2. Magic Systems Integration

The Consortium views magic purely as a commodity and a logistical tool.

- **Arcane Magic (Logistics & Profit):** The primary magic used by the Consortium. They employ wizards not as soldiers, but as navigators, weather-controllers, and accountants. Illusion magic is heavily regulated to prevent counterfeiting.
- **Psionics (Contracts & Security):** Psions are highly paid to act as living polygraphs during negotiations and to secure vaults against magical intrusion.
- **Spirit Magic (Commodified):** They don't revere spirits; they commodify them. They harvest rare pearls, coral, and leviathan bones to sell to Arcane researchers at a massive markup.
- **Deep Magic (The Black Market):** The Consortium publicly outlaws Deep Magic, but secretly, wealthy Factors pay exorbitant sums for Drow artifacts pulled from the Sunless Sea.

## 3. Faction Relations (Geopolitical Matrix)

The Consortiumâ€™s foreign policy is simple: maximize profit, minimize risk.

- **Free Captains Alliance:** *Profitable Nuisance.* They publicly condemn piracy and execute pirates to maintain a "safe" image, but secretly hire Free Captains as deniable privateers to sabotage rival trade routes.
- **Crimson Dominion:** *Cold War / Essential Client.* The Dominion is the Consortium's biggest customer for raw materials. The Consortium walks a razor's edge, selling them enough to profit, but not enough to let the Dominion become completely self-sufficient and annex Port Meridian.
- **Drowned Silk Houses (Drow):** *Black Market Partners.* The Consortium officially denies trading with the Drow, while simultaneously maintaining highly lucrative smuggling routes with them through the Drown-Gates.
- **Spirit Keepers:** *Annoying Obstacles.* The Keepers constantly blockade Consortium whaling and logging operations. The Consortium views them as backward zealots holding up progress.
- **Independent Salvagers:** *Primary Investors.* The Consortium funds Salvager expeditions into the Unwritten Sea, retaining exclusive rights to auction off any First Captain technology they find.

## 4. Societal Hierarchy (The Corporate Ladder)

Status in the Consortium is entirely determined by wealth and guild rank.

1. **The Board of Factors:** The CEOs of the massive trading companies. They dictate economic policy, interest rates, and naval deployment.
2. **The Guildmasters:** Heads of specific industries (e.g., The Spice Cartel, The Arcane Materials Guild). 
3. **The Gold Cloaks:** The elite, highly paid mercenary army and private navy that enforces Consortium contracts and protects the trade routes.
4. **The Clerks & Sailors:** The middle class. Bureaucrats, accountants, and contracted sailors who keep the gears of commerce turning.
5. **The Indentured:** Those who fell into debt. They are not technically "slaves" like in the Dominion; they are "contractually obligated laborers." The debt is often mathematically impossible to pay off.

## 5. Internal Politics & Rival Factions

The Consortium is a cutthroat meritocracy where hostile takeovers are literal.

- **The Expansionists:** Factors who believe the Consortium should build a massive military to actively conquer the Unwritten Sea and monopolize First Captain ruins.
- **The Shadow Brokers:** Guilds deeply invested in the black market, wanting to formalize trade with the Drow and the pirate lords.
- **The Debtors' Union:** A growing, violent underground movement of indentured laborers plotting to burn the central bank ledgers to free millions from generational debt.
- **Corporate Sabotage:** Factors routinely hire assassins, privateers, and spies to ruin their rivals' trade routes and stock prices. 

## 6. The Military Machine

The Consortium's military is entirely privatized. They do not have citizens defending a homeland; they have mercenaries defending cargo. Their combat doctrine revolves around risk mitigation, defensive superiority, and overwhelming the enemy with expensive, disposable magical ordnance.

### 6.1 Chain of Command & Ranks
The command structure is organized exactly like a corporate hierarchy, with shares and profit margins replacing traditional medals and honor.
- **The Board of Defense:** A committee of high-ranking guild factors who authorize military budgets and declare bounties.
- **Commodore-Shareholder (Admiral):** The highest rank in the private navy. They hold direct stock in the Consortium's success and command large convoys. 
- **Captain-Contractor (Captain):** Independent operators hired to command escort ships. They are paid massive bonuses for every ship that safely reaches port, and severely fined for lost cargo.
- **Ledger-Mage (First Mate):** A specialized wizard whose job is to calculate combat risk, project damage expenses during a battle, and cast defensive abjuration magic.
- **Gold Cloak Enforcer (Marine):** Elite mercenaries clad in yellow-gold enchanted leather. They are highly paid, well-fed, and armed with masterwork weaponry.

### 6.2 Standard Naval Doctrines
- **The Toll-Gate Defense:** Consortium ships prefer to control chokepoints rather than chase enemies. They set up blockades at narrow straits or trade winds, demanding "protection fees."
- **The Calculated Retreat:** Unlike the Dominion, the Consortium has no honor in fighting to the death. If a battle is deemed mathematically unprofitable by the Ledger-Mage, the ship immediately deploys alchemical smoke clouds, activates enchanted sails for a massive speed boost, and flees.
- **The Iron Bank Boarding:** If forced to board, Gold Cloak Enforcers use wands of *Sleep* or *Glitterdust* to blind and incapacitate the enemy. They do not slaughter crews if they can avoid it; captured pirates are branded and sold into the Debt Docks to recoup the cost of the battle.

### 6.3 Specific Ship Classes
- **The *Ledger*-Class Treasure Galleon (The Vault):** Massive, lumbering behemoths built exclusively to transport fortunes. They are heavily warded and practically unsinkable. Their hulls are reinforced with lead to block divination and teleportation magic.
- **The *Audit*-Class Escort Frigate (The Guard Dog):** Sleek, fast, heavily armed frigates that guard the trade lanes. They lack cargo space entirely, maximizing room for ballistae, crew, and alchemical fire-throwers.
- **The *Dividend*-Class Cutter (The Courier):** Tiny, hyper-fast magical skiffs used for transporting high-value intelligence, magical artifacts, or VIPs. They rely entirely on speed and illusion magic to avoid combat.

### 6.4 Magical Ship Integration
- **Abjuration Shield Emitters:** Expensive ship modifications that project a temporary, regenerative forcefield around the hull, absorbing a set amount of damage before shattering.
- **Alchemical Ordnance:** Consortium ships rarely use heavy iron cannons. They use precision-engineered ballistae that fire glass vials of *Golden Fire* or *Thunderstones*, maximizing collateral damage to enemy rigging while preserving the hull (so it can be salvaged).
- **Communication Runes:** Every Captain-Contractor's cabin contains a secure magical slate linked directly to the Golden Spire, allowing instantaneous market updates and distress signals.

## 7. Notable Figures

- **High Factor Vance:** Head of the Gold Scale (the central banking guild). A meticulously dressed, incredibly wealthy human who treats geopolitics like a ledger. He is currently funding Salvager expeditions to secure First Captain weapons.
- **Guildmaster Seraphina:** Leader of the Spice Cartel. She secretly employs a massive network of Free Captain privateers to sabotage her rivals' shipments, all while maintaining plausible deniability.
- **Captain "Ironclad" Thorne:** Commander of the *Golden Defender*, the flagship of the Gold Cloaks. A highly paid mercenary who cares only for his contract, completely devoid of personal morality.

## 8. Player Character Integration

- **Best Fit Backgrounds:** Guild Artisan, Charlatan, Criminal/Spy, Sailor, Noble.
- **Favored Classes:** Rogue (Swashbuckler, Mastermind), Bard (College of Eloquence), Warlock (The Fathomless - flavored as a contract with a sea entity), Wizard (Divination, Illusion).
- **Roleplay Hook:** A Consortium PC might be a mid-level manager trying to climb the corporate ladder, a debt-dodger on the run from the Gold Cloaks, or a corporate spy embedded in another faction.

## 9. Unique Regional Assets

- **Alchemical Ordnance:** Custom-engineered ballistae that fire glass vials of *Golden Fire* or *Thunderstones*, maximizing collateral damage to enemy rigging while preserving the hull.
- **Contract Marks:** Magical tattoos given to indentured laborers or sworn mercenaries. They act as tracking devices and can inflict pain if the wearer breaches their contract.
- **Golden Fire:** A highly volatile, alchemically modified Greek Fire that burns even underwater. The Consortium sells it at a premium.
# Chapter 21: Regional Gazetteer - The Drowned Silk Houses

*â€œThe surface-dwellers fight over scraps of driftwood and claim they rule the ocean. They do not realize the true power lies in the crushing dark, where their wooden toys splinter and their kings drown.â€* â€” Matron Viconia of House Xorlarrin

The Drowned Silk Houses are not a surface nation; they are an ancient, predatory civilization operating primarily within the Sunless Sea (the Underdark Ocean). They are a fractured society of Drow noble houses who survived a cataclysmic flood centuries ago by adapting their magic to the deep ocean. They view the surface world as a hunting ground, a political chessboard, and a resource pool to be exploited from the shadows.

## 1. Geography, Demographics & Hubs

The Silk Houses exist in a perpetual state of twilight, occupying massive caverns and bioluminescent trenches beneath the ocean floor. 

**Population Overview:**
- Total Estimated Population: ~800,000 (Drow and deep-sea races)
- The Matron Mothers & Nobles: 2%
- The Crimson Guard & Arcane Scholars: 28%
- The Thralls (Enslaved surface races & lesser aquatic beings): 70%

### Nymorath Deep Harbor (Capital Hub)
Nymorath is a breathtaking and terrifying city built into the walls of a massive underwater trench, illuminated by bioluminescent fungi and chained deep-sea leviathans. A massive sphere of magically maintained air encompasses the noble districts, while the lower slums and slave pens are partially flooded, forcing thralls to rely on water-breathing enchantments to survive. 
- **The Obsidian Web:** The central political arena where the Matron Mothers hold court, negotiate alliances, and plot assassinations.
- **The Abyssal Forges:** Where Drow artificers craft weapons from black coral and deep-sea metals that shatter surface steel.
- **The Drown-Gates:** Massive, magical whirlpool-portals that allow their fleets to instantly surface in the Charted Waters for raids.

### The Trench Outposts
Scattered listening posts and forward operating bases located near major surface trade routes, used to monitor the Merchant Consortium and Crimson Dominion.

## 2. Magic Systems Integration

The Drow possess the most advanced, ancient, and dangerous understanding of magic in the Shattered Expanse.

- **Deep Magic (The Foundation):** The Drow are the undisputed masters of Deep Magic. They use it to manipulate gravity, control water pressure, and mutate deep-sea beasts into war mounts. It is inherently tied to darkness and cold.
- **Arcane Magic (Weaponized):** Highly developed, but focused on stealth, illusion, and assassination. Necromancy is widely practiced, utilizing drowned sailors as cheap, mindless labor.
- **Psionics (Espionage):** Used extensively by their elite spies to mind-read captured captains and communicate silently during surface raids.
