- **Spirit Magic (Corrupted):** They capture ocean spirits and systematically torture and corrupt them, twisting them into dark entities to power their submarine fleets.

## 3. Faction Relations (Geopolitical Matrix)

The Drow do not seek open war; they seek absolute, unseen control.

- **Grand Merchant Consortium:** *Lucrative Exploitation.* The Drow officially do not exist according to the Consortium, but in reality, the two factions run massive, secret black-market smuggling rings. The Drow sell ancient artifacts and poisons for surface luxuries and slaves.
- **Crimson Dominion:** *The Shadow War.* The Dominion is the only surface power that poses a military threat. The Drow actively sabotage Dominion shipyards, sink their fleets in "unexplained storms," and assassinate their mages to keep them fractured.
- **Free Captains Alliance:** *Convenient Pawns.* The Drow frequently manipulate pirate fleets into attacking Dominion or Consortium targets by leaking false maps or rumors, using them as unwitting mercenaries.
- **Spirit Keepers:** *Hated Enemies.* The Keepers actively try to heal the ocean spirits the Drow corrupt. The Drow view the Keepers as naive fools and will slaughter them on sight.
- **Independent Salvagers:** *Prey.* The Drow consider all First Captain artifacts in the Unwritten Sea to be their rightful property. They actively hunt Salvager vessels that delve too deep.

## 4. Societal Hierarchy (The Matriarchy)

Drow society is a brutal, paranoid matriarchy where advancement is achieved through betrayal and assassination.

1. **The Matron Mothers:** The absolute rulers of the Silk Houses. High Priestesses of Deep Magic who command unquestioned obedience.
2. **The Noble Houses:** The extended families of the Matrons. They constantly war with one another in secret for political favor.
3. **The Crimson Guard:** Elite, fanatically loyal male warriors and assassins who serve the Matrons.
4. **The Male Arcane Scholars:** While denied true political power, male wizards are highly valued for their magical research and tactical knowledge.
5. **The Thralls:** The enslaved underclass. Comprised of captured surface sailors, tritons, and Kuo-toa. They are considered disposable and are often used as biological experiments.

## 5. Internal Politics & Rival Factions

The Silk Houses are united only in their hatred of the surface; internally, they are a snake pit.

- **House Xorlarrin vs. House Baenre:** A centuries-old blood feud between the two most powerful houses. Xorlarrin favors aggressive surface raids, while Baenre prefers economic manipulation and smuggling.
- **The Bregan D'aerthe (The Mercenaries):** A rogue faction of exiled male nobles and elite assassins who operate independently, selling their services to the highest bidder (even surface dwellers).
- **The Cult of the First Captains:** A heretical, underground religious movement that believes the First Captains were actually ancient Drow gods, and seeks to awaken the slumbering horrors of the Unwritten Sea to wipe out the surface completely.

## 6. The Military Machine

The Drow do not fight fair. They have no standing army in the traditional sense; their military is a collection of house-funded assassination fleets and biological terror weapons. They strike from the shadows, cripple their enemies' logistics, and vanish before retaliation can be organized.

### 6.1 Chain of Command & Ranks
Command is based entirely on House affiliation, gender, and magical prowess. Mutiny is common but must be executed flawlessly to be recognized by the Matrons.
- **The Matron-Admiral (Fleet Commander):** A High Priestess who commands multiple vessels. She does not leave the capital; she commands telepathically through enslaved aboleths or psionic relays.
- **The Web-Master (Captain):** Usually a female cleric or necromancer. They have absolute authority on their vessel and can execute any male crew member without cause.
- **The Shadow-Blade (First Mate/Marine Commander):** The highest rank a male Drow can achieve. They lead the Crimson Guard boarding parties and handle the physical brutality of combat.
- **The Flesh-Weaver (Ship Surgeon/Artificer):** Necromancers and biomancers who maintain the biological functions of their undead or mutated vessels.
- **The Crimson Guard (Marines):** Elite male Drow warriors armed with poisoned hand-crossbows, dual rapiers, and innate darkness magic.

### 6.2 Standard Naval Doctrines
- **The Abyssal Breach:** Drow vessels do not sail on the surface. They track surface targets from hundreds of feet below using sonar and psionics. When the target is isolated, the Drow vessel rapidly ascends, breaching the surface directly beneath or alongside the enemy ship, creating massive rogue waves that capsize smaller escorts.
- **The Silent Boarding:** As the Drow ship breaches, the Crimson Guard casts *Darkness* directly onto the enemy's command deck and cannon batteries. They board in total silence using spider-climb boots, specifically targeting officers and spellcasters with sleep poison before the crew can mount a defense.
- **The Leviathan's Grip:** If a target is too heavily armored (like a Dominion Dreadnought), the Drow do not board. They deploy mutated giant squids or undead kraken tentacles to wrap around the enemy hull, dragging the entire vessel beneath the waves to crush it with atmospheric pressure.

### 6.3 Specific Ship Classes
- **The *Arachna*-Class Submersible (The Hunter):** A 100-foot-long biological vessel crafted from the hollowed-out chitin of a massive deep-sea crustacean. Propelled by magical impellers, it carries 20 Crimson Guard.
- **The *Necropolis*-Class Carrier (The Hive):** A horrifying vessel built into the ribbed skeleton of an ancient, undead whale. It serves as a mobile forward operating base, carrying hundreds of disposable Kuo-toa and drowned zombie thralls that are deployed as cannon fodder.
- **The *Viper*-Class Skiff (The Assassin):** A tiny, sleek, magically-silenced submersible used strictly for deploying high-value assassins (like Bregan D'aerthe) into heavily guarded harbors like Port Meridian.

### 6.4 Magical Ship Integration
- **Biological Hulls:** Drow vessels heal over time. Flesh-Weavers can cast *Inflict Wounds* or *Harm* on the hull to rapidly seal breaches during combat (since the vessels are undead or Aberrations).
- **Sonic Projectors:** Mounted in the 'maw' of their vessels. They fire concentrated blasts of thundering sound that bypass magical abjuration shields and shatter wooden hulls through sheer vibration.
- **Cryo-Torpedoes:** Living, magically modified eels packed with volatile elemental ice. When they ram an enemy hull, they detonate, instantly freezing the water around the ship and trapping it in a block of ice, making it a sitting duck for boarding.

## 7. Notable Figures

- **Matron Viconia:** The ancient, terrifying head of House Xorlarrin. She is a master of Deep Magic and seeks to completely collapse the surface world's trade network by manipulating the Free Captains into war with the Dominion.
- **Jarlaxle Baenre:** The charismatic, flamboyant leader of Bregan D'aerthe. He operates on the surface more than any other Drow, dealing in secrets, assassinations, and rare magical artifacts. He views the surface world as highly entertaining.
- **Vhaeraun's Blade (Zin):** A heavily scarred, silent male assassin of the Crimson Guard who has begun questioning the absolute rule of the Matron Mothers after encountering a surface-dwelling Cleric of Eilistraee.

## 8. Player Character Integration

- **Best Fit Backgrounds:** Outlander, Urchin, Spy, Faction Agent, Prisoner (Thrall).
- **Favored Classes:** Rogue (Assassin, Arcane Trickster), Ranger (Gloom Stalker), Sorcerer (Aberrant Mind, Shadow), Cleric (Trickery).
- **Roleplay Hook:** A Drow PC is almost certainly an exile, a runaway Thrall, or a Bregan D'aerthe mercenary acting as a "consultant" for a Wayfinder crew. Trust will be a major, ongoing roleplay element.

## 9. Unique Regional Assets

- **Sonic Projectors:** Cannons that fire concentrated blasts of sound instead of iron balls. Highly effective at shattering wood and deafening surface crews.
- **Piwafwi (Cloak of Elvenkind):** Drow cloaks woven from spider silk and infused with shadow magic, granting superior stealth. They degrade rapidly if exposed to direct sunlight for extended periods.
- **Deep-Breather Gills:** Biological symbiotes (often looking like a slimy collar) forced upon Thralls that allow them to breathe underwater but cause intense pain if they remain out of the water for too long.
# Chapter 22: Regional Gazetteer - The Free Captains Alliance

*â€œThe Dominion claims the ocean by divine right. The Consortium claims it by contract. We claim it because our sails are full, our guns are loaded, and the horizon belongs to whoever is bold enough to chase it.â€* â€” Pirate King "Hurricane" Jax

The Free Captains Alliance is not a government; it is a chaotic, rowdy, and fiercely independent confederacy of pirate lords, privateers, explorers, and exiles. They are bound together not by laws, but by a shared hatred of empire and a mutual agreement known as "The Corsair's Code." To the rest of the world, they are lawless terrorists. To themselves, they are the only truly free souls on the Shattered Expanse.

## 1. Geography, Demographics & Hubs

The Alliance operates out of the **Jagged Reefs**, a vast, treacherous maze of razor-sharp coral and shifting sandbars that only the most skilled (or magically guided) navigators can traverse without tearing their hulls open.

**Population Overview:**
- Total Estimated Population: ~400,000 (Highly transient)
- The Pirate Lords (The Brethren Court): <1%
- Wayfinders, Privateers, & Sworn Crews: 60%
- Smugglers, Tavern-Keepers, & Fence Merchants: 39%

### Blackwake Harbor (Capital Hub)
Blackwake Harbor is a massive, floating city that shouldn't exist. It is constructed entirely from lashed-together galleons, ancient driftwood, and salvaged hulls suspended within a calm lagoon in the center of the Jagged Reefs. It is a city of rope bridges, swinging lanterns, and perpetual celebration (or brawling).
- **The Brethren's Table:** A massive, hollowed-out hull of a first-rate ship-of-the-line where the Pirate Lords meet to vote on the rare occasions the Alliance needs to act as a unified fleet.
- **The Powder Keg:** The chaotic tavern district, where fortunes are won and lost on games of dice, and where most crews are recruited.
- **The Chop Shop:** A massive drydock dedicated to refitting stolen ships, scrubbing their identifying marks, and outfitting them with illegal weaponry.

### The Hidden Coves
The Alliance maintains dozens of secret inlets, hidden coves, and disguised anchorages throughout the Charted Waters, allowing them to strike and vanish before the Dominion or Consortium can respond.

## 2. Magic Systems Integration

The Free Captains are highly pragmatic about magic; if it keeps the ship afloat or helps them steal gold, they use it.

- **Spirit Magic (The Lifeline):** The most common and respected magic among the Captains. Many crews employ shamans to speak with the winds, beg safe passage from minor ocean spirits, and bond with Navigator Spirits to guide them through the Reefs.
- **Arcane Magic (Stolen & Scavenged):** They rarely have formally trained wizards. Instead, they rely on rogue sorcerers, stolen wands, and scavenged scrolls. Their magic is chaotic, loud, and unpredictable.
- **Deep Magic (Taboo):** Most Captains are highly superstitious and consider the deep ocean cursed. Those who practice Deep Magic are viewed with intense suspicion and are often accused of making deals with the Drow.
- **Psionics (Untrusted):** Psions are rarely found on pirate ships. The concept of someone reading thoughts violates the core tenet of piratical freedom; mind-readers are usually thrown overboard.

## 3. Faction Relations (Geopolitical Matrix)

The Alliance views the world through a lens of targets, threats, and temporary allies.

- **Crimson Dominion:** *Kill on Sight.* The Dominion represents everything the Captains hate: slavery, conformity, and imperial control. The Alliance will actively target Dominion slaver ships and border forts.
- **Grand Merchant Consortium:** *Primary Targets (But Occasional Employers).* Consortium treasure galleons are the ultimate prize. However, the Captains are pragmatic; they will gladly accept a Consortium "Letter of Marque" to act as privateers against the Dominion, provided the gold is good.
- **Drowned Silk Houses (Drow):** *Superstitious Terror.* The Free Captains rarely engage the Drow willingly. When ships go missing in calm waters, the pirates blame the Drow. 
- **Spirit Keepers:** *Wary Respect.* The Keepers are one of the few factions the Pirates try not to anger. A pirate captain knows better than to hunt a leviathan in Keeper territory, lest the ocean itself turn against them.
- **Independent Salvagers:** *Friendly Rivals.* The Captains frequently trade with the Salvagers. They often race each other into the Unwritten Sea to claim First Captain wrecks.

## 4. Societal Hierarchy (The Meritocracy)

In Blackwake Harbor, nobility means nothing. You are only worth the strength of your sword arm, the loyalty of your crew, and the size of your ship.

1. **The Pirate Lords:** The most feared and successful captains in the Expanse. They command massive fleets (armadas) and hold a seat on the Brethren Court.
2. **The Wayfinders / Captains:** Independent ship commanders. Within the Alliance, every Captain is considered a sovereign ruler of their own deck.
3. **The Quartermasters:** Highly respected officers elected by the crew to represent their interests, distribute loot, and ensure the Captain doesn't abuse their power.
4. **The Sworn Crew:** The sailors who have sworn the Corsair's Code. They are guaranteed a share of the plunder and the right to vote on the ship's destination.
5. **The Scallywags (Landlubbers):** The un-sworn. Merchants, tavern keeps, and dockworkers who live in Blackwake Harbor but do not sail.

## 5. Internal Politics & Rival Factions

The Alliance is constantly on the verge of tearing itself apart, held together only by external threats.

- **The Old Guard:** Traditional pirate lords who believe the Alliance should focus purely on raiding the Consortium and enjoying their freedom.
- **The Abolitionists:** Radical captains who believe the Alliance's primary goal should be a full-scale war to destroy the Crimson Dominion and free the slave caste.
- **The Traitor Captains:** Crews who secretly accept pardons and paychecks from the Dominion to hunt down other pirates (the ultimate sin in Blackwake).
- **The Mutiny Problem:** Every Captain is one bad voyage away from being overthrown by their own crew. Democracy on a pirate ship is a double-edged sword.

## 6. The Military Machine

The Free Captains do not have a uniform standing navy. They fight a guerrilla war at sea characterized by extreme independence, unpredictable tactics, and highly customized vessels. They are the undisputed masters of asymmetrical naval warfare.

### 6.1 Chain of Command & Ranks
There is no centralized admiralty. Command structures exist only on a per-ship basis or temporary armadas.
- **Pirate King / Queen (Armada Commander):** A temporary rank granted by the Brethren Court only when a massive, unified fleet is required to repel a Dominion invasion.
- **Wayfinder / Captain (Sovereign Leader):** The absolute authority on a ship during combat. However, their position relies on the continued consent of the crew.
- **Quartermaster (The Adjudicator):** The second-in-command, elected by the crew. They lead the boarding parties, distribute the plunder, and step in if the Captain makes decisions that threaten the ship's survival.
- **The Powder Monkey (Artillery Chief):** Given the volatile nature of black powder and scavenged arcane weapons, this role is highly respected and heavily hazard-paid.
- **Sworn Corsair (Marine/Sailor):** There is no distinction between a sailor and a marine in the Alliance. Every deckhand is expected to fight, and every fighter is expected to rig the sails.

### 6.2 Standard Naval Doctrines
- **The Fog Ambush:** Free Captains rarely engage in the open ocean where Dominion Dreadnoughts can out-range them. They use Spirit Magic or weather-controlling artifacts to conjure blinding fog banks, hiding within them until they are mere feet from the enemy hull.
- **The Swarm Tactic (The Wolfpack):** When attacking a massive target like a Consortium Treasure Galleon, Free Captains coordinate strikes from 4 or 5 smaller, hyper-fast ships. They circle the target, tearing down the rigging and harassing the defenders, waiting for the heavy ship to run out of magical ordnance before closing in for the kill.
- **The "No Quarter" Boarding:** Unlike the Consortium's sleep magic or the Dominion's methodical slaughter, an Alliance boarding action is pure, terrifying chaos. Corsairs swing across on grappling lines, swinging cutlasses, firing flintlock pistols, and screaming sea-shanties. The psychological shock is often enough to force a quick surrender.

### 6.3 Specific Ship Classes
- **The *Zephyr*-Class Sloop (The Blockade Runner):** Impossibly light and fast, heavily modified to carry more sail than should be structurally sound. They are fragile but can outrun anything on the ocean. Used primarily by smugglers and scouts.
- **The *Corsair*-Class Brigantine (The Raider):** The backbone of the pirate fleets. Fast enough to catch merchant galleons, armed well enough to fight off light escorts. They are usually bristling with black-powder cannons and heavy grappling winches.
- **The *Kraken*-Class Ship-of-the-Line (The Pirate Lord's Flagship):** Extremely rare. These are usually stolen Dominion or Consortium heavy warships that have been aggressively retrofitted. They sacrifice armor and luxury for raw speed, extra cannons, and massive ramming prows.

### 6.4 Magical Ship Integration
- **Scavenger Sails:** Canvas sails patched with the hides of minor elementals or sea monsters, allowing a ship to catch magical Aether winds that normal canvas would tear in.
- **Jury-Rigged Ballistae:** When pirates steal Dominion Arcane Ballistae, they often lack the magical training to use them properly. Instead, they hot-wire them with unstable elemental shards, resulting in incredibly destructive, but highly dangerous, weapons.
- **Living Figureheads:** Wealthy captains hire shamans to bind minor spirits (like water nymphs or wind sylphs) directly into the ship's wooden figurehead, granting the vessel a rudimentary personality and minor localized weather control.

## 7. Notable Figures

- **Pirate King "Hurricane" Jax:** The boisterous, dangerously unpredictable leader of the Brethren Court. A Storm Sorcerer who claims to have won his crown in a drinking contest with a kraken. 
- **Captain Elara "Ghost-Wake" Vance:** A brilliant tactician and defector from the Crimson Dominion's Magi-Officers. She commands the *Reckoning*, a heavily modified frigate dedicated solely to hunting Dominion slaver ships.
- **Quartermaster Thatch:** The pragmatic, ruthless manager of Blackwake's "Chop Shop." If you need a stolen ship's identity scrubbed or a cursed artifact fenced, Thatch is the man to see.

## 8. Player Character Integration

- **Best Fit Backgrounds:** Sailor, Pirate, Criminal, Folk Hero, Entertainer.
- **Favored Classes:** Fighter (Battle Master, Champion), Rogue (Swashbuckler, Thief), Barbarian (Storm Herald, Berserker), Sorcerer (Storm Sorcery).
- **Roleplay Hook:** A Free Captain PC might be a newly minted Wayfinder looking to make a name for themselves, a runaway seeking true freedom, or an exiled noble seeking revenge against the faction that wronged them.

## 9. Unique Regional Assets

- **Black-Powder Firearms:** The Alliance is the primary manufacturer and user of flintlock pistols and muskets. While less reliable than a wand, they don't require magical aptitude to blow a hole in a Dominion marine.
- **Scavenger Sails:** Canvas sails patched with the hides of minor elementals or sea monsters, allowing a ship to catch magical Aether winds that normal canvas would tear in.
- **The Corsair's Coin:** A magically minted coin given to Sworn Crew. It acts as a token of safe passage in Blackwake Harbor and can cast *Sending* once to contact the ship's Quartermaster in an emergency.
# Chapter 23: Regional Gazetteer - The Spirit Keepers

*â€œThe ocean is not a road for your merchants, nor a battlefield for your empires. It is a living, breathing entity, and you are sailing across its open wounds. We are the immune system.â€* â€” High Elder Naia of the Kelp Wardens

The Spirit Keepers are a reclusive, deeply spiritual, and fiercely territorial faction dedicated to preserving the ancient magic of the ocean. They believe that the Shattered Expanse is fundamentally broken, kept alive only by the slumbering leviathans and the chaotic elemental spirits that inhabit the Aether Streams. To the Consortium, they are ecoterrorists. To the Dominion, they are primitive heretics. To the Keepers themselves, they are the only thing preventing the world from drowning.

## 1. Geography, Demographics & Hubs

The Spirit Keepers do not build cities of stone or iron. They reside within the **Verdant Expanse**, a massive, magically accelerated kelp forest that stretches for hundreds of miles, thick enough to entangle and sink heavy galleons. 

**Population Overview:**
- Total Estimated Population: ~250,000
- The Council of Elders: <1%
- The Wardens & Shamans: 30%
- The Tenders (Civilians & Gatherers): 69%

### The Last Harbor (Capital Hub)
The Last Harbor is a hidden sanctuary located deep within the Verdant Expanse. It is woven directly into the canopy of ancient, petrified mangrove trees that rise hundreds of feet out of the water. The harbor is not visible from the open ocean; a ship must be guided by a bonded Navigator Spirit to find the entrance through the shifting kelp walls.
- **The Heartwood Spire:** A massive, hollowed-out ancient tree where the Council of Elders communes directly with ancient ocean spirits.
- **The Leviathan Nurseries:** Protected lagoons where the Keepers tend to wounded sea beasts and foster the growth of young elementals.
- **The Wreckage Gardens:** Instead of a traditional shipyard, the Keepers use magic to accelerate coral and kelp growth over sunken ships, creating living, organic vessels.

### The Aether Shrines
Small, heavily warded outposts located along the major magical currents (Aether Streams). The Keepers use these to monitor the flow of magic and detect arcane pollution from the Dominion or Consortium.

## 2. Magic Systems Integration

The Keepers' entire society is built around a symbiotic relationship with magic, rather than exploitation.

- **Spirit Magic (The Core):** The foundation of their culture. Every Keeper ship is bonded with a minor elemental or spirit. Their shamans can summon localized storms, command the tides, and communicate over vast distances through the water itself.
- **Divine Magic (Revered):** They have a high concentration of Clerics and Druids dedicated to nature deities or the ocean itself. Healing and preservation are highly valued.
- **Arcane Magic (Distrusted):** They view Arcane magicâ€”specifically Evocation and Necromancyâ€”as a brutal, unnatural forcing of the world's laws. They consider Dominion wizards to be dangerous vandals.
- **Deep Magic (The Enemy):** They recognize Deep Magic as a corrupting, horrific force. They know what the Drow do to ocean spirits, and they consider the eradication of Deep Magic to be a holy crusade.

## 3. Faction Relations (Geopolitical Matrix)

The Keepers are inherently defensive, but they will strike with overwhelming, terrifying force if their sanctuaries are threatened.

- **Crimson Dominion:** *Holy War.* The Dominion hunts leviathans for components and pollutes the Aether Streams with arcane engines. The Keepers actively sink Dominion logging and whaling fleets.
- **Grand Merchant Consortium:** *Hostile Blockade.* The Keepers despise the Consortium's rampant industrialization and overfishing. They frequently blockade Consortium trade routes, using summoned storms to force merchant galleons to turn back or sink.
- **Drowned Silk Houses (Drow):** *Mortal Enemies.* The Drow corrupt the ocean spirits that the Keepers revere. The Keepers have specialized strike teams (The Deep-Divers) trained specifically to hunt Drow submarines and cleanse corrupted waters.
- **Free Captains Alliance:** *Wary Neutrality.* The pirates respect the power of the ocean and usually leave the Verdant Expanse alone. In return, the Keepers occasionally offer sanctuary to pirate crews fleeing the Dominion, provided they swear an oath to harm no spirits.
- **Independent Salvagers:** *Suspicious Watchers.* The Keepers allow the Salvagers to explore, but they carefully monitor what artifacts are pulled from the Unwritten Sea, intervening if they believe a relic is too dangerous for humanity to possess.

## 4. Societal Hierarchy (The Symbiocracy)

Status among the Keepers is not determined by wealth or bloodline, but by one's connection to the spirits.

1. **The Council of Elders:** The oldest and most spiritually attuned shamans and druids. They do not give orders; they interpret the will of the ocean.
2. **The Wardens:** The elite warriors and captains. They are physically bonded to minor elementals, granting them supernatural speed and strength in the water.
3. **The Shamans / Navigators:** The spellcasters who maintain the Aether Shrines and guide the organic ships through the ocean.
4. **The Tenders:** The civilian population who cultivate the kelp forests, craft organic weapons, and raise the young. 
5. **The Penitent:** Outsiders (often ex-pirates or defectors from the Dominion) who are seeking redemption and are serving a period of grueling, humbling labor to prove their respect for the ocean.

## 5. Internal Politics & Rival Factions

The Keepers are generally united in purpose, but deeply divided on methodology.

- **The Preservers (The Majority):** Believe the Keepers should remain isolationist, defending the Verdant Expanse and healing the ocean slowly.
- **The Cleansing Tide:** A radical, militant sect that believes the only way to save the ocean is to actively wage war on the surface factions and completely dismantle the Dominion and Consortium.
- **The Firstborn Cult:** A secretive group that believes the First Captains were not heroes, but the original corrupters of the ocean, and that all First Captain artifacts must be destroyed, putting them in direct conflict with the Salvagers.

## 6. The Military Machine

The Keepers do not fight in line-of-battle formations, nor do they possess a standardized navy. They are protectors of the deep, utilizing environmental warfare, symbiotic magic, and the wrath of the ocean itself as their primary weapons.

### 6.1 Chain of Command & Ranks
Their military structure is highly decentralized, based entirely on spiritual attunement rather than military discipline.
- **The High Elder (Grand Shaman):** They do not issue combat orders directly; they commune with the Aether Streams to declare a region "sacred" or "lost," authorizing the Wardens to use lethal force.
- **The Grove-Tender (Fleet Commander):** A senior Druid or Shaman who coordinates the defense of a large sector of the Verdant Expanse. They communicate via water-conduits and animal messengers.
- **The Reef-Warden (Captain):** The commanding officer of a single Living Ship. They are physically and spiritually bonded to the vessel's animating spirit. If the ship dies, the Reef-Warden often suffers catastrophic psychic backlash.
- **The Tide-Caller (First Mate):** Specialized weather-mages whose sole duty in combat is to manipulate the wind and current, allowing the Living Ship to maneuver impossibly fast against the wind.
- **The Coral-Bound (Marines):** Elite warriors who have undergone a ritual grafting of living coral armor to their skin. They wield weapons carved from leviathan bone and enchanted kelp-whips.

### 6.2 Standard Naval Doctrines
- **The Wrath of the Deep (Environmental Warfare):** Keepers rarely engage in broadside cannon duels. They use *Control Water* to create massive whirlpools beneath enemy fleets, or summon localized squalls to shatter masts before moving in.
- **The Symbiotic Boarding:** Keepers do not use grappling hooks. Their Living Ships physically ram the enemy vessel, and the magically accelerated kelp of their hull rapidly grows and entwines with the enemy ship, locking them together. The Coral-Bound then cross the living bridge to engage in melee.
- **The Leviathan's Call:** In dire situations (such as a Dominion Dreadnought attack), a Reef-Warden will sacrifice their own life force to sing the "Deep Song," summoning a slumbering, gargantuan sea beast to shatter the enemy fleet from below.

### 6.3 Specific Ship Classes
- **The *Bramble*-Class Skiff (The Scout):** Small, incredibly fast interceptors grown from flexible mangrove roots. They are practically invisible when resting against a coastline or within a kelp forest.
- **The *Iron-Reef*-Class Galleon (The Defender):** The core combat vessel. The hull is a calcified, incredibly dense mass of living coral. They are slow but possess an armor class and regeneration rate that frustrates traditional artillery.
- **The *World-Tree*-Class Carrier (The Sanctuary):** Extremely rare. These are massive, floating island-ships grown around a single ancient seed from the Heartwood Spire. They serve as mobile bases of operation and carry entire pods of bonded sea beasts (like dire sharks or elemental water weirds) that they deploy into combat.

### 6.4 Magical Ship Integration
- **Living Hull Plating:** The ships bleed sap instead of splintering. They regenerate hull points passively, and Reef-Wardens can cast *Cure Light Wounds* directly on the ship to repair it instantly.
- **Coral-Shard Ballistae:** Instead of heavy iron cannons, Keepers use massive ballistae that fire magically pressurized, razor-sharp coral shards. When the shard strikes wood or flesh, it violently splinters into hundreds of pieces.
- **Aether-Sails:** They do not use canvas. Their sails are woven from bioluminescent, gossamer-like algae that catches magical Aether currents, allowing them to sail swiftly even in a dead calm.

## 7. Notable Figures

- **High Elder Naia:** The ancient, serene voice of the Council of Elders. She is bonded to a massive, ancient sea turtle and advocates for isolationism and healing the ocean, rather than war.
- **Warden Kael:** The aggressive, militant leader of the Cleansing Tide. He believes the only way to protect the Verdant Expanse is to sink every Dominion and Consortium ship that dares cross the horizon.
- **Tender Maris:** A highly skilled crafter of "Living Ships" and a sympathetic contact for Wayfinders. She frequently trades minor magical charms to independent captains in exchange for news of the outside world.

## 8. Player Character Integration

- **Best Fit Backgrounds:** Hermit, Outlander, Sailor, Acolyte, Folk Hero.
- **Favored Classes:** Druid (Circle of the Land - Coast, Circle of the Moon), Cleric (Nature, Tempest), Ranger (Swarmkeeper, Hunter), Monk (Way of the Four Elements).
- **Roleplay Hook:** A Keeper PC is likely on a sacred pilgrimage to find the source of the Aether Streams, a Warden hunting a specific Drow that escaped, or a member of the "Penitent" trying to work off a past sin against the ocean.

## 9. Unique Regional Assets

- **Living Hull Plating:** Magically grown iron-wood kelp and coral that can be grafted onto a standard ship. It provides temporary hit points to the hull and slowly regenerates over time when submerged in seawater.
- **Breath-Weed:** A highly prized, magically cultivated kelp that, when chewed, grants the user the ability to breathe underwater for 1 hour. The Keepers trade this very rarely.
- **Storm-Callers:** Conches infused with elemental air magic. When blown, they can alter the direction of the wind for a short duration, crucial for escaping a naval ambush.
# Chapter 24: Regional Gazetteer - The Independent Salvagers

*â€œThe world ended once already. The evidence is scattered across the ocean floor. The fools in the Dominion think they can ignore it. We know that whoever controls the wreckage of the past, controls the future.â€* â€” Chief Archaeologist Silas Vance

The Independent Salvagers (often referred to simply as "The Guild") are a faction of extreme risk-takers, rogue historians, artificers, and deep-sea divers. They are dedicated to one singular, incredibly dangerous goal: retrieving the lost, highly advanced, and highly unstable technology of the "First Captains" from the Unwritten Sea. They operate on the fringes of society, driven by a mix of scholarly obsession and raw greed.

## 1. Geography, Demographics & Hubs

The Salvagers do not hold territory in the traditional sense. They are nomadic, following rumors and shifting Aether currents toward ancient ruins. However, they maintain one permanent, neutral stronghold.

**Population Overview:**
- Total Estimated Population: ~150,000 (Highly dispersed)
- The Guild Masters (Lead Archaeologists): 2%
- The Divers & Artificers (Active Crews): 48%
- Support Staff, Appraisers, & Fences: 50%

### Salvager's Haven (Capital Hub)
Salvager's Haven is built entirely inside the skeletal remains of a truly massive, petrified leviathan that washed up on a barren atoll near the edge of the Unwritten Sea. The ribs of the beast arch over the city like a cathedral ceiling, draped in heavy canvas to keep out the harsh sun. It is a city of workshops, black markets, and intense paranoia.
- **The Athenaeum:** A heavily guarded library where recovered First Captain logs, star-charts, and technical schematics are translated and hoarded.
- **The Decompression Wards:** Hospitals specifically designed to treat divers suffering from "The Bends" or magical radiation poisoning from handling unstable artifacts.
- **The Auction Block:** A massive, reinforced amphitheater where the Consortium and the Dominion quietly outbid each other for recovered weaponry.

### The Diving Bells (Mobile Outposts)
Massive, heavily armored floating platforms equipped with heavy cranes and diving bells, acting as temporary forward operating bases above newly discovered underwater ruins.

## 2. Magic Systems Integration

The Salvagers use magic purely as a tool to aid their excavations and survive the crushing depths.

- **Arcane Magic (The Toolkit):** Abjuration is highly valued for shielding against traps and magical radiation. Transmutation is essential for *Water Breathing* and manipulating heavy wreckage.
- **Deep Magic (The Necessary Evil):** They are the only surface faction that actively studies Deep Magic. They don't worship it like the Drow, but they use it to counteract the crushing pressure of the deep ocean. This makes them highly suspicious to other factions.
- **Psionics (Artifact Bonding):** Many First Captain artifacts require a psionic "handshake" to operate. Psions are highly prized and highly paid to act as keys to these ancient machines.
- **Spirit Magic (Ignored):** They view Spirit Magic as superstitious nonsense that gets in the way of scientific discovery.

## 3. Faction Relations (Geopolitical Matrix)

The Salvagers try to remain neutral, playing the major powers against each other to fund their expeditions.

- **Grand Merchant Consortium:** *Primary Benefactors.* The Consortium funds most Salvager expeditions in exchange for the first right of refusal on any discovered technology. The relationship is highly transactional.
- **Crimson Dominion:** *Tense Tolerance.* The Dominion wants the artifacts but hates paying for them. They frequently try to bully or annex Salvager fleets, forcing the Salvagers to hire Free Captains as escorts.
- **Free Captains Alliance:** *Friendly Associates.* The pirates provide protection, and the Salvagers fence strange magical loot the pirates don't understand. Many Wayfinders consider themselves freelance Salvagers.
- **Drowned Silk Houses (Drow):** *Lethal Competition.* The Drow consider the Unwritten Sea their territory. Encounters between Drow submarines and Salvager diving bells always end in violence.
- **Spirit Keepers:** *The Watchdogs.* The Keepers despise the Salvagers for unearthing things that should remain buried. They will actively sink a Salvager ship if they believe the artifact on board is too dangerous.

## 4. Societal Hierarchy (The Academic Meritocracy)

Rank in the Guild is determined by what you have discovered, and more importantly, what you have survived.

1. **The Guild Masters (Chief Archaeologists):** The few who have successfully commanded major expeditions into the Unwritten Sea and returned with paradigm-shifting technology.
2. **The Artificers / Translators:** The scholars who can actually figure out how to make First Captain technology work without it detonating.
3. **The Master Divers:** The veterans. Anyone who has survived more than ten deep-sea dives into ancient ruins is treated with immense respect (and usually sports several magical prosthetics).
4. **The Dredgers:** The rank-and-file sailors and laborers who operate the cranes, winches, and diving bells.
5. **The Investors:** Wealthy outsiders (usually Consortium) who fund the expeditions but have no operational authority. 

## 5. Internal Politics & Rival Factions

The Guild is fractured by differing philosophies on what to do with the technology they find.

- **The Preservationists:** Believe artifacts should be studied, cataloged, and kept in the Athenaeum for the good of all, rather than sold to warmongers.
- **The Profiteers:** Believe the only purpose of the Guild is to get rich selling weapons to the Dominion and Consortium.
- **The Integrationists:** Radical artificers who actively replace their own body parts with First Captain technology, viewing it as the next step in human evolution.
- **The Curse-Touched:** Divers who have been driven mad by the magical radiation of the deep ruins, who occasionally form violent, unpredictable cults within the lower levels of the Haven.

## 6. The Military Machine

The Salvagers do not have a standing army. They view naval combat as a massive waste of resources and an unnecessary risk to their precious cargo. However, when cornered, they are the most unpredictable and terrifying combatants on the water, relying on experimental, highly volatile First Captain weaponry.

### 6.1 Chain of Command & Ranks
There is no military hierarchy; rank is determined entirely by academic standing and technical expertise.
- **The Principal Investigator (Captain):** The academic lead who funds and directs the expedition. They rarely have combat experience, leaving tactical decisions to mercenaries.
- **The Chief Engineer (First Mate):** The most important person on the ship when things go wrong. They maintain the containment fields on the unstable artifacts in the cargo hold.
- **The Master Diver (Operations Commander):** The veteran who leads the descent into the ruins. In ship-to-ship combat, they usually command the defense of the lower decks.
- **Contracted Muscle (Marines):** Hired Free Captains or independent mercenaries paid to stand on the deck and shoot anything that gets too close.

### 6.2 Standard Naval Doctrines
- **The Evasive Protocol:** If a Salvager ship is attacked, the first priority is never to return fire; it is to spool up the Aether-Drives and flee. They will jettison non-essential cargo (and sometimes non-essential crew) to increase speed.
- **The Scorched-Sea Defense:** If trapped, the Chief Engineer will jury-rig whatever volatile First Captain artifacts they recently hauled up. This could result in creating a localized gravity inversion, summoning a swarm of mechanical constructs, or accidentally vaporizing half their own ship along with the enemy.
- **The Deep Dive Retreat:** As a last resort, some advanced Salvager vessels can magically seal their upper decks and rapidly alter their buoyancy, sinking like a stone to the ocean floor to escape surface attackers (praying the hull holds against the pressure).

### 6.3 Specific Ship Classes
- **The *Bathysphere*-Class Dredger (The Workhorse):** Slow, incredibly sturdy, and bristling with heavy winches, decompression chambers, and diving bells. They look like floating factories and move like wounded whales.
- **The *Archivist*-Class Corvette (The Scout):** Fast, lightly armored ships used to chart new Aether Streams and locate ruins before the Consortium or Dominion can find them.
- **The *Goliath*-Class Mobile Platform (The Drill):** Massive, semi-stationary rigs that drop anchor over a major ruin. They are heavily armored and usually surrounded by an escort of hired pirate ships. 

### 6.4 Magical Ship Integration
- **Unstable Energy Cores:** Salvager ships are often retrofitted to run on glowing, humming power cores pulled from the deep. These allow for incredible bursts of speed or temporary forcefields, but a critical hit to the engine room usually results in a localized arcane nuclear explosion.
- **Decompression Wards:** The lower decks of Salvager ships are heavily enchanted with abjuration magic to regulate pressure, allowing divers to rapidly ascend without their blood boiling.
- **Grav-Cranes:** Instead of relying purely on block-and-tackle, Salvagers use reverse-engineered gravity manipulators to lift multi-ton statues and stone tablets from the ocean floor with ease.

## 7. Notable Figures

- **Chief Archaeologist Silas Vance:** An aging, paranoid genius who has integrated several First Captain ocular implants into his skull. He firmly believes the First Captains didn't die out, but left the planet entirely.
- **Master Diver "Iron-Lung" Kael:** A legendary diver whose torso is almost entirely encased in a magical, pressure-regulating suit. He is the only man known to have survived a direct encounter with a Drow Leviathan and lived to tell the tale.
- **Appraiser Lira:** The ruthless head of the Auction Block. She speaks seven languages, can identify a fake artifact at fifty paces, and has the Consortium wrapped around her finger.

## 8. Player Character Integration

- **Best Fit Backgrounds:** Sage, Guild Artisan, Sailor, Charlatan, Hermit.
- **Favored Classes:** Artificer (All subclasses, especially Battlesmith and Armorer), Wizard (Transmutation, Scribes), Rogue (Thief, Arcane Trickster), Fighter (Psi Warrior).
- **Roleplay Hook:** A Salvager PC is likely an adrenaline junkie, an academic desperate to make a name for themselves, or someone severely in debt to the Consortium looking for one big score in the Unwritten Sea.

## 9. Unique Regional Assets

- **Diving Suits (Pressure Armor):** Heavy, magically sealed suits of armor that allow deep-sea exploration, providing immunity to the crushing pressure of the depths but imposing disadvantage on Dexterity checks.
- **Unstable Energy Cores:** Glowing crystals pulled from ruins. They can be used to instantly recharge spell slots or power magic items, but carry a high risk of wild magic surges or explosive failure.
- **The Translator's Monocle:** A highly coveted magic item that casts *Comprehend Languages* specifically calibrated for ancient, dead scripts and First Captain cipher logs.
# Chapter 25: Religion & The Cosmic Forces

*â€œThe mainlanders pray to gods of iron and gold. They build cathedrals to shield themselves from the rain. But out here on the open ocean, when the sky turns black and the waves crest higher than your mast, you donâ€™t pray for a contract or a law. You pray to whatever entity is currently deciding whether or not to swallow you whole.â€* â€” Wayfinder Chaplain Silas

Religion in the Shattered Expanse is deeply divided. Unlike other fantasy settings where a unified pantheon watches over the world, the Charted Waters are caught in a theological cold war. On one side are the "New Gods"â€”deities of civilization, law, commerce, and human ambition, brought by the empires that settled the islands. On the other side are the "Old Entities"â€”primordial, terrifying forces of nature, chaos, and the deep ocean that were here long before humanity arrived.

---

## 1. The Civilized Pantheon (The "New Gods")

The New Gods are the deities of the mainland, the Crimson Dominion, and the Grand Merchant Consortium. They represent the triumph of civilization over the wild.

### The Iron Arbiter
**God of Law, Conquest, and the Forge**
- **Alignment:** Lawful Neutral
- **Suggested Domains:** Order, War, Forge
- **Worshippers:** The Crimson Dominion, magistrates, soldiers, armorers.
- **Lore:** Depicted as a massive, faceless knight clad in impenetrable plate armor, holding a perfectly balanced scale in one hand and a heavy maul in the other. He teaches that the world is chaotic and must be brought to heel through discipline, strict hierarchy, and military might. The Dominion views him as their patron deity, using his doctrine to justify their subjugation of the Expanse.
- **Symbol:** A red anvil struck by an iron hammer.

### The Golden Ledger
**Deity of Commerce, Contracts, and Wealth**
- **Alignment:** True Neutral
- **Suggested Domains:** Knowledge, Trickery
- **Worshippers:** The Grand Merchant Consortium, bankers, smugglers, guildmasters.
- **Lore:** A genderless, multifaceted entity often depicted as a golden figure holding an endlessly unspooling scroll. The Golden Ledger does not care about good or evil, only that debts are paid, contracts are honored, and wealth circulates. The high priests of the Ledger are essentially divine accountants. To break a sworn contract in the Consortium is not just illegal; it is a profound sin against the Ledger.
- **Symbol:** A golden coin with a keyhole in the center.

### The Guiding Star
**Goddess of Navigation, Hope, and Safe Voyages**
- **Alignment:** Neutral Good
- **Suggested Domains:** Light, Twilight, Peace
- **Worshippers:** Wayfinders, honest sailors, navigators, cartographers.
- **Lore:** Depicted as a glowing, ethereal woman holding a celestial astrolabe. She is the most widely worshipped of the New Gods among the common folk of the Expanse. She does not calm the stormsâ€”that is the domain of the Old Entitiesâ€”but she provides the light to navigate through them.
- **Symbol:** A silver, eight-pointed compass rose.

### The Hearth-Keeper
**Goddess of Agriculture, Hearth, and Community**
- **Alignment:** Lawful Good
- **Suggested Domains:** Life, Nature
- **Worshippers:** Island farmers, kelp-harvesters, commoners, healers.
- **Lore:** The islands of the Expanse are harsh, and not everyone is a soldier or a merchant. The Hearth-Keeper is the motherly figure who ensures the kelp farms yield and the coastal storms don't wash away the villages. Her clerics are often local doctors or midwives.
- **Symbol:** A woven basket of glowing kelp.

### The Silent Archivist
**God of Secrets, History, and Espionage**
- **Alignment:** True Neutral
- **Suggested Domains:** Knowledge, Trickery
- **Worshippers:** Spies, intelligence officers, scholars, the Consortium's "Shadow Brokers".
- **Lore:** A cloaked figure whose face is a blank scroll. The Archivist teaches that knowledge is the ultimate weapon, and that history belongs to those who control the narrative. Temples to the Archivist are hidden libraries where secrets are traded like currency.
- **Symbol:** A closed eye with a keyhole in the pupil.

---

## 2. The Free Gods (Deities of Chaos)

The Free Captains and outcasts of the Expanse often reject organized religion, but they hold deep superstitions and revere certain chaotic forces.

### The Laughing Storm
**God of Revelry, Rebellion, and Luck**
- **Alignment:** Chaotic Neutral
- **Suggested Domains:** Tempest, Trickery
- **Worshippers:** Free Captains, gamblers, bards, rebels.
- **Lore:** Not so much a god as a personification of pirate philosophy. The Laughing Storm is said to be the spirit of a legendary rogue who stole a divine spark and threw it into the ocean. He rewards the bold, the foolish, and the free. A common pirate blessing is, "May the Storm laugh with you, not at you."
- **Symbol:** A pair of crossed cutlasses over a grinning skull.

### The Drowned Avenger
**Deity of the Sunken Dead, Vengeance, and Mutiny**
- **Alignment:** Chaotic Evil
- **Suggested Domains:** Death, War
- **Worshippers:** Mutineers, the wronged, privateers seeking bloody revenge.
- **Lore:** A grim, relentless entity prayed to by those who have been betrayed. When a sailor is keel-hauled or a slave is tossed overboard, they often use their dying breath to curse their murderers in the Avenger's name. It is said the Avenger commands a ghost fleet of every ship sunk by treachery.
- **Symbol:** A skeletal hand rising from the waves holding a rusted dagger.

---

## 3. The Old Entities (The Primordial Powers)

The Old Entities do not have churches. They do not care about mortal morality. Worshipping them is less about seeking salvation and more about negotiating survival.

### The Slumbering Leviathans
**The Physical Gods of the Ocean**
- **Alignment:** Neutral
- **Suggested Domains:** Nature, Tempest (Cleric) / The Fathomless (Warlock)
- **Worshippers:** The Spirit Keepers, druids, deep-sea shamans.
- **Lore:** The Keepers do not pray to invisible gods in the sky; they pray to the very real, continent-sized beasts sleeping beneath the crust of the ocean. They believe these Leviathans generate the magical Aether Streams that keep the world alive. When a Leviathan stirs, tsunamis wipe out islands. Their clerics draw power directly from the ambient magic these beasts radiate.
- **Symbol:** A stylized wave crashing over a massive eye.

### The First Captains (The "Cargo Cults")
**The Ancient Precursors**
- **Alignment:** Unknown
- **Suggested Domains:** Knowledge (Cleric) / The Great Old One (Warlock)
- **Worshippers:** The Independent Salvagers, Doomsday Cults, mad artificers.
- **Lore:** The extinct civilization that ruled the world before the flood. Some view them as gods who ascended, while others (like the Salvagers) worship their *technology*. This "Cargo Cult" mentality leads artificers to treat schematics like holy scripture, praying to engines to start and performing maintenance as religious rituals. 
- **Symbol:** An intricate, glowing geometric cipher (often tattooed on the worshipper).

### The Deep Mother
**The Abyssal Horror**
- **Alignment:** Chaotic Evil
- **Suggested Domains:** Death, Grave (Cleric) / The Hexblade (Warlock)
- **Worshippers:** The Drowned Silk Houses (Drow), cultists of the deep.
- **Lore:** The horrific entity that the Drow turned to when the world flooded. The Deep Mother is not a spider, but an oceanic horror of crushing pressure, bioluminescence, and parasitic control. She resides in the darkest trenches of the Sunless Sea, demanding the corruption of surface spirits and absolute matriarchal dominance. 
- **Symbol:** A black, jagged silhouette of a multi-tentacled leviathan clutching a pearl.

---

## 4. Mechanical Integration for Players

When creating a character in the Wayfinder setting, consider how your class connects to these cosmic forces:

### Clerics
- **Order / War / Forge:** You are likely an Inquisitor, War-Priest, or Armorer of the Crimson Dominion, enforcing the Iron Arbiter's will.
- **Tempest / Nature:** You are a shaman of the Spirit Keepers, drawing power from the ocean itself, violently opposed to industrialization.
- **Light / Twilight / Peace:** You are a beacon of hope for a Wayfinder crew, guiding them through the treacherous Jagged Reefs via the Guiding Star.
- **Death / Grave:** You are a rogue Drow priestess of the Deep Mother, or a bitter pirate seeking vengeance through the Drowned Avenger.

### Warlocks
- **The Fathomless:** Your patron is a specific, ancient Leviathan or a powerful, unbound ocean elemental. You are a conduit for the sea's wrath.
- **The Great Old One:** You touched a humming, glowing artifact pulled from the Unwritten Sea, and now the fractured, digital-like consciousness of a First Captain speaks to you in mathematical riddles.
- **The Hexblade:** You wield a weapon forged of black coral and abyssal iron, gifted to you by an exile of the Drowned Silk Houses, or pulled from a sunken wreck.

### Paladins
- **Oath of Conquest:** A perfect fit for the Crimson Dominion's Iron Legion, swearing to bring law to the chaotic seas.
- **Oath of the Open Sea (or Devotion):** A Wayfinder or Free Captain who has sworn to protect the freedom of the Charted Waters against the tyranny of the empires.
- **Oath of the Ancients:** A heavily armored warden of the Spirit Keepers, sworn to protect the Verdant Expanse and the slumbering Leviathans from the taint of Deep Magic.

### Sorcerers
- **Storm Sorcery:** You were born during a magical super-cell, blessed (or cursed) by the Laughing Storm. You are highly prized by the Free Captains.
- **Aberrant Mind:** You were exposed to the deep-sea radiation of the Unwritten Sea, or survived a parasitic encounter with a minion of the Deep Mother.

### Barbarians
- **Path of the Storm Herald:** You are a warrior of the Spirit Keepers, channeling the raw elemental power of the Aether Streams.
- **Path of the Zealot:** You are an unkillable shock-trooper for the Crimson Dominion, resurrected repeatedly by the priests of the Iron Arbiter to continue fighting.
# Chapter 26: Custom Player Options

The Shattered Expanse is a world defined by the sea, ancient magic, and cutthroat factions. The options presented in this chapter are designed for the **Pathfinder 1st Edition** ruleset, providing an unparalleled depth of customization to root your character in the Charted Waters. 

---

## 1. Custom Races

The standard races exist in the Shattered Expanse, but the unique environment has given rise to new peoples.

### The Deep-Touched
Humans, elves, and half-orcs who spent generations in the abyssal trenches have been mutated by the crushing pressure and the proximity to Slumbering Leviathans. They have pale, slightly translucent skin and large, ink-black eyes.

- **+2 Constitution, +2 Wisdom, -2 Charisma:** The Deep-Touched are incredibly resilient and highly attuned to their environment, but their alien appearance and unsettling stillness put others on edge.
- **Medium or Small:** Deep-Touched retain the size of their original parent race.
- **Normal Speed:** Deep-Touched have a base speed of 30 feet (or 20 feet if Small).
- **Swim Speed:** Deep-Touched have a swim speed of 30 feet and gain a +8 racial bonus on Swim checks.
- **Amphibious:** Deep-Touched can breathe both water and air.
- **Deep-Sight:** Deep-Touched have darkvision out to 60 feet. When underwater, this darkvision extends to 120 feet.
- **Pressure Adaptation:** Deep-Touched suffer no penalties from high pressure or extreme depths and gain cold resistance 5.
- **Languages:** Common and Aquan.

### The Aether-Forged
When the First Captains fell, their advanced aether-tech constructs lay dormant for millennia. Recently, some of these constructs have re-activated, possessing fragmented memories and sentience. They are forged of brass, glass, and glowing green aether-coils.

- **+2 Intelligence, +2 Constitution, -2 Dexterity:** Aether-Forged possess vast databanks and durable chassis, but their ancient joints are stiff and clunky.
- **Medium:** Aether-Forged are Medium creatures.
- **Normal Speed:** Aether-Forged have a base speed of 30 feet.
- **Construct (Half-Construct):** Aether-Forged are humanoids with the half-construct subtype. They gain a +2 racial bonus on saving throws against disease, mind-affecting effects, poison, and exhaustion.
- **Aether-Battery:** Aether-Forged do not need to eat, breathe, or sleep, though they must enter a dormant state for 8 hours a day to recharge their aether-coils. They can be healed by *cure* spells as well as *make whole*.
- **Integrated Tool:** An Aether-Forged possesses a built-in tool (choose one artisan's tool or a thieves' tool) that grants a +2 racial bonus on related checks.
- **Languages:** Common and Aether-Code (a binary precursor language).

---

## 2. Campaign Traits

| Trait | Benefit |
| :--- | :--- |
| **Dominion Legionnaire** | +1 Intimidate (class skill), +1 CMD vs trip/bull rush. |
| **Consortium Smuggler** | +1 Sleight of Hand or Bluff (choose one as class skill). |
| **Silk House Exile** | +1 Survival (class skill), low-light vision. |
| **Spirit Keeper Warden** | +1 Knowledge (Nature) (class skill), predict weather. |
| **Independent Salvager** | +1 Disable Device or Know (Eng) (class skill), start with trinket. |

During character creation, a player may select one Campaign Trait to represent their background in the Shattered Expanse.

**Dominion Legionnaire**
You were a soldier for the Crimson Dominion, used to strict hierarchy and ironclad law.
- **Benefit:** You gain a +1 trait bonus on Intimidate checks, and Intimidate is always a class skill for you. Additionally, you gain a +1 trait bonus to your CMD against trip and bull rush attempts.

**Consortium Smuggler**
You grew up dealing in the black markets of the Grand Merchant Consortium.
- **Benefit:** You gain a +1 trait bonus on Sleight of Hand checks and Bluff checks. One of these skills (your choice) becomes a class skill for you.

**Silk House Exile**
You survived the terrifying Drowned Silk Houses, carrying the trauma of the deep dark.
- **Benefit:** You gain a +1 trait bonus on Survival checks in underground or aquatic environments, and Survival becomes a class skill for you. You also gain low-light vision (or darkvision +10 ft. if you already possess it).

**Spirit Keeper Warden**
You revere the Slumbering Leviathans and protect the Verdant Expanse.
- **Benefit:** You gain a +1 trait bonus on Knowledge (Nature) checks, and it becomes a class skill for you. You can also perfectly predict the weather in your current biome for the next 24 hours.

**Independent Salvager**
You dive into the treacherous Unwritten Sea to retrieve artifacts of the First Captains.
- **Benefit:** You gain a +1 trait bonus on Disable Device checks and Knowledge (Engineering) checks, and one of these becomes a class skill for you. You also start with one random mundane precursor trinket.

---

## 3. New Class Archetypes

| Class | Archetype / Option | Focus |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| Barbarian | Leviathan Totem | Deep-sea breathing, crushing pressure aura, size bonuses. |
| Bard | Shantyman | Group CMB buffs, massive Intimidate scaling. |
| Cleric | Deep Domain | Water manipulation, cold resistance, touch attacks. |
| Druid | Reef Warden | Coral armor wild shape, symbiotic grappling. |
| Fighter | Dominion Marine | Firearm proficiency, initiative bonuses, swift movement. |
| Gunslinger | Aether-Corsair | Boarding leaps, hull-breaking shots, aether-weapons. |
| Monk | Way of the Shifting Tide | Immediate-action trips, ranged water whips. |
| Paladin | Oath of the Horizon | Navigation buffs, radiant damage bonuses, survival auras. |
| Ranger | Leviathan Hunter | Harpoon mastery, massive bonuses against Large+ targets. |
| Rogue | Consortium Smuggler | Black market access, sleight of hand buffs, rerolls. |
| Rogue | Deep Diver | Swim speeds, darkvision, flanking on downward plunges. |
| Sorcerer | Aether-Touched | Force damage conversion, overclocked casting (Burn). |
| Oracle | Sunken King | Hard-light shields, construct animation, AI channeling. |
| Wizard | Cartography | Short-range teleportation, forced position swapping. |

### Barbarian: Leviathan Totem (Archetype)
Barbarians who channel the terrifying, crushing power of the ancient sea beasts that slumber beneath the waves.

- **Abyssal Lungs (Ex):** At 3rd level, while raging, the barbarian can breathe underwater and ignores the pressure of deep aquatic environments. This ability replaces Trap Sense +1.
- **Crushing Pressure (Su):** At 6th level, the barbarian's rage manifests as a field of heavy pressure. Enemies within 10 feet of the barbarian treat the area as difficult terrain. This ability replaces Trap Sense +2.
- **Leviathan's Mass (Ex):** At 9th level, while raging, the barbarian gains a +2 bonus to their CMB for grapple and bull rush checks, and counts as one size category larger for the purpose of determining carrying capacity and CMD. This ability replaces Trap Sense +3.
- **Tsunami Strike (Ex):** At 12th level, once per rage as a standard action, the barbarian can make a single melee attack that sends out a shockwave. All enemies in a 30-foot line must make a Fortitude save (DC 10 + 1/2 barbarian level + Str modifier) or be knocked prone and take damage equal to the weapon's base damage. This ability replaces Trap Sense +4.

### Bard: Shantyman (Archetype)
Bards who use booming voices and percussive instruments to keep a crew working in perfect synchronization.

- **Heave Away (Su):** At 1st level, a Shantyman can use bardic performance to coordinate allies. Allies who can hear the Shantyman gain a +1 competence bonus on attack rolls and a +2 competence bonus on combat maneuver checks. This bonus increases by +1 at 5th, 11th, and 17th levels. This replaces Inspire Courage.
- **Voice of the Captain (Ex):** At 2nd level, a Shantymanâ€™s voice cuts through hurricanes. The bard gains a bonus equal to half their class level on Intimidate checks and can be heard clearly up to half a mile away. This replaces Versatile Performance.
- **Rhythm of the Sea (Su):** At 8th level, the Shantyman can use a swift action to expend 2 rounds of bardic performance. For 1 minute, all allies within 30 feet ignore movement penalties from difficult terrain or slippery decks, and gain a +4 morale bonus on saving throws against fear and charm effects. This replaces Dirge of Doom.

### Cleric: Deep Domain
Clerics who worship the Deep Mother or the ocean itself wield the terrifying pressure of the abyss.

- **Granted Powers:** You can manipulate water and crush your foes.
  - *Crushing Touch (Sp):* As a standard action, you can make a melee touch attack against a foe. On a hit, the target takes 1d6 points of nonlethal bludgeoning damage + 1 for every two cleric levels you possess, and its movement speed is reduced by 10 feet for 1 round. You can use this ability a number of times per day equal to 3 + your Wisdom modifier.
  - *Abyssal Resilience (Ex):* At 8th level, you gain a swim speed equal to your base land speed and can breathe underwater indefinitely. You gain cold resistance 10.
- **Domain Spells:** 1stâ€”*hydraulic push*, 2ndâ€”*slipstream*, 3rdâ€”*water breathing*, 4thâ€”*control water*, 5thâ€”*geyser*, 6thâ€”*fluid form*, 7thâ€”*vortex*, 8thâ€”*seamantle*, 9thâ€”*tsunami*.

### Druid: Reef Warden (Archetype)
Fierce protectors of the Shattered Expanse's fragile underwater ecosystems.

- **Reef Bond (Ex):** A Reef Warden must choose a domain (such as Water or Weather) instead of an animal companion. 
- **Living Coral Armor (Su):** At 4th level, as a standard action, a Reef Warden can expend one daily use of Wild Shape to grow a jagged carapace of coral. The druid gains a +4 natural armor bonus and temporary hit points equal to their druid level. Any creature that strikes the druid with a non-reach melee weapon takes 1d4 piercing damage. This effect lasts for 10 minutes per druid level. This ability alters Wild Shape.
- **Symbiotic Grapple (Su):** At 9th level, when the druid casts a spell that entangles or restricts movement (like *entangle*), they can attempt a grapple combat maneuver check against one creature in the area, using their caster level + their Wisdom modifier as their CMB. If successful, the creature is grappled by spectral kelp vines. This ability replaces Venom Immunity.

### Fighter: Dominion Marine (Archetype)
The Crimson Dominion relies on disciplined, heavily armored shock-troops.

- **Aether-Tech Training (Ex):** At 1st level, the Marine gains Exotic Weapon Proficiency (Firearms) as a bonus feat. They suffer no penalties for firing a two-handed firearm while prone. This replaces the 1st-level fighter bonus feat.
- **Breach and Clear (Ex):** At 3rd level, the Marine gains a +2 bonus on initiative checks and a +2 bonus on damage rolls during the surprise round or the first round of combat against targets that are flat-footed. This replaces Armor Training 1.
- **Ironclad Advance (Ex):** At 5th level, the Marine gains a +1 dodge bonus to AC when wearing heavy armor, and a +1 bonus to CMD. These bonuses increase by +1 for every four levels beyond 5th. This replaces Weapon Training 1.
- **Relentless Assault (Ex):** At 9th level, as a swift action, the Marine can move up to half their speed. This movement does not provoke attacks of opportunity if the Marine ends their movement adjacent to a hostile creature. They can use this ability a number of times per day equal to 3 + their Constitution modifier. This replaces Weapon Training 2.

### Gunslinger: Aether-Corsair (Archetype)
The Shattered Expanse is fraught with danger, and the Aether-Corsair uses volatile precursor technology and flawless agility to dominate ship-to-ship boarding actions.

- **Aether-Gunsmith (Ex):** At 1st level, the Aether-Corsair gains a battered aether-pistol or aether-rifle (which targets touch AC in its first range increment but misfires on a 1-2). They gain the Gunsmithing feat, but their craft relies on precursor scrap rather than black powder. This alters Gunsmithing.
- **Corsair's Deeds:** An Aether-Corsair swaps out several standard deeds for those suited to the high seas.
  - *Boarding Leap (Ex):* At 1st level, as long as the corsair has at least 1 grit point, they gain a +4 bonus on Acrobatics checks made to jump or swing on ropes. By spending 1 grit point as a swift action, they can move up to 15 feet ignoring difficult terrain and without provoking attacks of opportunity. This replaces the *Deadeye* deed.
  - *Hull-Breaker (Ex):* At 3rd level, the corsair can spend 1 grit point when making a ranged attack against an object, vehicle, or ship. The attack ignores up to 10 points of the target's hardness and deals double damage on a hit. This replaces the *Pistol-Whip* deed.
  - *Deep-Sea Shot (Su):* At 7th level, as long as the corsair has at least 1 grit point, their firearm attacks are not penalized for being fired underwater, and the range increment is not reduced by the water. This replaces the *Dead Shot* deed.
- **Nimble Rigging (Ex):** At 2nd level, an Aether-Corsair gains a +1 dodge bonus to AC while wearing light or no armor. This bonus increases by +1 for every four levels beyond 2nd. Additionally, they gain a climb speed equal to half their base speed when climbing rigging, ropes, or ship hulls. This alters Nimble.

### Monk: Way of the Shifting Tide (Archetype)
Martial artists who fight like the sea itself: calm one moment, a devastating wave the next.

- **Sea Legs (Ex):** At 1st level, the monk uses their Wisdom modifier instead of Acrobatics to keep their balance on a moving ship. They can stand up from prone as a swift action without provoking attacks of opportunity. This replaces Stunning Fist.
- **Tidal Redirect (Ex):** At 3rd level, when an enemy misses the monk with a melee attack, the monk can spend 1 ki point as an immediate action to attempt a reposition or trip combat maneuver against the attacker with a +2 bonus. This replaces Still Mind.
- **Water Whip (Su):** At 7th level, as a standard action, the monk can spend 2 ki points to strike an enemy within 30 feet with a whip of magical water. The monk makes a ranged touch attack. On a hit, the target takes bludgeoning damage equal to the monk's unarmed strike damage + their Wisdom modifier, and the monk can attempt a free pull combat maneuver. This replaces Wholeness of Body.

### Paladin: Oath of the Horizon (Oath)
Driven by a holy compulsion to map the Unwritten Sea and protect their crew from abyssal horrors.

- **Tenets:** Discover the unknown, guide your crew, and adapt to the sea.
- **Oath Spells:** 1stâ€”*know direction (as a 1st-level spell)*, 2ndâ€”*status*, 3rdâ€”*water walk*, 4thâ€”*freedom of movement*.
- **Guiding Light (Su):** At 4th level, as a standard action, the paladin can expend one use of Channel Positive Energy to summon a flare of divine light. For 1 minute, the paladin sheds bright light in a 30-foot radius. Allies within this light gain a sacred bonus on attack rolls equal to the paladin's Charisma modifier (minimum +1). This replaces the standard Channel Positive Energy feature.
- **Aura of the Navigator (Su):** At 8th level, the paladin and allies within 10 feet gain a +4 bonus on Survival checks to avoid becoming lost, and ignore penalties from natural difficult terrain (like deep snow or mud). This replaces Aura of Resolve.

### Ranger: Leviathan Hunter (Archetype)
Fearless sailors who wield massive harpoons to butcher colossal beasts.

- **Harpoon Mastery (Ex):** At 1st level, the ranger treats the harpoon as a martial weapon. When the ranger hits a creature with a thrown harpoon attached to a trailing rope, they can attempt a trip combat maneuver as a free action that does not provoke attacks of opportunity. This replaces Wild Empathy.
- **Colossus Slayer (Ex):** At 2nd level, a Leviathan Hunter gains a +1 bonus on attack and damage rolls against creatures of Large size or larger. This bonus increases by +1 at 6th, 10th, 14th, and 18th levels. This replaces Combat Style Feat at 2nd level (the ranger still gains combat style feats at later levels).
- **Iron Nerve (Ex):** At 3rd level, the ranger gains a +4 bonus on saving throws against fear effects. This replaces Endurance.
- **Whaler's Evasion (Ex):** At 11th level, when targeted by a melee attack from a creature Large or larger, the ranger can spend an immediate action to gain a dodge bonus to AC equal to their Wisdom modifier against that attack. This replaces Quarry.

### Rogue: Consortium Smuggler (Archetype)
Masters of the black market, moving illicit goods and avoiding harbor inspections.

- **Black Market Contacts (Ex):** At 1st level, the smuggler adds half their rogue level (minimum 1) to Appraise and Knowledge (Local) checks related to illicit goods. They can find a fence in any settlement within 1d4 hours. This replaces Trapfinding.
- **Hidden Pockets (Ex):** At 2nd level, the smuggler gains a +4 bonus on Sleight of Hand checks made to conceal small items on their person. This replaces the rogue talent gained at 2nd level.
- **Silver Tongue (Ex):** At 3rd level, the smuggler can reroll a failed Bluff or Diplomacy check once per day, but must take the second result. This replaces Trap Sense +1.

### Rogue: Deep Diver (Archetype)
Specialized in aquatic infiltration, striking from the darkest waters.

- **Aquatic Infiltrator (Ex):** At 1st level, the rogue gains a swim speed equal to half their base land speed. They can hold their breath for a number of rounds equal to four times their Constitution score before risking drowning. This replaces Trapfinding.
- **Plunge Strike (Ex):** At 4th level, if the rogue falls or swims at least 10 feet directly toward a target immediately before making a melee attack, they are treated as flanking the target for the purpose of dealing Sneak Attack damage. This replaces the rogue talent gained at 4th level.
- **Abyssal Ambush (Ex):** At 8th level, the rogue gains darkvision out to 60 feet. While underwater, the rogue can use the Stealth skill to hide even while being observed, as long as they are in dim light or darkness. This replaces the rogue talent gained at 8th level.

### Sorcerer: Aether-Touched (Bloodline)
A living battery of raw, unstable precursor energy.

- **Class Skill:** Knowledge (Engineering).
- **Bonus Spells:** *magic missile* (3rd), *levitate* (5th), *lightning bolt* (7th), *resilient sphere* (9th), *telekinesis* (11th), *disintegrate* (13th), *reverse gravity* (15th), *telekinetic sphere* (17th), *meteor swarm* (19th).
- **Bonus Feats:** Combat Casting, Empower Spell, Point-Blank Shot, Precise Shot, Spell Focus (Evocation), Toughness.
- **Bloodline Arcana:** Whenever you cast a spell that deals damage, you can choose to change the damage type to force damage. If you do, the spell gains the chaotic descriptor, and you take 1 point of nonlethal damage per spell level.
- **Bloodline Powers:** 
  - *Aetheric Blast (Sp):* At 1st level, as a standard action, you can unleash a ray of green force targeting a foe within 30 feet as a ranged touch attack. It deals 1d6 points of force damage + 1 for every two sorcerer levels you possess. You can use this ability a number of times per day equal to 3 + your Charisma modifier.
  - *Aetheric Resonance (Su):* At 3rd level, you gain a constant feather fall effect, and ignore difficult terrain caused by rubble or uneven stone.
  - *Overclocked Spells (Su):* At 9th level, you can increase the caster level of a spell you cast by +2. If you do, you must succeed on a Fortitude save (DC 15 + spell level) or become staggered for 1 round.

### Oracle: Sunken King (Mystery)
Your powers are drawn from a dormant AI or digitized ancient admiral at the bottom of the abyss.

- **Class Skills:** Knowledge (Engineering), Knowledge (History).
- **Bonus Spells:** *shield* (2nd), *shatter* (4th), *lightning bolt* (6th), *secure shelter* (8th), *wall of force* (10th), *chain lightning* (12th), *control construct* (14th), *iron body* (16th), *meteor swarm* (18th).
- **Revelations:**
  - *Aegis Shielding (Su):* As a swift action, you can conjure a hard-light shield, granting you a +4 armor bonus to AC. At 7th level, and every four levels thereafter, this bonus increases by +2. The shield lasts for 1 hour per oracle level or until dismissed.
  - *Technomantic Thrall (Sp):* You can cast *make whole* at will. At 7th level, you can animate a medium-sized pile of scrap metal as an *animate objects* spell, but it functions as a permanent construct thrall under your control (similar to a familiar) until destroyed.
  - *Override Protocol (Su):* You gain a +4 insight bonus on saving throws against mind-affecting effects. At 11th level, you become immune to charm effects.

### Wizard: Cartography (Arcane School)
Wizards who manipulate the geometry of space, bending distance and location.

- **Prohibited Schools:** You must select two opposed schools (often Necromancy and Enchantment).
- **Cartographer's Eye (Ex):** You gain a bonus equal to half your wizard level on Knowledge (Geography) and Survival checks to navigate. You always know which way is north.
- **Spatial Shift (Su):** As a swift action, you can magically teleport up to 10 feet to an unoccupied space you can see. You can use this ability a number of times per day equal to 3 + your Intelligence modifier. This movement does not provoke attacks of opportunity.
- **Rewrite Geometry (Su):** At 8th level, as a standard action, you can force a creature within 30 feet to swap positions with you. An unwilling creature can make a Will save (DC 10 + 1/2 wizard level + Int modifier) to negate the effect. You can use this ability a number of times per day equal to your Intelligence modifier.

---

## 4. Faction Prestige Classes

These 5-level prestige classes represent the absolute elite of the Shattered Expanse's major powers. 

**Prerequisite:** A character *must* have a Renown score of 20 or higher with the associated faction to take levels in these classes.

### Dominion Justiciar (Crimson Dominion)
The terrifying, heavily armored law-bringers of the Crimson Dominion, tasked with crushing rebellion and executing pirates.
- **Prerequisites:** Renown 20 (Crimson Dominion), Base Attack Bonus +5, Intimidate 5 ranks.
- **Hit Die:** d10.
- **Iron Dictate (Su):** At 1st level, as a swift action, the Justiciar can designate one enemy within 30 feet as a Lawbreaker. The Justiciar gains a +2 sacred bonus on attack and damage rolls against the Lawbreaker. If the Lawbreaker moves more than 10 feet in a single round, they take 2d6 points of sonic damage. 
- **Unstoppable Advance (Ex):** At 3rd level, the Justiciar is immune to difficult terrain and cannot be staggered or entangled.
- **Executioner's Strike (Ex):** At 5th level, if the Justiciar strikes a Lawbreaker with a melee attack and reduces them to 0 or fewer hit points, the Justiciar immediately regains 2d10 hit points and can make an Intimidate check to demoralize all enemies within 30 feet as a free action.

### Silk House Weaver (Drowned Silk Houses)
Assassins and infiltrators who weave the abyssal silk of the deep spiders into deadly traps and garrotes.
- **Prerequisites:** Renown 20 (Drowned Silk Houses), Sneak Attack +2d6, Stealth 5 ranks.
- **Hit Die:** d8.
- **Abyssal Web (Sp):** At 1st level, the Weaver can cast *web* as a spell-like ability three times per day. The webs are pitch black and block line of sight.
- **Silk Garrote (Ex):** At 3rd level, if the Weaver successfully grapples a target from stealth, they can apply their Sneak Attack damage as precision damage every round they maintain the grapple.
- **Shadow Step (Su):** At 5th level, as a move action, the Weaver can step into any area of dim light or darkness and teleport up to 60 feet to another area of dim light or darkness. 

### Smuggler-Baron (Grand Merchant Consortium)
Masterminds who control the battlefield through wealth, bribery, and illicit aether-tech.
- **Prerequisites:** Renown 20 (Grand Merchant Consortium), Bluff 5 ranks, Appraise 5 ranks.
- **Hit Die:** d8.
- **Combat Bribery (Ex):** At 1st level, as a standard action, the Smuggler-Baron can throw a handful of valuable coins or precursor scrap at an enemy. The enemy must make a Will save (DC 10 + Baron level + Cha modifier) or be fascinated for 1 round as they instinctively scramble for the wealth.
- **Aether-Tech Arsenal (Ex):** At 3rd level, the Smuggler-Baron can draw any mundane or aether-tech item worth less than 500 gp from their coat as a swift action, as if they had always prepared for this exact moment. They can do this three times per day.
- **Market Manipulation (Su):** At 5th level, once per day, the Smuggler-Baron can automatically critically hit with an attack, or force an enemy to automatically critically miss with an attack, as they manipulate the probabilistic aether-fields of luck and commerce.

---

## 5. The Aether-Burn Mechanic

Precursor technology is unstable, and the magic of the Shattered Expanse is wild. **Aether-Burn** is a universal, high-risk/high-reward mechanic available to all characters.

### Accepting Burn
As a free action on their turn, any character can choose to accept 1 or more points of **Aether-Burn**. 
- Each point of Aether-Burn inflicts 1 point of nonlethal damage per character level.
- **This damage cannot be healed by any means** (mundane or magical) until the character completes a full 8-hour rest, which clears all Burn.
- If a character takes nonlethal damage exceeding their current hit points, they fall unconscious.

### Spending Burn
By accepting Burn, characters can instantly access overwhelming power:
- **Overclock Weapon (1 Burn):** Maximize the damage dice of one weapon attack this turn.
- **Aetheric Surge (2 Burn):** Cast any prepared spell or spell known with a casting time of 1 standard action as a swift action instead.
- **Bend Probability (3 Burn):** Reroll a single failed d20 roll (attack, save, or skill check). You must take the second result.

### Aether-Sickness
If a character accumulates a number of Burn points equal to or greater than their Constitution modifier (minimum 1), they suffer **Aether-Sickness**. They take a -2 penalty to all saving throws and ability checks until their Burn is cleared, as their veins glow violently green and their skin cracks.

---
