The ruins of the First Captains litter the Unwritten Sea, and salvage crews have learned to adapt their weapons. Aether-tech weapons are treated as Firearms for the purpose of proficiencies and feats.

*Note: Aether-weapons target Touch AC within their first range increment, but deal half Piercing / half Force damage. They run on rechargeable Aether-batteries rather than black powder.*

*Note: The Deep-Diving Suit provides a constant supply of air for up to 8 hours and grants immunity to pressure damage.*

## 7. Symbiotic Ship Relics

Ship Relics are a new category of magic items. They are massive, magical components bolted onto a ship, but they require a mortal soul to function. 
- A Symbiotic Ship Relic takes up one of a specific character's Magic Item Slots (e.g., Shoulders, Ring, Neck).
- The attuned character gains a small personal buff, but the **Ship** gains a massive supernatural ability.

**The Leviathan Figurehead (Slot: Head/Headband)**
- **Personal Buff:** The attuned character gains a +2 competence bonus on Intimidate checks.
- **Ship Buff:** When the ship rams another vessel, it deals an additional 4d10 bludgeoning damage, and the enemy vessel must make a Fortitude save or suffer a hull breach.

**Aetheric Wind-Engines (Slot: Shoulders)**
- **Personal Buff:** The attuned character gains a +10 ft. enhancement bonus to their base movement speed.
- **Ship Buff:** As a standard action, the attuned character can command the ship to magically teleport up to 500 feet in any direction across the water. This can be used once per day.

**The Drowned Lantern (Slot: Ring)**
- **Personal Buff:** The attuned character gains darkvision 60 ft.
- **Ship Buff:** The ship is surrounded by a 100-foot aura of magical darkness that only the crew can see through, granting the ship total concealment at night.

---

## 8. Setting-Specific Feats

| Feat | Prerequisites | Benefit |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Sea Legs** | Dex 13 | +2 Acrobatics/Climb, ignore ship movement penalties. |
| **Aether-Scarred** | Con 13 | Resist 5 Force/Lightning, +2 saves vs chaotic spells. |
| **Ship-Breaker** | Str 15, Power Attack, BAB +3 | Melee attacks ignore 5 points of object hardness. |
| **Abyssal Caster** | Cast 2nd level spells, Spell Focus (Evocation) | +1 DC and +2 SR checks on Cold/Bludgeoning evocation spells. |

**Sea Legs (Combat)**
You have spent so much time on unsteady decks that rolling waves feel natural.
- **Prerequisites:** Dex 13.
- **Benefit:** You gain a +2 bonus on Acrobatics and Climb checks. You do not suffer penalties on attack rolls or skill checks due to fighting on a moving ship or uneven ground.

**Aether-Scarred**
You survived exposure to chaotic, magical radiation.
- **Prerequisites:** Con 13.
- **Benefit:** You gain resistance 5 to force damage and lightning damage. Additionally, you gain a +2 bonus on saving throws against chaotic-aligned spells or effects.

**Ship-Breaker (Combat)**
You have trained to breach enemy hulls and destroy fortifications.
- **Prerequisites:** Str 15, Power Attack, base attack bonus +3.
- **Benefit:** When you make a melee attack against an object or a vehicle, you ignore up to 5 points of its hardness.

**Abyssal Caster**
You weave the freezing, crushing power of the deep ocean into your magic.
- **Prerequisites:** Ability to cast 2nd-level spells, Spell Focus (Evocation).
- **Benefit:** Whenever you cast an evocation spell that deals cold or bludgeoning damage, the DC to resist the spell increases by +1, and you gain a +2 bonus on caster level checks made to overcome spell resistance.

Creatures in the area take 1d6 points of force damage per caster level (maximum 5d6) and are pushed 5 feet away from you for every 10 points of damage taken. You manipulate the atmospheric pressure in the area, simulating the abyssal depths. The area becomes difficult terrain. Any creature entering the area or starting its turn there takes 4d6 points of bludgeoning damage and is deafened for 1 minute. A successful Fortitude save halves the damage and negates the deafened condition. Spellcasting in the area requires a concentration check (DC 15 + spell level).
# Chapter 27: Naval Combat & Ship Mechanics

The Shattered Expanse is a world of oceans, storms, and piracy. While standard combat excels at close-quarters skirmishes, simulating ship-to-ship combat requires a system that makes the vessel feel like a customizable fortress, while keeping the playersâ€”the officersâ€”at the center of the action.

The system presented here is both **cinematic and deeply modular**. Rather than treating ships as static tokens, this system allows players to capture, retrofit, and build highly customized vessels using Weapon Slots and Upgrade Slots.

---

## 1. Ship Chassis & Size Categories

Every vessel in the Expanse is built upon a fundamental chassis, defined by its **Size Category**. A ship's size dictates its durability, speed, and how many modular upgrades and weapons it can support.

| Size Category | Description | Examples | Base HP | Base AC | Base Speed | Weapon Slots | Upgrade Slots | Minimum Crew |
| :---: | :--- | :--- | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: |
| **1** | Light / Skiff | Salvage Skiff, Dinghy | 50 | 12 | 50 ft. | 1 | 2 | 1-2 |
| **2** | Sloop / Escort | Smuggler's Sloop, Corvette | 150 | 15 | 60 ft. | 4 | 8 | 10 |
| **3** | Cutter / Brig | Heavy Cutter, Corsair Brig | 300 | 16 | 45 ft. | 12 | 15 | 40 |
| **4** | Galleon / Heavy Cargo | Merchant Galleon, Carrack | 600 | 14 | 30 ft. | 20 | 25 | 100 |
| **5** | Capital Vessel / Dreadnought | Experimental Ironclad, Flagship | 1,200 | 18 | 20 ft. | 40 | 50 | 300+ |
| **6** | Leviathan / Floating City | Sovereign Braid, Mobile Pirate Haven | 5,000 | 20 | 10 ft. | 100 | 150 | 5,000+ Population |

*Note: Base HP and AC represent a standard wooden hull. Precursor Plating or Magical Wards will modify these numbers.*

---

## 2. Weapon Slots & Naval Artillery

A ship's **Weapon Slots** represent the hardpoints available for mounting cannons, ballistas, or magical siege engines. Weapons take up a specific number of slots based on their size and power. When a Gunner takes the "Fire Artillery" Ship Action, they command a battery of installed weapons to fire simultaneously.

### Available Naval Artillery

| Weapon Module | Slots Required | Cost | Attack | Damage & Effect |
| :--- | :---: | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Light Swivel Gun** | 1 | 250 gp | Ranged Weapon (+5) | 2d8 Piercing. Range 100/400. Hits personnel as well as hulls. |
| **Standard Broadside Cannon** | 2 | 800 gp | Ranged Weapon (+6) | 3d10 Bludgeoning. Range 120/480. Standard ship-to-ship armament. |
| **Heavy Ballista** | 2 | 600 gp | Ranged Weapon (+6) | 4d8 Piercing. Range 150/600. Can be loaded with chain-shot to cripple rigging. |
| **Precursor Naval Cannon** | 4 | 2,500 gp | Ranged Weapon (+8) | 5d10 Bludgeoning + 1d10 Arcane. Range 200/800. Ignores standard wood hardness. |
| **Heavy Siege-Breaker Mortar** | 4 | 3,000 gp | Ranged AoE (DC 15 Reflex) | 6d10 Fire/Bludgeoning in a 40 ft radius. Range 400/1600. Long reload times (2 rounds). |
| **Experimental Aether-Beam** | 6 | Rare/Loot | Ranged Spell (+10) | 6d10 Force. Range 300/1200. Leaves lingering Aether-Burn on the target hull. |

---

## 3. Upgrade Slots & Ship Customization

A ship's **Upgrade Slots** represent the internal volume and structural hardpoints available for retrofits, magical wards, armor plating, and specialized facilities. 

### Common Upgrades

| Upgrade Module | Slots Required | Cost | Effect |
| :--- | :---: | :--- | :--- |
| **Smuggler's Compartment** | 2 | 1,000 gp | Adds a hidden cargo hold that requires a DC 20 Perception check to find. |
| **Diving Bell & Winch** | 3 | 1,500 gp | A pressurized sphere that can be lowered to the ocean floor, holding up to 4 Medium creatures. |
| **Captain's Extravagant Quarters** | 4 | 2,000 gp | Opulent furnishings. Grants the Captain a +2 circumstance bonus on Diplomacy and Intimidate checks while parleying in the cabin. |
| **Medical / Triage Bay** | 5 | 3,000 gp | Grants a +4 circumstance bonus on Heal checks. Crew natural healing rates double while aboard. |

### Heavy / Precursor Upgrades
*These upgrades often require specific salvaged materials, like Aether-Slag or intact Precursor Cores, and a Master Artificer to install.*

| Upgrade Module | Slots Required | Materials Needed | Effect |
| :--- | :---: | :--- | :--- |
| **Aether-Rammed Prow** | 5 | 50 Crates Aether-Slag | Adds +3d6 Force damage on successful naval ramming maneuvers. |
| **Masterwork Precursor Plating** | 10 | 50 Crates Aether-Slag | Replaces the hull with Precursor alloy. Grants +2 Hardness (Damage Reduction) and +10 to +50 HP depending on ship size. |
| **Violet Aether-Core Engine** | 15 | Pristine Violet Core | Ship no longer relies on wind. Grants +2 Speed (e.g. +20 ft), +1 Maneuverability, and a passive 20 HP Ward-Shield that regenerates daily. |
| **Sentential Drone Bay** | 4 | Salvaged Drone Core | Houses an aquatic drone that provides automated perimeter defense and underwater scouting. |

### City Districts (Size 6 Only)
*These massive installations turn a Dreadnought into a true Floating City. They consume vast amounts of space and require a permanent civilian population to operate.*

| District Module | Slots Required | Cost / Materials | Effect |
| :--- | :---: | :--- | :--- |
| **The Warrens (Slums)** | 10 | 10,000 gp | Basic civilian housing. Increases maximum population capacity by 1,000. Reduces city Morale if overcrowded. |
| **Merchant's Ward** | 15 | 25,000 gp | A sprawling bazaar. Generates a passive income of 1d4 x 1,000 gp per week, provided the ship visits populated waters to trade. |
| **Temple of the Four Energies** | 20 | Pristine Core + 50,000 gp | A monumental ziggurat channeling Arcane, Divine, Psionic, and Deep Magic. Grants massive city-wide wards (e.g. permanent *Control Weather* and *Forbiddance*). |
| **Precursor Forge District** | 20 | Automated Forge Module | A massive industrial zone. Allows the city to mint its own Precursor Naval Cannons and Aether-tech over time. |

---

## 4. Floating City Mechanics (Size 6)

When a vessel reaches Size 6 (Leviathan), it transcends standard naval combat. It operates on macro-scale mechanics:
1. **Population & Morale:** Instead of a Minimum Crew, the ship requires a **Population** to man the districts. The Captain must manage a **Morale Gauge** (1-100). If Morale drops below 30 (due to starvation, plague, or defeat in battle), riots break out in The Warrens, shutting down District modules.
2. **City Turns:** In ship-to-ship combat, a Size 6 vessel is too massive to maneuver like a sloop. It operates on "City Turns," acting at Initiative Count 20 and 10 to unleash devastating, synchronized broadsides across multiple batteries at once.
3. **Internal Ecosystem:** A Floating City must consume massive resources. Feeding 5,000 citizens requires specialized Upgrades (e.g., Hydroponic Gardens, Colossal Fishing Nets) or draining the Pirate Economy treasury weekly.
4. **Flotilla Mooring:** A Floating City does not expand by simply building higher wooden towers; it expands by consuming other ships. Allied or captured vessels (Sizes 1-4) can permanently moor themselves to the hull of a Size 6 vessel, physically lashing themselves to the main structure. Each permanently moored vessel adds its internal volume to the Floating City, allowing the Captain to install new City Districts inside the lashed hulls of the flotilla.

---

## 5. Officer Roles & Combat Flow

Naval combat happens in standard initiative. The ship itself does not roll initiative; instead, it moves and acts on the turn of the **Helmsman**. 

During combat, players take on specific **Officer Roles**. An officer can use their Standard Action to take a typical action (like casting a spell or making an attack), OR they can use their Standard Action to take a special **Ship Action** associated with their role.

### The Captain
The leader of the vessel, responsible for morale and coordination.
- **Standard Action - "Fire at Will!":** You grant a +2 bonus to all artillery attack rolls made by your ship until the start of your next turn.
- **Standard Action - "Brace for Impact!":** You shout a warning. Until the start of your next turn, the ship gains Damage Reduction (DR) 5/- against ship weapons or environmental hazards, and the crew gains a +4 bonus on saving throws to avoid being knocked prone.

### The Helmsman (Tide-Caller)
The one steering the ship. The ship moves on the Helmsman's turn.
- **Move Action - Move:** You use the ship's speed to move it across the battlefield.
- **Standard Action - "Hard to Port/Starboard!":** You make a DC 15 Profession (Sailor) check. On a success, the ship can immediately turn up to 90 degrees and move up to half its speed, avoiding an incoming hazard or lining up a perfect broadside.
- **Standard Action - Ramming Speed:** You move the ship in a straight line toward a target. If you hit, make an opposed Profession (Sailor) check or a melee attack. On a hit, both ships take 6d10 bludgeoning damage (modified by Aether-Rammed Prows or Precursor Plating).

### The Gunner
The master of the ship's artillery.
- **Standard Action - Fire Artillery:** You direct the crew to fire a battery of installed Weapon Modules. Make an attack roll for the battery. You can add your Base Attack Bonus and your Intelligence or Dexterity modifier to the attack roll instead of the ship's base modifier. (Having a Master Cannoneer officer grants a flat +2 to these rolls).
- **Standard Action - Targeted Shot:** You aim for a specific part of the enemy ship (e.g., the mast, the rudder). Make an artillery attack with a -4 penalty. If it hits, you cripple that component. (A crippled mast halves speed; a crippled rudder prevents turning).

### The Bosun (Artificer / Engineer)
The chief engineer and disciplinarian, keeping the ship and crew functioning.
- **Standard Action - Emergency Repairs:** You direct the damage control crew. Make a DC 15 Craft (ships) or Knowledge (engineering) check. On a success, the ship regains 4d10 Hull Points or a crippled system is temporarily bypassed.
- **Standard Action - Overclock Engine:** If the ship has an Aether-Core, you push the engines beyond their limits. The ship's speed increases by 30 feet until the end of the round, but the engine requires an Aether-Burn check or takes 1d10 damage from the strain.

---

## 6. Wayfinder Ship Bond Synergy

Wayfinder Corsairs possess the **Ship Bond** class feature. When a Wayfinder acts as the Captain of a vessel, the ship transcends its mechanical limitations:
1. **Magical Evasion:** The ship can substitute the Wayfinder's AC bonuses or saving throws for its own when subjected to magical or broadside attacks.
2. **Support Crew Multiplier:** A Wayfinder's crew acts with supernatural coordination. Even massive Capital Vessels (Size 5) can be operated effectively by a skeleton crew of magically bound sailors without suffering the standard penalties for being undercrewed.
# Chapter 28: Faction Ship Statblocks

This chapter provides GM-ready statblocks for enemy vessels encountered in the Shattered Expanse. Rather than generic statblocks, each faction uses a unique "Chassis Trait" that fundamentally alters how their ships perform in combat. 

*Note: The Weapon Slots listed represent the standard armament for these NPC vessels. DMs can customize them by swapping artillery from Chapter 27.*

---

## 1. The Crimson Dominion (The Iron Juggernauts)

**Faction Chassis Trait: Aether-Iron Plating.** Dominion ships have +2 Base AC and possess Hardness 5 (Damage Reduction 5 against all non-magical, non-siege damage). However, their base Speed is reduced by 10 ft, and they require a DC 20 Profession (Sailor) check to turn 90 degrees.
**Special Ability: Arcane Ramming.** When moving in a straight line, their Aether-Rammed Prows deal maximum structural damage, crushing smaller ships and halving any ramming damage they would normally take themselves.

*The workhorse patrol vessel, heavily armored but slow.*
- **HP:** 150 | **AC:** 17 | **Speed:** 50 ft. | **Hardness:** 5
- **Standard Armament (4 Slots):**
  - *2x Standard Broadside Cannons (2 slots each):* Ranged Weapon (+6), 3d10 Bludgeoning. Range 120/480.
- **Standard Action:** *Imperial Discipline.* Once per round, the Captain can grant a reroll to any failed attack roll or Profession (Sailor) check.

### Dominion Magi-Cruiser (Size 3)
*A line-breaker designed to deliver devastating magical payloads.*
- **HP:** 300 | **AC:** 18 | **Speed:** 35 ft. | **Hardness:** 5
- **Standard Armament (12 Slots):**
  - *4x Standard Broadside Cannons (8 slots):* Ranged Weapon (+6), 3d10 Bludgeoning. Range 120/480.
  - *1x Precursor Naval Cannon (4 slots):* Ranged Weapon (+8), 5d10 Bludgeoning + 1d10 Arcane. Range 200/800.
- **Standard Action:** *Aether Surge.* The Bosun can overcharge the weapons, causing the next broadside to deal an additional 2d10 Arcane damage.

### Dominion Dreadnought (Size 4)
*A floating fortress. Terrifyingly durable, but incredibly cumbersome.*
- **HP:** 600 | **AC:** 16 | **Speed:** 20 ft. | **Hardness:** 5
- **Standard Armament (20 Slots):**
  - *6x Standard Broadside Cannons (12 slots):* Ranged Weapon (+6), 3d10 Bludgeoning.
  - *2x Precursor Naval Cannons (8 slots):* Ranged Weapon (+8), 5d10 Bludgeoning + 1d10 Arcane.
- **Standard Action:** *Unbreakable Line.* The Captain can grant the ship DR 10/- against all damage until the start of their next turn.

**Faction Chassis Trait: Abjuration Shield Emitters.** Consortium ships have -2 Base AC. However, they gain a pool of Temporary HP equal to half their maximum HP (The Abjuration Shield). This shield regenerates 20 HP at the start of the ship's turn.
**Special Ability: Alchemical Smokescreen.** As a reaction when targeted by a ranged attack, the ship can deploy an obscuring cloud to force disadvantage on all incoming artillery for the rest of the round. The ship may immediately move up to half its speed without provoking opportunity attacks.
**Innate Upgrade: Smuggler's Hold.** All Consortium ships come equipped with hidden, lead-lined cargo compartments designed to evade inspections and divination magic (DC 20 Perception check to find). On Size 3 or larger vessels, these holds are enchanted as Extra-Dimensional spaces (functioning like massive *Bags of Holding*), allowing them to transport an impossible amount of cargo without affecting the ship's buoyancy or speed.

### Consortium Audit-Frigate (Size 2)
*Fast, shielded escorts designed to stall enemies while cargo escapes.*
- **HP:** 150 (Base) + 75 (Shield) | **AC:** 13 | **Speed:** 60 ft.
- **Standard Armament (4 Slots):**

- **Standard Action:** *Divert Power.* The Bosun can redirect engine power to the shields, regenerating an immediate 50 Shield HP, but reducing the ship's speed to 0 until the next turn.

### Consortium Vault-Ship (Size 4)
*An unsinkable, slow-moving bank vault transporting immense wealth.*
- **HP:** 600 (Base) + 300 (Shield) | **AC:** 12 | **Speed:** 30 ft.
- **Standard Armament (20 Slots):**
  - *8x Standard Broadside Cannons (16 slots):* Ranged Weapon (+6), 3d10 Bludgeoning.
  - *1x Heavy Siege-Breaker Mortar (4 slots):* Ranged AoE (DC 15 Reflex), 6d10 Fire/Bludgeoning in a 40 ft radius.
- **Standard Action:** *Total Lockdown.* The ship sacrifices all attacks and movement for the round to double its Shield regeneration (40 HP) and grant a +4 circumstance bonus on all saving throws against boarding actions.

**Faction Chassis Trait: Undead Leviathan Hull.** Drow ships have -2 Base AC. However, they regenerate 10% of their maximum HP at the start of every round. They are immune to Fire damage and cannot be set on fire.
**Special Ability: Abyssal Breach.** Drow ships can submerge beneath the surface. While submerged, they cannot be targeted by conventional naval artillery. They can breach the surface anywhere within their movement range. Their Sonic Projectors bypass Abjuration Shields and Damage Reduction entirely.

- **Standard Action:** *Silent Running.* The ship gains a +4 circumstance bonus on Stealth checks to remain undetected while submerged.

### Drow Arachna-Submersible (Size 3)
*A terrifying hunter made of enchanted chitin.*
- **HP:** 300 | **AC:** 14 | **Speed:** 45 ft. | **Regeneration:** 30 HP/round
- **Standard Armament (12 Slots):**
  - *2x Sonic Projectors (8 slots):* Ranged Weapon (+8), 4d10 Thunder.
  - *2x Cryo-Torpedo Tubes (4 slots):* Ranged Weapon (+6), 3d10 Cold damage. On a hit, the target ship's speed is halved for 1 round as ice forms on the hull.
- **Standard Action:** *Deployment.* The Captain can deploy a boarding party of Crimson Guard without needing to grapple the enemy ship, provided they breach adjacent to the target.

- **HP:** 600 | **AC:** 12 | **Speed:** 30 ft. | **Regeneration:** 60 HP/round

  - *4x Sonic Projectors (16 slots):* Ranged Weapon (+8), 4d10 Thunder.
  - *2x Cryo-Torpedo Tubes (4 slots):* Ranged Weapon (+6), 3d10 Cold damage.
- **Standard Action:** *Thrall Swarm.* The ship releases a swarm of undead Kuo-toa into the water, acting as a hazard that deals 4d6 slashing damage to any ship passing through a 50-foot radius.

## 4. The Free Captains Alliance (The Swarm)

**Faction Chassis Trait: Scavenger Sails.** Free Captain ships have +10 ft Base Speed. They ignore difficult terrain (like rough waves or reefs) and can make 90-degree turns effortlessly without penalty. 
**Special Ability: Jury-Rigged Volatility.** Whenever a Free Captain ship hits with naval artillery, they may choose to roll an additional 2d10 damage. However, if they roll a natural 1 on an attack roll, the weapon explodes, destroying the Weapon Slot permanently and dealing 4d10 fire damage to their own ship.

**Faction Chassis Trait: Living Iron-Wood.** Keeper ships are immune to critical hits and fire damage, as the wet kelp and coral simply absorb the blow. 
**Special Ability: Grasping Vines.** When a Keeper ship rams or is rammed by an enemy, the hull immediately vines out, grappling the enemy ship. A grappled ship has its speed reduced to 0. A crew must spend a Standard Action (Engineering/Damage Control) and succeed on a DC 16 check to chop away the vines.

### Keeper Bramble-Skiff (Size 2)
*A stealthy, organic scout vessel.*
- **HP:** 150 | **AC:** 15 | **Speed:** 60 ft.
- **Standard Armament (4 Slots):**

*A massive floating island carrying enraged sea beasts.*

- **Standard Action:** *Deploy Beasts.* The ship releases a pod of bonded Elementals or Dire Sharks, acting as independent combatants on the battlefield.
# Chapter 28: Support Crew Classes

In the Shattered Expanse, a ship is only as strong as its crew. While the Captain and the combat specialists repel boarders and fire the cannons, the ship is kept afloat, the crew fed, and the course charted by the vital Support Crew. 

## The Secondary Class System

**Support Crew Classes** are a secondary leveling system designed to grant players (or vital NPC Sidekicks) exceptional role-related bonuses and significant combat defense, without increasing their direct offensive capabilities. 

- **Automatic Progression:** A character gains a level in their chosen Support Crew Class automatically whenever their overall Character Level increases. 
- **Non-Combat Focus:** These classes grant no Extra Attacks, no offensive spellcasting, and no damage-scaling features.
- **Exceptional Defense:** Because these characters are often the targets of boarding actions but lack offensive scaling, these classes provide potent defensive mechanics to ensure their survival.

---

## The Ship's Cook
An army marches on its stomach; a pirate crew sails on its rations. The Ship's Cook is the heart of morale, capable of turning hardtack and aether-mutated fish into a feast that fortifies the soul and hardens the body against the harsh ocean.

| Level | Feature |
| :---: | :--- |
| 1st | Iron Stomach, Hearty Rations |
| 5th | Aether-Infused Broth |
| 9th | Cleaver's Ward |
| 13th | Feast of the Leviathan |
| 17th | Unbreakable Constitution |
| 20th | Master of the Galley |

**Iron Stomach (1st Level)**
You gain proficiency with Cook's Utensils. You have resistance to poison damage and a +4 bonus on saving throws against being poisoned or diseased. 

**Hearty Rations (1st Level)**
During a short rest, you can prepare meals for a number of creatures up to your proficiency bonus. Those creatures regain the maximum number of hit points from any Hit Dice they spend during that short rest, rather than rolling.

**Aether-Infused Broth (5th Level)**
You learn to cook using the chaotic aether-water of the Unwritten Sea. Over a long rest, you can brew a number of thermos-flasks equal to your Constitution modifier. As a swift action, a creature can drink from a flask to gain temporary hit points equal to 2d10 + your Cook level. These flasks lose their potency after 24 hours.

**Cleaver's Ward (9th Level)**
The galley is a dangerous place, and you've learned to deflect harm instinctively. While you are holding a knife, cleaver, or similar cooking implement, and are wearing no armor, your Armor Class equals 10 + your Dexterity modifier + your Constitution modifier.

**Feast of the Leviathan (13th Level)**
Once per week, you can spend 4 hours preparing a massive feast. Up to 10 creatures who partake in this feast are cured of all diseases and poisons, have all exhaustion levels removed, and have a +4 bonus on all Fortitude saving throws for the next 24 hours.

**Unbreakable Constitution (17th Level)**
Your body has endured so many strange ingredients and harsh conditions that it is nigh-indestructible. Whenever you take damage, you can use your reaction to halve the damage against you.

**Master of the Galley (20th Level)**
Your presence on a ship guarantees morale will never break. As long as you are alive and cooking, the ship's crew is immune to being frightened, and they gain a +10 bonus to their maximum hit points.

---

## The Surgeon
Battles on the high seas are brutal affairs of splintering wood and rending cannonballs. The Surgeon is responsible for triage, patching up the crew, and ensuring that those who fall can stand again. 

| Level | Feature |
| :---: | :--- |
| 1st | Combat Medic, Do No Harm |
| 5th | Alchemical Salves |
| 9th | Evasive Triage |
| 13th | Miraculous Stitching |
| 17th | Aura of Preservation |
| 20th | Master Surgeon |

**Combat Medic (1st Level)**
You gain proficiency in the Medicine skill and with the Herbalism Kit. When you use a healer's kit to stabilize a dying creature, that creature also regains 1 hit point.

**Do No Harm (1st Level)**
You project an aura of neutrality. As long as you have not dealt damage to an enemy creature on your turn, you are under the effects of the *Sanctuary* spell (save DC = 8 + your proficiency bonus + your Wisdom modifier). This effect refreshes at the start of each of your turns, provided you continue to deal no damage.

**Alchemical Salves (5th Level)**
You can spend 1 hour during a long rest brewing medicinal salves. You create a number of salves equal to your Wisdom modifier. As an action, you can apply a salve to a creature to cure them of one disease, neutralize one poison, or end the blinded, deafened, or paralyzed condition.

**Evasive Triage (9th Level)**
Your dedication to reaching the wounded makes you incredibly difficult to pin down. Your walking speed increases by 10 feet. When you take the run action or a double move, opportunity attacks made against you take a -4 penalty.

**Miraculous Stitching (13th Level)**
If you spend 10 minutes treating a creature that has lost a body part (a finger, an eye, a whole limb) and you have the severed part, you can perfectly reattach it. Alternatively, if a creature has died within the last minute, you can expend three uses of a healer's kit to cast *Revivify* without expending a spell slot or material components. Once you use this revivification feature, you cannot do so again until you finish a long rest.

**Aura of Preservation (17th Level)**
Your sheer medical expertise creates a zone of safety. Friendly creatures within 30 feet of you have a +4 bonus on stabilization checks, and if they roll a 15 or higher on a check to stabilize, they regain 1 hit point.

**Master Surgeon (20th Level)**
You have mastered the anatomy of all mortal creatures. As an action, you can touch a creature and restore it to its hit point maximum, curing all ailments, curses, and levels of exhaustion. Once you use this feature, you cannot use it again until you finish a long rest.

---

## The Quartermaster
The Quartermaster handles logistics, distributes the loot, and serves as the intermediary between the Captain and the crew. A good Quartermaster ensures the ship runs like a well-oiled machine and that the crew feels valued.

| Level | Feature |
| :---: | :--- |
| 1st | Silver Tongue, Manifest Destiny |
| 5th | Valuable Asset |
| 9th | "I Know a Guy" |
| 13th | Morale Officer |
| 17th | Impeccable Ledger |
| 20th | Master of Logistics |

**Silver Tongue (1st Level)**
You gain proficiency in Persuasion and Intimidation. When interacting with merchants, port authorities, or rival crews, you have a +4 bonus on Charisma checks to negotiate prices or secure passage.

**Manifest Destiny (1st Level)**
You always know exactly what is in the ship's cargo hold. Once per long rest, if the crew desperately needs a mundane item (a coil of rope, a spyglass, a specific common tool) worth 50 gp or less, you can "find" one buried in the hold, adding it to the party's inventory.

**Valuable Asset (5th Level)**
The crew instinctively protects the one who signs their paychecks. While you are within 5 feet of an allied creature, you gain a +2 bonus to your Armor Class and saving throws.

**"I Know a Guy" (9th Level)**
Your network of informants and merchants spans the Charted Waters. When you arrive in any port, you can spend 1 hour to locate an individual who can provide specific, highly sensitive information, illegal goods, or a safehouse.

**Morale Officer (13th Level)**
You know exactly what to say to keep the crew fighting. As a swift action, you can shout words of encouragement. All friendly creatures within 60 feet of you who can hear you gain temporary hit points equal to your Quartermaster level + your Charisma modifier. You can use this feature a number of times equal to your Charisma modifier per long rest.

**Impeccable Ledger (17th Level)**
You are immune to being charmed, and magic cannot read your thoughts or determine if you are lying. Additionally, when you use your Manifest Destiny feature, you can "find" a single magical item of Uncommon or Rare rarity, which lasts for 24 hours before you realize it was actually a highly unstable counterfeit that dissolves into dust.

**Master of Logistics (20th Level)**
Your ship operates with terrifying efficiency. The travel time for any voyage your ship undertakes is halved, the cost of mundane ship repairs is reduced to zero, and the crew's daily ration requirement is halved.

---

## The Navigator
The sea is a treacherous, shifting maze. The Navigator reads the stars, the aether-currents, and the behavior of the Leviathans to chart safe passage where others would find only destruction.

| Level | Feature |
| :---: | :--- |
| 1st | Master Cartographer, Weather Eye |
| 5th | Evasive Maneuvers |
| 9th | Star-Guided Path |
| 13th | Uncanny Dodge |
| 17th | Predict the Flow |
| 20th | Master Navigator |

**Master Cartographer (1st Level)**
You gain proficiency with Navigator's Tools and Cartographer's Tools. You have perfect recall of any map you have studied, and you cannot become lost by magical or non-magical means while you can see the sky or the ocean.

**Weather Eye (1st Level)**
You can accurately predict the weather at your location for the next 48 hours. You also have a +4 bonus on any ability checks made to steer or command a ship during a storm or hazardous environmental condition.

**Evasive Maneuvers (5th Level)**
Your ability to read the battlefield is as sharp as your ability to read a map. When you are not wearing heavy armor, your Armor Class increases by an amount equal to your Intelligence modifier (minimum of +1).

**Star-Guided Path (9th Level)**
You can spend 10 minutes charting a course for a specific destination. For the next 24 hours, the travel pace of your party or your ship is doubled, and you have a +4 bonus on Survival checks made to avoid natural hazards or ambushes along that route.

**Uncanny Dodge (13th Level)**
Your spatial awareness is unparalleled. When an attacker that you can see hits you with an attack, you can use your reaction to halve the attack's damage against you.

**Predict the Flow (17th Level)**
You can read the aetheric currents to see seconds into the future. You have a +4 bonus on all Reflex saving throws, and attackers take a -4 penalty on attack rolls against you as long as you are not incapacitated.

**Master Navigator (20th Level)**
You can chart a course through the fabric of reality itself. Once per week, you can guide your ship through a localized aether-rift, effectively casting the *Teleport* spell, affecting the entire ship and everyone aboard it.

---

## The Bosun
The Bosun (Boatswain) is the chief engineer and the primary enforcer of discipline on deck. They are tough, pragmatic, and capable of fixing a broken mast with nothing but twine, tar, and sheer willpower.

| Level | Feature |
| :---: | :--- |
| 1st | Jury-Rigger, Barked Orders |
| 5th | Tough as Nails |
| 9th | Damage Control |
| 13th | Hold the Line |
| 17th | Unstoppable Force |
| 20th | Master Bosun |

**Jury-Rigger (1st Level)**
You gain proficiency with Carpenter's Tools and Tinker's Tools. You can perform temporary repairs on ships, vehicles, or mechanical objects in half the usual time. During combat, you can use an action to restore 2d10 Hull Points to your ship.

**Barked Orders (1st Level)**
Your voice commands instant obedience. You can use the aid another action as a swift action, provided the creature you are helping can hear you and understand you.

**Tough as Nails (5th Level)**
Your skin is like tanned leather and your bones like ironwood. Bludgeoning, piercing, and slashing damage that you take from non-magical attacks is reduced by 3.

**Damage Control (9th Level)**
When your ship takes damage, you can use your reaction to immediately organize a bucket brigade or brace the hull. Roll a d12 and subtract the result from the damage the ship takes. You can use this feature a number of times equal to your Strength or Constitution modifier per long rest.

**Hold the Line (13th Level)**
You are immovable when you choose to be. You have a +4 bonus on any saving throw or ability check to resist being grappled, shoved, or knocked prone. Additionally, your Tough as Nails damage reduction increases to 5, and applies to all damage types except psychic.

**Unstoppable Force (17th Level)**
If you drop to 0 hit points and don't die outright, you can make a DC 10 Constitution saving throw. If you succeed, you drop to 1 hit point instead. Each time you use this feature after the first, the DC increases by 5. When you finish a short or long rest, the DC resets to 10.

**Master Bosun (20th Level)**
Your ship is an extension of your own invincible will. While you are conscious and on board, your ship gains resistance to all damage types, and it cannot be sunk (even if reduced to 0 Hull Points) as long as you remain standing on its deck.
# Chapter 29: The Expanse Arsenal

The Shattered Expanse is littered with the technological remnants of the First Captains, the aether-infused weaponry of the Dominion, and the cursed artifacts of the deep ocean. The items presented in this chapter are unique to the Charted Waters and can be used as high-value salvage, faction rewards, or black-market purchases.

---

## 1. Dominion Tech
*Forged in the industrial foundries of the Crimson Dominion, these items prioritize lethal efficiency, absolute order, and aether-combustion.*

#### Aether-Rifle
*Weapon (firearm), rare (requires attunement)*
**Cost:** 2,500 gp
This heavy, brass-and-iron rifle hums with volatile energy. You gain a +1 bonus to attack and damage rolls made with this magic weapon.
It fires concentrated bolts of force rather than physical bullets.
- **Damage:** 2d8 force damage.
- **Properties:** Ammunition (range 100/400), two-handed, reload (6 shots).
- **Overcharge:** As a swift action, you can overcharge the rifle for your next attack. If the attack hits, it deals an additional 2d8 force damage, but the weapon immediately jams. You must spend a standard action to vent the aether-steam before the weapon can be fired again.

#### Inquisitor's Monocle
*Wondrous item, uncommon (requires attunement)*
**Cost:** 800 gp
A brass monocle fitted with a lens carved from raw aether-crystal. While wearing this monocle, you have a +4 bonus on Sense Motive checks to determine if someone is lying.
Additionally, the monocle holds 1 charge. You can expend the charge to cast the *zone of truth* spell (save DC 13). The monocle regains its expended charge daily at dawn.

#### Ironclad Plating
*Armor (half plate), very rare (requires attunement by a creature with a Strength score of 15 or higher)*
**Cost:** 12,000 gp
This heavy suit of mechanized armor is powered by a small aether-engine mounted on the back. While wearing this armor, you gain a +1 bonus to AC.
- **Juggernaut:** Any critical hit against you becomes a normal hit.
- **Heavy Machinery:** The armor is incredibly loud and unwieldy. You take a -4 penalty on Stealth and Swim checks. If you fall into water while wearing this armor, you sink at a rate of 30 feet per round.

---

## 2. Consortium Artifacts
*Created by the Guildmasters of the Grand Merchant Consortium, these items are designed for securing wealth, enforcing contracts, and moving illicit goods.*

#### The Golden Ledger
*Wondrous item, rare*
**Cost:** 5,000 gp (Rarely sold, highly illegal to steal)
A heavy book bound in gilded leather. If a creature voluntarily signs their name in the ledger, agreeing to a specific debt or contract, the terms become magically binding.
If the signer breaks the terms of the contract, the ledger glows red. The oathbreaker is immediately subjected to a curse: they suffer a -4 penalty on all Charisma checks, and any gold or gems they touch instantly turn into worthless lead. The curse can only be lifted by the owner of the ledger crossing out the signature, or by a *remove curse* spell cast at 5th level or higher.

#### Smuggler's Coin
*Wondrous item, uncommon*
**Cost:** 500 gp
This silver coin bears the face of a laughing skull on one side and a blank surface on the other. As an action, you can flip the coin. When you do, a thick cloud of magical, obscuring fog erupts from the coin, heavily obscuring a 10-foot radius centered on you.
Simultaneously, you become *invisible* (as the spell) for 1 minute, or until you attack or cast a spell. The fog lasts for 1 minute or until dispersed by a strong wind. Once used, the coin becomes a mundane piece of silver.

#### Guildmaster's Signet
*Ring, very rare (requires attunement)*
**Cost:** 15,000 gp
A heavy gold ring bearing the seal of the Consortium. While wearing this ring, your words carry the supernatural weight of absolute authority.
- **Appraisal:** You can use an action to touch an object and instantly know its exact monetary value in the current local market, as well as identify any magical properties it possesses (as the *identify* spell).
- **Compel the Market:** The ring has 3 charges. You can expend 1 charge as an action to cast the *suggestion* spell (save DC 16). The ring regains 1d3 expended charges daily at dawn.

---

## 3. Deep Magic
*Salvaged from the Drowned Silk Houses or the abyssal trenches, these items carry the terrifying cold and predatory nature of the deep ocean.*

#### Black Coral Dagger
*Weapon (dagger), rare (requires attunement)*
**Cost:** 3,000 gp
Carved from a single piece of razor-sharp, obsidian-black coral, this blade seems to drink the light around it. You gain a +1 bonus to attack and damage rolls made with this magic weapon.
When you hit a creature with this dagger, the target takes an extra 1d6 cold damage. Furthermore, the freezing necrotic energy prevents healing; the target cannot regain hit points until the start of your next turn.

#### Abyssal Lure
*Wondrous item, uncommon*
**Cost:** 600 gp
A necklace bearing a small, bioluminescent orb harvested from a deep-sea predator. As an action, you can cause the orb to flare with hypnotic blue light.
One beast or monstrosity of your choice that you can see within 30 feet of you must succeed on a DC 13 Wisdom saving throw or become charmed by you for 1 minute. While charmed in this way, the creature is incapacitated and uses all its movement on its turn to move as close to the Lure as possible. The effect ends early if the creature takes any damage. Once used, the Lure cannot be used again until the next midnight.

#### Cloak of the Deep Mother
*Wondrous item, very rare (requires attunement)*
**Cost:** 18,000 gp
Woven from the silk of abyssal spiders and enchanted by the High Priestesses of the Silk Houses. While wearing this cloak, you can breathe underwater, and you gain a swimming speed of 40 feet.
- **Ink Cloud Escape:** When you take damage, you can use your reaction to magically transform into a cloud of thick, black ink. You take no damage from the triggering attack or effect, and you instantly teleport up to 30 feet to an unoccupied space you can see. Once you use this property, you can't use it again until you finish a long rest.

---

## 4. Relics of the First Captains
*These items are ancient, highly advanced technology that is often indistinguishable from magic. They are powerful, highly sought after by the Independent Salvagers, and occasionally unstable.*

#### Aegis Shield-Bracer
*Armor (shield), rare (requires attunement)*
**Cost:** 4,500 gp
A sleek, metallic bracer that hums with energy. While wearing this bracer, you can use a swift action to activate it, causing a disc of crackling blue "hard-light" to project from your wrist. While active, it provides a +2 bonus to AC, leaving your hand free to hold (but not attack with) other items.
- **Unstable Matrix:** When a creature rolls a natural 20 on a melee attack against you while the shield is active, the matrix overloads. You and the attacker both take 3d6 force damage, and the shield deactivates and cannot be used again until you finish a short rest.

#### Gravity Anchor
*Wondrous item, very rare*
**Cost:** 20,000 gp
A heavy cube of polished white metal, covered in shifting geometric lines. As an action, you can press the activation rune and throw the cube to a point on the ground up to 60 feet away.
Upon landing, the cube alters the localized gravity field. For 1 minute, the gravity in a 20-foot-radius cylinder extending 40 feet upward from the cube is completely reversed (as the *reverse gravity* spell, save DC 16). The cube cannot be moved while active. Once used, the cube goes dormant and cannot be used again until 7 days have passed.

#### The First Compass
*Wondrous item, legendary (requires attunement)*
**Cost:** Priceless
A perfectly spherical compass made of an unknown, indestructible glass. The needle inside does not point north. Instead, while you hold it, the needle points toward the object, creature, or location you desire most in the world.
- **The True Path:** The compass always shows the most direct route, but not the safest. If the path intersects with a physical barrier (like a mountain or the bottom of the ocean), the needle will point directly through it.
- **Aetheric Resonance:** The compass is highly radioactive with ancient magic. For every 24 hours you remain attuned to it, you must succeed on a DC 15 Constitution saving throw or your hit point maximum is reduced by 1d10. This reduction lasts until you end your attunement to the compass and cast a *greater restoration* spell on yourself.
# Chapter 29: Thematic Ship Models

While Chapter 28 provides the mechanical statblocks for enemy vessels, this chapter provides the "theater of the mind." These thematic models describe what the ships look like, what they smell like, how they are laid out internally, and how the crew behaves in combat. DMs can use these descriptions to bring the Shattered Expanse to life.

---

## 1. The Crimson Dominion

Dominion ships are industrial nightmares of iron and smoke, designed to project absolute authority.

### The Ironclad Escort (Size 2)
*The fist of the Dominion, patrolling borders and enforcing blockades.*
- **Visual Silhouette:** A low, squat vessel almost entirely encased in riveted, dark-grey Aether-Iron. It lacks traditional sails, driven instead by a churning rear paddlewheel powered by an arcane furnace. A thick plume of black, acrid smoke constantly trails behind it.
- **Deck & Aesthetics:** The deck is cramped, hot, and smells strongly of ozone and sulfur. The command deck is completely enclosed in an iron pillbox to protect the Captain. 
- **Encounter Hook:** The players are caught sailing through Dominion-claimed waters. The Ironclad does not ask for identification; it fires a warning shot of pure arcane energy across the bow and demands immediate surrender and boarding for "inspection."
- **Tactical Behavior:** Relentless pursuit. They will use their *Arcane Ramming* ability to smash through smaller escort vessels or debris to get directly alongside their target.

### The Magi-Cruiser (Size 3)
*A line-breaker designed to deliver devastating magical payloads.*
- **Visual Silhouette:** A sharp, angular vessel that cuts through the waves like a spearhead. It features three massive, glowing arcane conduits (acting as masts) that hum with contained elemental energy. The hull is painted a deep, blood red.
- **Deck & Aesthetics:** Impeccably clean and highly disciplined. The lower decks feature heavily warded containment rooms where chained, exhausted spellcasters (often slaves) act as living batteries for the ship's Precursor Cannon.
- **Encounter Hook:** A Magi-Cruiser is deployed specifically to hunt down the players after they successfully raid a Dominion stronghold. 
- **Tactical Behavior:** Methodical artillery bombardment. The Captain will keep the ship at maximum range, relying on the Bosun's *Aether Surge* to pummel the enemy ship into submission before ever attempting a boarding action.

### The Dreadnought (Size 4)
*A floating fortress. Terrifyingly durable, but incredibly cumbersome.*
- **Visual Silhouette:** A moving mountain of iron. It is so heavy it sits dangerously low in the water. It bristles with multi-tiered cannon batteries, and its central smokestacks are tall enough to rival a small castle tower.
- **Deck & Aesthetics:** The interior is a labyrinth of blistering hot boiler rooms, barracks for hundreds of marines, and lavish, insulated officer's quarters. The holding cells in the brig are specifically designed to suppress magic.
- **Encounter Hook:** The players must break a Dominion siege on a Free Captain harbor, and the Dreadnought is the anchor of the blockade.
- **Tactical Behavior:** The Dreadnought moves slowly, establishing an *Unbreakable Line*. It acts as a mobile command center, absorbing fire while its massive broadsides methodically tear apart the largest threats on the battlefield.

---

## 2. The Grand Merchant Consortium

Consortium ships are designed to protect investments. They are ostentatious, heavily shielded, and prioritize cargo over crew comfort.

### The Audit-Frigate (Size 2)
*Fast, shielded escorts designed to stall enemies while cargo escapes.*
- **Visual Silhouette:** Sleek and aerodynamic, painted in pristine white and gold. The hull is covered in subtle, glowing blue geometric runes (the Abjuration Shield). 
- **Deck & Aesthetics:** Functional but highly polished. The captain's cabin resembles a wealthy banker's office, complete with mahogany desks and magical ledgers. The air smells of expensive sea-salt perfume and polished brass.
- **Encounter Hook:** The players are hired to protect a Salvager vessel, and an Audit-Frigate intercepts them, demanding the "Guild Tax" on any recovered artifacts.
- **Tactical Behavior:** Annoy and delay. The Captain will use the *Bribe* action to confuse the enemy, while the ship uses its speed to stay out of ramming range, constantly regenerating its shield.

### The Dividend-Galleon (Size 3)
*A heavy cargo hauler that relies entirely on magical defenses to survive.*
- **Visual Silhouette:** A fat, wide-bodied galleon with massive, billowing yellow sails. The ship sits heavily in the water, visibly laden with cargo. The Abjuration Shield is so thick it visibly ripples the air around the hull.
- **Deck & Aesthetics:** The entire lower deck is a massive *Smuggler's Hold* Extra-Dimensional space. The crew quarters are incredibly cramped to maximize profit margins. The Ledger-Mage sits in a suspended glass dome above the main deck to calculate combat risks.
- **Encounter Hook:** The players discover a distressed Dividend-Galleon that has been caught in a storm, its shields fluctuating. Do they help it for a reward, or pirate its massive cargo?
- **Tactical Behavior:** Evasive cowardice. The ship will drop an *Alchemical Smokescreen* at the first sign of real danger and attempt to flee. If cornered, the Bosun will constantly *Divert Power* to keep the shields up until reinforcements arrive.

### The Vault-Ship (Size 4)
*An unsinkable, slow-moving bank vault transporting immense wealth.*
- **Visual Silhouette:** A colossal, square-rigged behemoth. It looks less like a ship and more like a floating fortress of polished stone and gilded wood. The hull is reinforced with sheets of alchemical lead to block scrying.
- **Deck & Aesthetics:** The interior is literally a bank vault. The cargo is kept behind massive, puzzle-locked iron doors. The Gold Cloak Enforcers patrol the decks in perfectly synchronized shifts.
- **Encounter Hook:** The ultimate heist. The players have been hired by the Free Captains to intercept a Vault-Ship carrying a year's worth of collected taxes.
- **Tactical Behavior:** *Total Lockdown.* The Vault-Ship will stop moving entirely, doubling its shield regeneration, and rely on its massive array of broadside cannons and Siege-Breaker Mortars to obliterate anything that gets too close.

---

## 3. The Drowned Silk Houses

Drow ships are terrifying, biological atrocities that strike from the pitch-black depths.

### The Viper-Skiff (Size 2)
*A stealthy, silent assassin sub.*
- **Visual Silhouette:** Looks like a massive, 60-foot-long sleek black eel. It has no sails, no deck, and no windows. It moves silently beneath the waves, propelled by undulating, magically animated fins.
- **Deck & Aesthetics:** There is no "deck." The interior is a cramped, suffocating tube of ribbed bone and pulsing purple flesh. The air is stale, humid, and smells of rotting fish. The crew operates in complete darkness using darkvision.
- **Encounter Hook:** The players are transporting a high-value political prisoner (or defector). A Viper-Skiff tracks them and attempts an underwater assassination.
- **Tactical Behavior:** *Silent Running.* The Skiff will stay submerged, tracking the target using sonar. It will surface directly alongside the target in the dead of night, deploy assassins, and submerge again before the alarm is raised.

### The Arachna-Submersible (Size 3)
*A terrifying hunter made of enchanted chitin.*
- **Visual Silhouette:** The hollowed-out exoskeleton of a massive deep-sea crab or spider. It bristles with spiked legs that it uses to physically cling to the underside of enemy ships. 
- **Deck & Aesthetics:** The "command bridge" is located in the transparent skull-casing of the beast. The Flesh-Weavers constantly stitch the internal membranes together to keep the water out. The ship literally bleeds when damaged.
- **Encounter Hook:** The players' ship suddenly stops moving in open water. An Arachna-Submersible has clamped onto their keel from below and is actively freezing the water around them with Cryo-Torpedoes.
- **Tactical Behavior:** The *Abyssal Breach*. The ship will erupt from the water, creating massive waves, and immediately initiate a *Deployment* of Crimson Guard marines directly onto the enemy deck under the cover of magical *Darkness*.

### The Necropolis-Carrier (Size 4)
*A mobile hive built into a skeletal whale.*
- **Visual Silhouette:** A 300-foot-long undead leviathan. Its ribcage is exposed, and within the cavernous chest cavity, glowing green necromantic fires illuminate thousands of swarming thralls. 
- **Deck & Aesthetics:** A nightmare of undeath. The decks are made of calcified bone. The ship is commanded by a Matron-Admiral who sits on a throne of coral attached directly to the leviathan's exposed spine, controlling it telepathically.
- **Encounter Hook:** The players must delve into the Sunless Sea, and their path is blocked by a Necropolis-Carrier guarding a Drown-Gate.
- **Tactical Behavior:** Overwhelming numbers. The Carrier will release a *Thrall Swarm* of undead Kuo-toa to clog the water and foul enemy propellers/rudders, while unleashing devastating barrages of Sonic Projectors that shatter enemy hulls from the inside out.

---

## 4. The Free Captains Alliance

Alliance ships are highly customized, wildly dangerous, and built for speed and shock-and-awe.

### The Zephyr-Sloop (Size 2)
*Impossibly fast blockade runners built for smuggling.*
- **Visual Silhouette:** A tiny hull with an absurd amount of rigging and *Scavenger Sails*. The sails are a patchwork of different colored canvas, kraken hide, and elemental silk. The ship looks like it should capsize in a stiff breeze.
- **Deck & Aesthetics:** Chaotic and cluttered. Ropes are everywhere. The deck smells of cheap rum, sea spray, and black powder. The ship is stripped of all luxury to reduce weight.
- **Encounter Hook:** The players need to hire a smuggler to get them past a Dominion blockade, and a cocky Wayfinder captain offers them a ride on a Zephyr-Sloop.
- **Tactical Behavior:** *Full Sail!* They will never engage in a prolonged fight. They rely on their ridiculous speed and maneuverability to dart through narrow reefs where heavier ships cannot follow, taking potshots with Swivel Guns.

### The Corsair-Brig (Size 3)
*The backbone of the pirate fleets, built for swift boarding.*
- **Visual Silhouette:** A menacing, dark-wood brigantine. It is bristling with more cannons than its size category should normally allow. The prow is often decorated with a terrifying (and sometimes living) figurehead.
- **Deck & Aesthetics:** Loud, rowdy, and dangerous. The Powder Monkeys run frantically between decks. Weapons are piled everywhere. The Quartermaster can usually be found shouting orders from the main mast.
- **Encounter Hook:** A classic pirate ambush. The players are caught in a magically summoned fog bank, only to hear sea-shanties echoing through the mist just before the Corsair-Brig rams them.
- **Tactical Behavior:** Aggressive and suicidal. They rely on *Jury-Rigged Volatility* for massive burst damage to cripple the enemy, followed immediately by a *Grapple and Board!* action to overwhelm the defenders in melee.

### The Kraken-Class Flagship (Size 4)
*A stolen, heavily modified capital ship prioritizing guns over armor.*
- **Visual Silhouette:** A massive Dominion Dreadnought or Consortium Galleon that has been aggressively "redecorated." The armor has been stripped away to increase speed, replaced by terrifying war-paint, spiked rams, and scavenged Precursor weaponry that sparks with unstable energy.
- **Deck & Aesthetics:** It feels less like a ship and more like a floating, heavily armed tavern. The Pirate Lord commands from a throne made of stolen Dominion armor plates. 
- **Encounter Hook:** The players have angered the Brethren Court, and Pirate King Jax has sent his personal Flagship to make an example of them.
- **Tactical Behavior:** Pure, overwhelming force. The Pirate Lord will issue the *Pirate King's Command*, sending hundreds of screaming corsairs swinging across the rigging while the unstable Precursor Cannons tear the enemy hull to shreds.

---

## 5. The Spirit Keepers

Keeper ships are organic, symbiotic entities that use the ocean itself as a weapon.

### The Bramble-Skiff (Size 2)
*A stealthy, organic scout vessel.*
- **Visual Silhouette:** It doesn't look like a ship; it looks like a floating tangle of driftwood, mangrove roots, and green kelp. It has no visible sails, moving instead by the manipulation of water currents beneath it.
- **Deck & Aesthetics:** The deck is soft and spongy, made of living moss and wet wood. The air smells strongly of fresh rain and blooming lotus. The crew moves silently, communicating through hand signs and spirit whispers.
- **Encounter Hook:** The players anchor near a seemingly deserted island, only to realize the "mangrove swamp" next to them is actually a fleet of Bramble-Skiffs surrounding them.
- **Tactical Behavior:** *Camouflage.* They rely on ambush and guerrilla tactics. They will decloak, fire a volley of Coral-Shard Ballistae, and immediately blend back into the coastal foliage.

### The Iron-Reef Galleon (Size 3)
*A heavy, calcified combat vessel.*
- **Visual Silhouette:** A sunken ship that has been brought back to the surface, completely encrusted in magically hardened, razor-sharp coral. The *Living Iron-Wood* hull is jagged and vibrant with bioluminescence. 
- **Deck & Aesthetics:** The interior is cavernous and damp, illuminated by glowing algae. Water flows freely through the lower decks, allowing bonded aquatic elementals to swim through the ship. 
- **Encounter Hook:** A Keeper Warden intercepts the players' ship because they are carrying a corrupted artifact that is polluting the Aether Streams.
- **Tactical Behavior:** Area denial. The First Mate will use *Tide-Caller's Wind* to stall the enemy ship, while the Shamanic Focuses call down lightning strikes. If rammed, their *Grasping Vines* will trap the enemy vessel against the razor-sharp coral hull.

### The World-Tree Carrier (Size 4)
*A massive floating island carrying enraged sea beasts.*
- **Visual Silhouette:** A literal floating island. A massive, ancient banyan tree grows from the center of a calcified coral atoll. The roots dangle hundreds of feet into the ocean. The canopy acts as a massive sail catching the Aether winds.
- **Deck & Aesthetics:** A thriving, enclosed ecosystem. Birds nest in the rigging, and freshwater springs bubble up from the deck. The High Elder communes with spirits at the base of the central tree.
- **Encounter Hook:** The players must seek out a World-Tree Carrier to ask a High Elder how to cure a magical plague affecting their crew.
- **Tactical Behavior:** The Wrath of Nature. The Carrier rarely fires its own weapons. Instead, it will *Deploy Beasts*, unleashing bonded Dire Sharks, Water Elementals, and Giant Squids to tear the enemy fleet apart while the High Elder manipulates the ocean currents to crush the enemy against the reefs.

# Chapter 30: The Bestiary of the Charted Waters

The Shattered Expanse is home to more than just rival pirate crews. Horrific monstrosities lurk in the abyssal depths, ancient machines guard ruined islands, and the empires of the world deploy terrifying weapons of war. 

This bestiary provides custom stat blocks tailored to the unique factions and environments of the setting.

---

## The Crimson Dominion

### Dominion Inquisitor
*Medium humanoid (human), lawful evil*
**Armor Class:** 15 (studded leather)
**Hit Points:** 71 (11d8 + 22)
**Speed:** 30 ft.

| STR | DEX | CON | INT | WIS | CHA |
| :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: |
| 12 (+1) | 16 (+3) | 14 (+2) | 14 (+2) | 16 (+3) | 15 (+2) |

**Saving Throws:** Dex +6, Wis +6
**Skills:** Insight +6, Investigation +5, Perception +6
**Senses:** passive Perception 16
**Languages:** Common, Dominion standard
**Challenge:** 5 (1,800 XP)

**Inquisitor's Monocle:** The inquisitor has advantage on Wisdom (Insight) checks, and can cast *zone of truth* (save DC 14) once per day without expending spell slots.
**Aether-Powered Munitions:** The inquisitor's weapon attacks are magical. When the inquisitor hits with any weapon, the weapon deals an extra 1d8 force damage (included in the attack).

#### Actions
**Multiattack:** The inquisitor makes two attacks with their Aether-Rifle or their Rapier.

**Rapier:** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +6 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. *Hit:* 7 (1d8 + 3) piercing damage plus 4 (1d8) force damage.

**Aether-Rifle:** *Ranged Weapon Attack:* +6 to hit, range 100/400 ft., one target. *Hit:* 12 (2d8 + 3) piercing damage plus 4 (1d8) force damage.

**Suppressing Fire (Recharge 5-6):** The inquisitor fires a barrage of aether-bolts in a 30-foot cone. Each creature in that area must make a DC 14 Dexterity saving throw, taking 27 (6d8) force damage on a failed save, or half as much damage on a successful one. Any creature that fails the save has its speed halved until the end of its next turn.

#### Reactions
**Parry:** The inquisitor adds 3 to its AC against one melee attack that would hit it. To do so, the inquisitor must see the attacker and be wielding a melee weapon.

---

### Ironclad Juggernaut
*Large humanoid (any race), lawful evil*
**Armor Class:** 19 (Ironclad Plating)
**Hit Points:** 114 (12d10 + 48)
**Speed:** 20 ft.

| STR | DEX | CON | INT | WIS | CHA |
| :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: |
| 20 (+5) | 8 (-1) | 18 (+4) | 10 (+0) | 12 (+1) | 9 (-1) |

**Saving Throws:** Str +8, Con +7
**Damage Resistances:** bludgeoning, piercing, and slashing from nonmagical attacks
**Condition Immunities:** frightened, poisoned
**Senses:** passive Perception 11
**Languages:** Common
**Challenge:** 8 (3,900 XP)

**Juggernaut Armor:** Any critical hit against the Juggernaut becomes a normal hit. It has disadvantage on Dexterity (Stealth) checks and sinks in water.
**Siege Monster:** The Juggernaut deals double damage to objects and structures.

#### Actions
**Multiattack:** The Juggernaut makes two attacks with its Siege Hammer.

**Siege Hammer:** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +8 to hit, reach 10 ft., one target. *Hit:* 18 (2d12 + 5) bludgeoning damage. If the target is a creature, it must succeed on a DC 16 Strength saving throw or be knocked prone.

**Aether-Vent (Recharge 6):** The Juggernaut vents superheated aether-steam from its armor in a 15-foot radius. Each creature in that area must make a DC 15 Constitution saving throw, taking 35 (10d6) fire damage on a failed save, or half as much on a successful one.

# Chapter 31: The Deep Economy

In the Shattered Expanse, gold is the true god. The Crimson Dominion needs it to fuel their warmachine, the Consortium hoards it, and the Free Captains bleed for it. 

This chapter provides a massive framework for running a dynamic mercantile campaign. Instead of just looting dungeons, players can act as privateers or smugglers, buying low, selling high, managing heat with the major factions, and investing their ill-gotten gains into a fully-staffed Pirate Hideout.

---

## 1. Trade & Cargo Operations

Cargo in the Shattered Expanse is measured in **Units**. A Unit represents roughly 1 ton of goods (a stack of crates, several large barrels, or a pallet of ingots).

### The Market Fluctuation System
The price of a Unit fluctuates based on the port. To determine the buy/sell price, roll 1d20 on the **Market Fluctuation Table** when players arrive, or have the GM set the modifier based on the narrative.

| 1d20 | Market Condition | Effect on Price | Narrative Example |
| :---: | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **1-3** | **Severe Surplus** | **Halved (x0.5)** | Port just received a massive shipment; goods rotting on docks. |
| **4-8** | **Mild Surplus** | **Reduced (x0.75)** | Common good; local warehouses are full. |
| **9-13** | **Stable** | **Base Value (x1)** | Supply meets demand perfectly. |
| **14-18** | **Shortage** | **Increased (x1.5)** | Trade routes disrupted; locals are worried. |
| **19-20** | **Desperation** | **Doubled (x2)** | Famine, siege, or plague. They will pay anything. |

### Expanded Cargo Table (20 Types)
Below are 20 distinct cargo types spanning four tiers of legality and value.

#### Tier 1: Bulk Goods (Legal everywhere)
| Cargo Type | Base Value | Description |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Scrap Wood/Iron** | 50 gp | Basic materials for ship repair. High demand in active warzones. |
| **Hardtack & Rations** | 100 gp | Standard food supplies. High demand in starving ports. |
| **Fresh Water** | 120 gp | Crucial for long voyages. Desperation pricing in the deep wastes. |
| **Sailcloth & Rigging** | 150 gp | Required for repairing masts and sails. |
| **Salt** | 180 gp | Used for preserving meat and certain arcane rituals. |

#### Tier 2: Refined Goods (Legal, heavily taxed by Consortium)
| Cargo Type | Base Value | Description |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Aether-Coal** | 250 gp | Low-grade fuel for industrial engines. |
| **Spices & Herbs** | 300 gp | Culinary luxuries and medicinal ingredients. |
| **Fine Textiles** | 400 gp | Silks, velvets, and high-quality leathers. |
| **Iron Cannons** | 450 gp | Standard naval artillery. Dominion ports will seize these if untaxed. |
| **Medical Supplies** | 500 gp | Healing kits, bandages, and basic potions. |

#### Tier 3: Luxuries & Contraband (Illegal in some ports, high profit)
| Cargo Type | Base Value | Description |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Drow Silk** | 800 gp | Woven by the deep-weavers. Illegal in Dominion space. |
| **Raw Aether-Crystals** | 1,000 gp | Volatile fuel. Illegal outside of Dominion-sanctioned trade routes. |
| **Abyssal Pearls** | 1,200 gp | Harvested from deep trenches. Prized by the Silk Houses. |
| **Blackpowder** | 1,500 gp | Explosives. Highly restricted by the Consortium. |
| **Spell-Gems** | 2,000 gp | Single-use magical batteries. Heavily regulated by all factions. |

#### Tier 4: Highly Illegal Artifacts (Black Market Only)
| Cargo Type | Base Value | Description |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Precursor Relic-Tech** | 3,000 gp | Intact ancient machinery. The Dominion executes anyone holding this. |
| **Leviathan Ivory** | 4,000 gp | Bone from a Leviathan. The Silk Houses view this as extreme heresy. |
| **Refined Aether-Plasma** | 5,000 gp | Weapons-grade energy. Selling this invites the wrath of the Grand Inquisitor. |
| **Mind-Flayer Brains** | 7,500 gp | Used for horrific alchemical experiments. |
| **First Captain's Blood** | 10,000 gp | A vial of true myth. Priceless to the right buyer, a death sentence if discovered. |

---

## 2. Heat & Notoriety Mechanics

Trading Tier 3 and Tier 4 goods requires accessing a port's **Black Market** (Requires a successful DC 15 Charisma (Investigation) check). However, smuggling comes with risks. 

The ship tracks a **Heat Score** for the three major factions (Dominion, Consortium, Silk Houses). Heat starts at 0 and caps at 100.

- **Selling Contraband:** +1d10 Heat with the faction that outlaws it.
- **Attacking a Faction Ship:** +2d10 Heat.
- **Sinking a Faction Ship:** +4d10 Heat.

### Heat Thresholds
| Heat Score | Consequence |
| :---: | :--- |
| **1-25 (Suspicious)** | Faction patrols are twice as likely to stop and search your ship. |
| **26-50 (Wanted)** | You are denied entry to any port controlled by this faction. Bounties are posted. |
| **51-75 (Hunted)** | The faction actively dispatches Hunter-Killer squadrons (CR 8+) to track you down. |
| **76-100 (Public Enemy)** | A Faction Leader or Dreadnought is deployed specifically to destroy you. Surrender is no longer an option. |

**Reducing Heat:** Heat naturally decays by 1d10 every month. Players can also reduce Heat rapidly by bribing corrupt officials (1,000 gp per 1 Heat reduced) or by completing dangerous loyalty quests for the faction.

---

## 3. The Pirate Base: Upgrades, Retainers, & Upkeep

Players can establish a **Hideout** (a secluded island cove, an abandoned Silk House structure, or a hollowed-out sea stack). Establishing the basic infrastructure costs **5,000 gp**. 

A basic Hideout provides Safe Harbor, a Drydock (repairing Hull points for 10gp each), and a secure Stash.

### Expanding the Hideout (Tier Upgrades)
The Hideout has a Tier level (starts at Tier 1). Upgrading to the next Tier increases the maximum number of Retainers you can hire and the passive benefits the base provides.

- **Upgrade to Tier 2:** Costs 15,000 gp. Takes 1 month. Max Retainers: 3.
- **Upgrade to Tier 3:** Costs 35,000 gp. Takes 2 months. Max Retainers: 6.
- **Upgrade to Tier 4:** Costs 75,000 gp. Takes 3 months. Max Retainers: 10. Can survive a direct assault by a Dreadnought.

### Hiring Retainers
Players can hire highly skilled NPCs to staff their base. Retainers provide powerful mechanical benefits but require a monthly salary. If the salary is not paid, they leave (and might sell your base's location to the Dominion).

| Retainer Type | Monthly Salary | Benefit |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Master Shipwright** | 500 gp | Ship repairs cost 0 gp at the Hideout, requiring only Scrap Wood. |
| **Black Market Fence** | 800 gp | You can buy/sell Contraband directly at the base. Generates 2d100 gp passive income per month. |
| **Aether-Mage** | 1,000 gp | Can identify any magical item. Halves the time and gold cost required to craft magic items at the base. |
| **Information Broker** | 1,200 gp | Automatically reduces Heat with a chosen faction by 1d10 every month. Provides early warning of Faction blockades. |
| **Master Gunner** | 1,500 gp | Upgrades the base's defenses. Grants the Hideout 4 Heavy Cannons (+8 to hit, 4d10 bludgeoning damage) that automatically fire on intruders. |
| **Alchemist** | 1,000 gp | Generates 1d4 random Potions (up to Rare quality) every month. |

### Monthly Upkeep
Running a pirate empire is expensive. At the start of every in-game month, the crew must pay **Upkeep** from their Stash.
- **Base Upkeep:** 500 gp x the Hideout's Tier.
- **Retainer Salaries:** The sum total of all hired retainers.
- **Ship Maintenance:** 100 gp per ship owned.

*If Upkeep is not paid, Retainers desert, the Hideout degrades by 1 Tier, and Crew Morale plummets (see Chapter 32.5).*

---

## 4. The City-Ship Economy (Size 6 Vessels)

When a vessel like *The Sovereign Braid* reaches Size 6, it effectively functions as a mobile Pirate Base. The standard Upkeep rules expand significantly to account for a massive civilian population.

### Population & Taxation
A City-Ship houses a **Population** rather than just a crew. 
- **Taxation:** At the start of every month, the Captain can levy a tax on the population. The city generates **100 gp per 1,000 Citizens**. However, levying taxes temporarily halts passive Morale regeneration.
- **The Food Sink:** Feeding thousands of people on the open ocean is a logistical nightmare. The base monthly Upkeep for a Size 6 vessel is **1,000 gp per 1,000 Citizens**. This represents the cost of bulk rations, fresh water, and aether-fuel.
- **Self-Sustaining Districts:** If the ship has specialized Upgrades (e.g., Hydroponic Gardens, Colossal Fishing Nets), the Food Sink cost is halved. 

### Merchant Districts
If the City-Ship has a **Merchant's Ward** installed, it acts as a mobile free-port. 
- While anchored near a trade route or another port, the Merchant's Ward generates an additional **1d4 x 1,000 gp** per week in passive trade income. 
- You may also permanently hire Retainers directly onto the ship, paying their salaries just as you would for a static Hideout.
# Chapter 32: The Game Master's Toolkit

To truly bring the Shattered Expanse to life, a Game Master must constantly inject unpredictability into the campaign. This chapter provides exhaustive, biome-specific roll tables to generate encounters, dynamic rumors, and bizarre black-market loot on the fly.

---

## 1. Biome-Specific Maritime Encounters
When the crew is sailing, roll 1d20 on the table corresponding to their current environment.

### A. The Shallow Seas (Coastal & Trade Routes)
| 1d20 | Encounter |
| :---: | :--- |
| **1-3** | **Clear Skies:** No encounter. The crew makes good time. |
| **4-5** | **Consortium Convoy:** 3 Merchant Cogs guarded by a Corvette. They are paranoid and heavily armed. |
| **6-7** | **Dominion Customs Check:** An Ironclad Cutter demands to board and inspect cargo. |
| **8-9** | **Rival Pirates:** A Free Captain galleon flies a false flag, attempting to get close before firing. |
| **10** | **Floating Bazaar:** A neutral, multi-ship raft city where players can trade cargo or buy rare items. |
| **11-12** | **Kelp Forest:** The ship enters a massive Sargasso sea. Speed is halved, and 1d4 Sentient Kelp Masses (CR 6) attack. |
| **13-14** | **Reef Graveyard:** Sharp coral. DC 15 Water Vehicles check or take 4d10 hull damage. |
| **15** | **Distress Signal:** A lifeboat with 1d4 starving sailors. (50% chance it is a trap by Abyssal Thralls). |
| **16** | **Merfolk Traders:** Friendly aquatic nomads offering to trade pearls for hardtack. |
| **17** | **The Governor's Yacht:** A lavish, lightly defended ship carrying a corrupt official and a fortune in gold. |
| **18** | **Kraken Hatchling:** A juvenile, but still massive, squid tests its tentacles against the hull. |
| **19** | **Smuggler's Drop:** A buoy marking a sunken chest. Contains 1d4 units of Tier 3 Contraband. |
| **20** | **The Flying Dutchman:** A cursed, legendary ghost ship attacks. It cannot be sunk, only outrun. |

### B. The Deep Abyss (Uncharted & Dangerous)
| 1d20 | Encounter |
| :---: | :--- |
| **1-3** | **Eerie Calm:** The ocean is black as glass. No wind. Silence. |
| **4-5** | **Silk House Ambush:** 2d4 Drow Deep-Weavers board the ship at midnight from beneath the waves. |
| **6-7** | **Ghost Ship:** An abandoned vessel drifting aimlessly. Dinner is still warm on the table. |
| **8-9** | **Bioluminescent Swarm:** Millions of glowing jellyfish. Beautiful, but highly acidic to the hull. |
| **10** | **Precursor Spire:** A metallic obelisk juts from the ocean. Emits a low hum that causes minor headaches. |
| **11-12** | **Whaling Fleet:** Highly skilled hunters chasing a massive beast. They offer a bounty if the players help. |
| **13-14** | **Abyssal Trench:** The water pressure inexplicably shifts. The ship is violently pulled toward a whirlpool. |
| **15** | **Dominion Dreadnought:** A massive warship (CR 15) searching for precursor tech. Run and hide. |
| **16** | **The Deep Mother's Blessing:** The ocean turns black, and all damaged ships are instantly repaired (hulls turn to bone). |
| **17** | **Leviathan Sighting:** A true Leviathan breaches miles away. A tidal wave forces a DC 15 Dex save to avoid 4d10 damage. |
| **18** | **Mind-Flayer Nautiloid:** A crashed alien ship floating on the surface. Contains bizarre tech and psychic horrors. |
| **19** | **Sunken City Rise:** For 1 hour, a block of an ancient city rises above the waves before sinking again. |
| **20** | **Lesser Leviathan Spawn (CR 10):** A direct attack by an apex predator. |

### C. The Aether-Storms (Magical Fallout Zones)
| 1d20 | Encounter |
| :---: | :--- |
| **1-3** | **Green Lightning:** All magic spells cast for the next hour trigger a Wild Magic Surge. |
| **4-5** | **Gravity Inversion:** The ocean water floats upward. The ship is lifted into the air for 1 hour before crashing down. |
| **6-7** | **Blood Rain:** Toxic rain falls. Any creature exposed outside takes 1d4 acid damage per minute. |
| **8-9** | **Time Dilation:** The ship sails for what feels like 10 minutes, but 3 days have passed in the outside world. |
| **10** | **Aether-Rift:** The sky tears open. The ship is instantly teleported 1d100 miles in a random direction. |
| **11-12** | **Crystal Formations:** Jagged Aether-Crystals grow rapidly on the hull. Must be chipped off carefully (DC 15 Dex) or they explode. |
| **13-14** | **Shadow Copies:** The storm creates hostile, shadowy duplicates of the crew that board the ship. |
| **15** | **The Silence:** All sound ceases to exist for 24 hours. Spellcasting with Verbal components is impossible. |
| **16** | **Rogue Aegis Automaton (CR 7):** A precursor machine, activated by the storm, descends from the clouds. |
| **17** | **Memory Fog:** Visibility drops to zero. Crew members must save vs Wisdom or forget the last year of their lives. |
| **18** | **Storm-Lords:** Elementals of pure Aether clash in the sky above. Their stray attacks might hit the ship. |
| **19** | **The Golden Wind:** A miraculously beneficial storm. The ship's speed is tripled and all crew heal to maximum HP. |
| **20** | **The Eye of the Gods:** A massive, unblinking eye opens in the storm clouds and stares directly at the Captain. Gain 1 permanent madness, but learn 1 cosmic secret. |

---

## 2. Dynamic Rumor Generator (1,000 Combinations)
Instead of a static list, roll 1d10 on each of the three columns below to generate a unique rumor heard in a tavern.

| 1d10 | Who (The Subject) | What (The Action) | Where (The Location) |
| :---: | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **1** | The Grand Inquisitor | was secretly assassinated | in the dungeons of Port Vengeance. |
| **2** | A Silk House Matron | discovered a massive gold hoard | beneath a dormant volcano. |
| **3** | The Consortium CEO | is building a super-weapon | at the edge of the map. |
| **4** | A legendary pirate ghost | summoned a Leviathan | inside a floating bazaar. |
| **5** | The Governor | is smuggling Aether-Crystals | in a sunken precursor ruin. |
| **6** | A mutinous crew | awakened a sleeping god | disguised as a merchant cog. |
| **7** | A Mind-Flayer | is trading secrets for blood | at the bottom of an abyssal trench. |
| **8** | The King's bastard son | stole a Dreadnought | right under the navy's nose. |
| **9** | A crazed Aether-Mage | opened a portal to the Abyss | inside the belly of a dead whale. |
| **10** | The First Captains | are returning from the dead | to reclaim their lost armada. |

---

## 3. Modular Black Market Loot (1,000 Combinations)

| :---: | :--- | :--- | :--- |

| **8** | A heavily restricted | ledger of blackmail | written entirely in a dead language. |
| **9** | A surprisingly heavy | parrot (living) | that constantly insults the Dominion. |
| **10** | A still-beating | heart in a jar | that can revive one dead creature, once. |
