# Chapter 33: The Living World

To surpass standard roleplaying campaigns, the Shattered Expanse must feel alive. The world does not wait for the players to act. The factions are actively fighting a war, the crew has their own psychological limits, and the depths of the ocean hold infinitely changing horrors.

This chapter provides three advanced GM frameworks: **The Faction Turn**, **Crew Morale**, and **Procedural Precursor Ruins**.

---

## 1. The Faction Turn

The **Faction Turn** occurs between game sessions (or at the start of every in-game month). The GM resolves this turn to simulate the active war between the Crimson Dominion, the Silk Houses, and the Consortium.

### Faction Assets & Action Points
Each major faction possesses a pool of **Action Points (AP)** equal to 1d4 + 2 each turn. The GM spends these points to take actions that actively alter the game world. 

| Faction Action | AP Cost | Effect |
| :--- | :---: | :--- |
| **Establish Blockade** | 2 | The faction blockades a specific port. All cargo buy/sell prices there are Doubled, but entry requires sneaking past a CR 10 fleet. |
| **Bounty / Inquisition** | 1 | The faction targets a specific NPC or the Players. Bounty hunters (CR 6+) are dispatched to their current location. |
| **Technological Leap** | 3 | The faction upgrades their fleet. For the next month, all their ships have +2 AC and deal +1d10 damage on broadsides. |
| **Covert Sabotage** | 1 | The faction damages a rival's infrastructure. One randomly chosen port loses its Shipyard or Black Market for a month. |
| **Major Offensive** | 4 | The faction launches an all-out assault on a rival territory. Resolves via a contested 1d20 roll between the factions. The winner claims the port. |

By using the Faction Turn, the GM ensures the map is constantly shifting. A port the players relied on for cheap Aether-Coal might suddenly be occupied by the Dominion, forcing the players to adapt.

---

## 2. Crew Morale & Mutiny

The crew of a pirate ship is a powder keg. Instead of treating them as mindless robots, the GM tracks a ship-wide **Morale Score**, ranging from 1 to 10 (starting at 5).

### Modifying Morale
The Captain's actions directly impact the crew's psychology.

**Morale Increases (+1) when:**
- The crew is paid a bonus (at least 100 gp per crew member).
- The ship wins a major naval battle without taking Hull damage.
- The Captain successfully defies or embarrasses a major faction.

**Morale Decreases (-1) when:**
- The crew's Monthly Upkeep is not paid.
- The ship retreats from a battle.
- Over 25% of the crew is killed in a single encounter.
- The ship spends more than a week in the Aether-Storms or Deep Abyss.

### Morale Effects Table
| Morale Score | Crew State | Mechanical Effect |
| :---: | :--- | :--- |
| **9 - 10** | **Fanatical** | The ship gains +2 to Initiative and all Cannon attacks have Advantage. |
| **7 - 8** | **Confident** | The ship gains +1 to Initiative. |
| **4 - 6** | **Neutral** | No effect. |
| **2 - 3** | **Disgruntled** | The ship suffers -2 to Initiative. Actions in naval combat take twice as long. |
| **1** | **Mutinous** | At the start of the next day, the crew attempts a Mutiny. |

### The Mutiny Event
If Morale drops to 1, the Quartermaster and Bosun lead an armed uprising. The players must either fight their own crew (using Swarm mechanics) or the Captain must make a highly contested DC 20 Charisma (Intimidation or Persuasion) check to talk them down. If the players lose, they are marooned on the nearest uncharted island, and their ship is stolen.

---

## 3. Procedural Precursor Ruins

The Shattered Expanse is littered with the sunken husks of an ancient, hyper-advanced civilization. These are not standard dungeons. GMs can generate an infinite number of these "Aether-Ruins" on the fly using the modular system below.

### Step 1: The Ruin's Function (1d6)
Determine what the crashed spacecraft was originally used for. This dictates the visual theme.
1. **Dreadnought:** Heavily armored, filled with inactive weapon silos.
2. **Medical Frigate:** Sterile white hallways, terrifying biological experiments in stasis.
3. **Terraformer:** Overgrown with bioluminescent, alien flora.
4. **Prison Barge:** Cell blocks holding mummified things that should not exist.
5. **Aether-Refinery:** Highly volatile. Green plasma arcs across the corridors.
6. **Command Nexus:** Filled with holographic maps of galaxies that no longer exist.

### Step 2: The Anomaly (1d6)
Precursor ruins are broken. The laws of physics do not apply normally inside them.
1. **Zero Gravity:** Players have a fly speed equal to their walking speed, but all melee attacks are made with disadvantage unless anchored.
2. **Time Loop:** Every 10 minutes, the dungeon resets. Doors close, traps re-arm, but enemies retain memory of the players.
3. **Flesh-Blight:** The metal walls are covered in breathing, organic meat that tries to absorb the players.
4. **Absolute Darkness:** Magical light only illuminates half its normal radius. Non-magical light is instantly snuffed out.
5. **Magnetic Fluctuations:** Anyone wearing heavy metal armor has their movement speed halved.
6. **Silent Protocol:** No sound can be produced in the ruin. Spellcasting requiring Verbal components is impossible.

### Step 3: The Guardian (1d6)
Something survived the crash, or something moved in after.
1. **Rogue Aegis Automaton (CR 7):** Still following its original security programming.
2. **Aether-Lich (CR 12):** An undead precursor captain fueled by raw green energy.
3. **Abyssal Hive:** 4d6 Abyssal Thralls have turned the engine room into a nesting ground.
4. **The Mimic-Ship:** The ruin is not a ship. The entire structure is a colossal Mimic (CR 15).
5. **Holographic AI:** A mad computer that controls the ship's internal defense turrets and blast doors.
6. **First Captain Revenant (CR 10):** A legendary pirate who died trying to loot this place, now cursed to guard it.

# Chapter 34: Rogues' Gallery

The following NPCs are the major power players of the Shattered Expanse. They can serve as endgame adversaries, uneasy allies, or powerful patrons. Their statistics are built using the Pathfinder 1st Edition ruleset, designed to challenge high-level parties.

---

## High Admiral Vane (The Crimson Dominion)
Vane is a towering, heavily scarred human who commands the Crimson Dominion's fleets with absolute, terrifying authority. He wields a massive, aether-charged halberd and is clad in impenetrable hellknight plate.

**High Admiral Vane** (CR 17)
XP 102,400
LE Medium humanoid (human)
Fighter (Dominion Marine) 10 / Hellknight 8
**Init** +6; **Senses** Perception +18
**Aura** fear (10 ft., DC 19)
**DEFENSE**
**AC** 33, touch 13, flat-footed 31 (+14 armor, +2 Dex, +2 deflection, +5 natural)
**hp** 211 (18 HD; 10d10+8d10+108)
**Fort** +19, **Ref** +9, **Will** +11 (+15 vs charm/compulsion)
**Defensive Abilities** Ironclad Advance, Force of Will; **Immune** difficult terrain, fire (120 points/day)
**OFFENSE**
**Speed** 30 ft. (20 ft. in armor)
**Melee** *+3 axiomatic flaming burst heavy halberd* +31/+26/+21/+16 (1d10+16/19-20 x3 plus 1d6 fire and 2d6 vs chaotic)
**Special Attacks** Breach and Clear, Relentless Assault, Smite Chaos 3/day (+4 attack, +8 damage), Hellknight Disciplines (Fearsome, Wrack)
**TACTICS**
**During Combat** Vane uses his halberd to keep enemies at reach. He uses *Smite Chaos* on the most obvious threat (usually a Free Captain or rogue caster) and uses *Fearsome* to demoralize opponents when he strikes them, leveraging his massive Intimidate bonus.
**STATISTICS**
**Str** 24, **Dex** 14, **Con** 20, **Int** 13, **Wis** 12, **Cha** 14
**Base Atk** +18; **CMB** +25; **CMD** 39
**Feats** Combat Reflexes, Critical Focus, Dazzling Display, Improved Critical (halberd), Improved Initiative, Iron Will, Lunge, Power Attack, Shatter Defenses, Stand Still, Toughness, Weapon Focus (halberd), Weapon Specialization (halberd)
**Skills** Intimidate +30, Knowledge (engineering) +18, Perception +18, Sense Motive +18
**Languages** Common, Infernal
**Gear** *+5 hellknight plate*, *+3 axiomatic flaming burst halberd*, *belt of physical might +4 (Str, Con)*, *ring of protection +2*, *amulet of natural armor +3*.

**Gear** *+5 leather armor*, *+2 shocking burst sword cane*, *belt of incredible dexterity +4*, *headband of vast intelligence +4*, an endless bandolier of unstable aether-tech scrap.
# Chapter 35: Bestiary - Faction Forces

This chapter provides standard 5e-compatible statblocks for the foot soldiers, elites, and specialized forces of the five major factions operating in the Shattered Expanse. DMs can use these creatures for boarding actions, port city brawls, and dungeon crawls within faction strongholds.

---

## 1. The Crimson Dominion

The Dominion relies on heavily armored infantry, arcane-suppression, and relentless discipline.

### Dominion Marine
*Medium humanoid (any race), lawful evil*
- **Armor Class:** 16 Chain Mail, touch 11, flat-footed 15
- **Hit Points:** 22 (4d8 + 4)
- **Speed:** 30 ft.
- **Challenge:** 1/2 (100 XP)

**STR:** 15 (+2) | **DEX:** 12 (+1) | **CON:** 13 (+1) | **INT:** 10 (+0) | **WIS:** 11 (+0) | **CHA:** 9 (-1)
- **Base Atk:** +3 | **CMB:** +5 | **CMD:** 16
- **Feats:** Toughness, Power Attack
- **Skills:** Athletics +4, Intimidation +1
- **Senses:** passive Perception 10
- **Languages:** Common, Dominion Standard

**Traits:**
- *Phalanx Formation:* The Marine has advantage on attack rolls and saving throws if at least one ally is within 5 feet of it and isn't incapacitated.

**Actions:**
- *Halberd:* Melee Weapon Attack: +4 to hit, reach 10 ft., one target. *Hit:* 7 (1d10 + 2) slashing damage.
- *Heavy Crossbow:* Ranged Weapon Attack: +3 to hit, range 100/400 ft., one target. *Hit:* 6 (1d10 + 1) piercing damage.

**STR:** 11 (+0) | **DEX:** 12 (+1) | **CON:** 14 (+2) | **INT:** 16 (+3) | **WIS:** 14 (+2) | **CHA:** 12 (+1)
- **Base Atk:** +6 | **CMB:** +6 | **CMD:** 17
- **Feats:** Toughness, Power Attack

**Actions:**
- *Multiattack:* The Inquisitor makes two melee attacks or uses Aether Blast twice.
- *Aether-Iron Rapier:* Melee Weapon Attack: +3 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. *Hit:* 5 (1d8 + 1) piercing damage plus 4 (1d8) force damage.
- *Aether Blast:* Ranged Spell Attack: +5 to hit, range 120 ft., one target. *Hit:* 12 (2d8 + 3) force damage.
- *Suppressing Field (Recharge 5-6):* The Inquisitor projects a 20-foot aura of antimagic for 1 minute. Spells cast within the aura require a DC 13 Constitution saving throw, failing on a botched roll.

# Chapter 35: Introductory Adventure - The Wreck of the Aether-Tide

*The Wreck of the Aether-Tide* is a short, level 1 introductory adventure designed for 4-5 players. It is designed to teach the GM and the players the core mechanics of the Shattered Expanse setting: Naval Combat, Aether-Burn, and the looming faction war.

**Character Advancement:** Characters who complete this adventure should reach level 2.

## Background
A small Grand Merchant Consortium survey ship, the *Gilded Wake*, has run aground on a jagged, uncharted reef. However, this is no ordinary reefâ€”it is a piece of precursor architecture (a "vault") that has suddenly surged from the ocean floor. The *Gilded Wake* is slowly sinking, but not before broadcasting an aether-distress signal. 

The players begin as the newly assembled crew of the *Salty Dog*, a barely seaworthy sloop. They intercept the signal. Unfortunately, so did a Free Captains pirate skiff and a Crimson Dominion patrol.

---

## Part 1: First Blood on the Water (Naval Combat Tutorial)

As the players' ship approaches the coordinates of the distress signal, the fog parts to reveal the jagged, black-metal spire of the precursor vault jutting from the waves. Impaled upon it is the *Gilded Wake*.

Before the players can dock and salvage the wreck, a Free Captains pirate skiff, the *Bloodfin*, intercepts them.

### Encounter: The *Bloodfin* Skiff
The *Bloodfin* is a tiny, fast ship crewed by three pirates who want the salvage for themselves. This is a tutorial for the **Naval Combat (Chapter 27)** rules.

**The Bloodfin (Skiff)**
- **Hull Points:** 30 (Hardness 5)
- **Speed:** 60 ft.
- **Armament:** 1 Light Ballista (3d8 piercing, Range 120 ft.)

**The Pirate Crew (x3)**
- **CR 1/2** (XP 200 each)
- Human Warrior 1
- **hp** 6 each; **AC** 13 (leather armor, +1 Dex)
- **Melee** Cutlass +2 (1d6+1/18-20)
- **Ranged** Light Crossbow +2 (1d8/19-20)

**Tactics:** The GM should use this encounter to teach the players about Ship Roles. Have the players roll initiative for their ship, assign someone to the Helm, someone to the Guns, and someone to Damage Control. The pirates will fire their ballista once, then attempt to ram and board. If two pirates are killed, the remaining pirate surrenders.

---

## Part 2: The Flooded Vault

With the pirates dealt with, the players can tie off their ship and board the precursor vault. The interior is flooded with waist-deep seawater (difficult terrain) and glows with unstable, green aether-light.

### Hazard: The Aether-Coil (Aether-Burn Tutorial)
In the first chamber, a massive, broken precursor engine sparks violently. To progress deeper into the vault and reach the *Gilded Wake's* cargo hold, the players must disable it.
- **Skill Check:** Disable Device or Knowledge (Engineering) DC 15.
- **The Mechanic:** The GM should encourage the player making the check to use the **Aether-Burn** mechanic (Chapter 26). By accepting 1 point of nonlethal Aether-Burn, the player can gain a massive advantage (or reroll a failure).
- **Failure:** If the check fails, the coil discharges, dealing 1d6 electricity damage to everyone in the room (Reflex DC 12 half).

### Looting the Vault
In the adjoining chamber, the players find the corpse of a deep-sea creature and their first piece of Aether-Tech: 
- An **Aether-Pistol** (with 10 charges).
- A **Symbiotic Ship Relic:** *The Drowned Lantern* (taking up the Ring slot). It grants the wearer darkvision, and the ship a 100-foot aura of magical darkness.

---

## Part 3: The Crimson Dominion Arrives (Boss Fight)

As the players reach the cargo hold of the *Gilded Wake* and secure the Consortium's lockbox (which contains a precursor data-drive), they hear the heavy, synchronized stomping of iron boots. 

A Crimson Dominion patrol has boarded the vault from the other side, intent on seizing the precursor technology in the name of the High Admiral.

### Encounter: Dominion Boarding Party
The party consists of two Dominion Legionnaires and their leader, a low-level Dominion Marine.

**Dominion Legionnaires (x2)**
- **CR 1/2** (XP 200 each)
- Human Fighter 1
- **hp** 11 each; **AC** 16 (chain shirt, heavy shield)
- **Melee** Longsword +3 (1d8+2/19-20)

**Sergeant Kael (Dominion Marine)**
- **CR 2** (XP 600)
- Human Fighter (Dominion Marine) 2
- **hp** 22
- **AC** 18 (breastplate, +2 Dex)
- **Melee** Halberd +5 (1d10+4/x3)
- **Special:** *Breach and Clear* (+2 initiative, +2 damage in surprise round/against flat-footed).

**Tactics:** Sergeant Kael orders his men to lock down the players using the cramped, flooded corridors to their advantage. Kael fights to the death, fanatically devoted to the Dominion.

---

## Conclusion & Campaign Hook

Once Kael is defeated, the players can loot his breastplate and halberd, and secure the Consortium data-drive. 

As they return to their ship, the fog lifts, and they see the massive silhouette of a Crimson Dominion dreadnought on the horizon. They must flee into the Unwritten Sea!

**Rewards:**
- The players reach **Level 2**.
- They possess a highly valuable precursor data-drive. They must now decide where to sail:
  - Do they go to **Port Meridian** to sell it back to the Consortium?
  - Do they take it to the **Independent Salvagers** to uncover its secrets?
  - Do they bring it to the **Free Captains** to gain favor with the Pirate King?

The choice is theirs, and the campaign officially begins!
# Chapter 36: Art Gallery & VTT Assets

To bring the Shattered Expanse to life at your table or on a Virtual Tabletop (VTT), we have provided this collection of high-quality, atmospheric illustrations representing the core factions, major NPCs, and central mechanics of the setting. 

Each piece of artwork is accompanied by a **VTT Prompt / Read-Aloud Text** that the GM can use to set the scene for the players.

---

## Phase 1: The Faction Hubs

### The Crimson Citadel (The Crimson Dominion)
![The Crimson Citadel](assets/crimson_citadel_1785873825465.jpg)
> **GM Read-Aloud:** *â€œThe fog parts, revealing a staggering display of military might. Carved directly into the jagged, smoking caldera of an active volcano is the Crimson Citadel. The dark-iron fortress looms over a natural harbor that churns with the wakes of heavy dreadnoughts. At the center, the Obsidian Spire pierces the sky, glowing with an eternal, magical red fire that casts a bloody reflection across the water.â€*

### Port Meridian (Grand Merchant Consortium)
![Port Meridian](assets/port_meridian_1785873833201.jpg)
> **GM Read-Aloud:** *â€œWelcome to the wealthiest city in the known world. Port Meridian is a dizzying, sprawling archipelago of towering marble banks, neon-lit arcane bazaars, and countless toll bridges. The docks are packed bow-to-stern with galleons flying the gold-scale flag. The air smells of exotic spices, ozone, and predatory commerce.â€*

### The Verdant Expanse (The Spirit Keepers)
![The Verdant Expanse](assets/verdant_expanse_1785873841374.jpg)
> **GM Read-Aloud:** *â€œThe open ocean gives way to a massive, magically accelerated kelp forest that stretches across the surface as far as the eye can see. Sunlight filters through the thick, vibrant green aquatic vines, illuminating ancient druidic structures woven seamlessly into the flora. It is deeply serene, but you can feel the predatory pulse of nature watching your ship.â€*

### The Jagged Reefs (Free Captains Alliance)
![The Jagged Reefs](assets/jagged_reefs_1785873849394.jpg)
> **GM Read-Aloud:** *â€œYou navigate the treacherous, shifting sandbars into the heart of the Jagged Reefs. # Chapter 36: Bestiary - Monsters of the Deep

This chapter provides 5e-compatible statblocks for the wild, unaligned horrors of the Shattered Expanse. These creatures represent the environmental dangers of a world warped by unstable magic and deep-sea gigantism.

---

### Drowned Sailor
*Medium undead, unaligned*
- **Armor Class:** 10, touch 10, flat-footed 10
- **Hit Points:** 15 (2d8 + 6)
- **Speed:** 20 ft., swim 30 ft.
- **Challenge:** 1/4 (50 XP)

**STR:** 14 (+2) | **DEX:** 10 (+0) | **CON:** 16 (+3) | **INT:** 3 (-4) | **WIS:** 8 (-1) | **CHA:** 5 (-3)
- **Base Atk:** +1 | **CMB:** +3 | **CMD:** 13
- **Feats:** Toughness, Power Attack
- **Damage Immunities:** poison
- **Condition Immunities:** poisoned

**Traits:**
- *Waterlogged:* The Drowned Sailor has resistance to fire damage but disadvantage on dexterity saving throws.
- *Death Throes (Bilge Vomit):* When the Drowned Sailor drops to 0 hit points, it ruptures, violently expelling putrid, black bilge water. Each creature within 5 feet must succeed on a DC 11 Constitution saving throw or be poisoned until the end of its next turn.

**Actions:**
- *Bloated Slam:* Melee Weapon Attack: +4 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. *Hit:* 5 (1d6 + 2) bludgeoning damage plus 2 (1d4) necrotic damage.

---

### Aether-Burned Scavenger (Shark)
*Large beast, unaligned*
- **Armor Class:** 13 Natural Armor, touch 10, flat-footed 12
- **Hit Points:** 45 (6d10 + 12)
- **Speed:** 0 ft., swim 40 ft.
- **Challenge:** 2 (450 XP)

**STR:** 18 (+4) | **DEX:** 13 (+1) | **CON:** 15 (+2) | **INT:** 2 (-4) | **WIS:** 10 (+0) | **CHA:** 4 (-3)
- **Base Atk:** +4 | **CMB:** +9 | **CMD:** 20
- **Feats:** Toughness, Power Attack
- **Skills:** Perception +2
- **Senses:** blindsight 30 ft., passive Perception 12
- **Damage Immunities:** force

**Traits:**
- *Aether-Blood:* When the Scavenger takes piercing or slashing damage, raw magical energy leaks from the wound. Any creature within 5 feet of the Scavenger takes 3 (1d6) force damage.
- *Blood Frenzy:* The Scavenger has advantage on melee attack rolls against any creature that doesn't have all its hit points.

**Actions:**
- *Bite:* Melee Weapon Attack: +6 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. *Hit:* 13 (2d8 + 4) piercing damage.

---

### Shattered Expanse Siren
*Medium fey, chaotic evil*
- **Armor Class:** 14 Natural Armor, touch 13, flat-footed 11
- **Hit Points:** 52 (8d8 + 16)
- **Speed:** 20 ft., swim 40 ft.
- **Challenge:** 3 (700 XP)

**STR:** 11 (+0) | **DEX:** 16 (+3) | **CON:** 14 (+2) | **INT:** 13 (+1) | **WIS:** 14 (+2) | **CHA:** 18 (+4)
- **Base Atk:** +6 | **CMB:** +6 | **CMD:** 19
- **Feats:** Toughness, Power Attack
- **Skills:** Deception +6, Performance +6
- **Senses:** darkvision 60 ft.

**Traits:**
- *Amphibious:* The siren can breathe air and water.

**Actions:**
- *Multiattack:* The siren uses its Luring Song, then makes one melee attack.
- *Coral Dagger:* Melee Weapon Attack: +5 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. *Hit:* 5 (1d4 + 3) piercing damage plus 7 (2d6) poison damage.
- *Luring Song:* The siren sings a magical melody. Every humanoid within 300 feet of the siren that can hear the song must succeed on a DC 14 Wisdom saving throw or be charmed until the song ends. A charmed creature must use its movement on its turns to get as close to the siren as possible.

---

### Abyssal Anglerfish
*Huge beast, unaligned*
- **Armor Class:** 15 Natural Armor, touch 8, flat-footed 15
- **Hit Points:** 68 (8d12 + 16)
- **Speed:** 0 ft., swim 40 ft.
- **Challenge:** 4 (1,100 XP)

**STR:** 19 (+4) | **DEX:** 10 (+0) | **CON:** 15 (+2) | **INT:** 3 (-4) | **WIS:** 12 (+1) | **CHA:** 6 (-2)
- **Base Atk:** +6 | **CMB:** +12 | **CMD:** 22
- **Feats:** Toughness, Power Attack
- **Senses:** blindsight 60 ft.

**Traits:**
- *Lure of the Deep:* The anglerfish's glowing appendage casts bright light in a 20-foot radius. Creatures that start their turn in the light must succeed on a DC 13 Wisdom saving throw or be magically drawn 10 feet toward the anglerfish.

**Actions:**
- *Bite:* Melee Weapon Attack: +6 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. *Hit:* 22 (4d8 + 4) piercing damage. If the target is a Medium or smaller creature, it is swallowed.

### Coral Golem
*Large construct, unaligned*
- **Armor Class:** 15 Natural Armor, touch 8, flat-footed 16
- **Hit Points:** 102 (12d10 + 36)
- **Speed:** 20 ft., swim 30 ft.
- **Challenge:** 6 (2,300 XP)

**STR:** 19 (+4) | **DEX:** 9 (-1) | **CON:** 16 (+3) | **INT:** 3 (-4) | **WIS:** 10 (+0) | **CHA:** 1 (-5)
- **Base Atk:** +9 | **CMB:** +14 | **CMD:** 23
- **Feats:** Toughness, Power Attack
- **Damage Immunities:** poison, psychic; piercing, and slashing from nonmagical attacks not made with adamantine weapons
- **Condition Immunities:** charmed, exhaustion, frightened, paralyzed, petrified, poisoned

**Traits:**
- *Razor Coral:* A creature that touches the golem or hits it with a melee attack while within 5 feet of it takes 5 (1d10) slashing damage.
- *Immutable Form:* The golem is immune to any spell or effect that would alter its form.
- *Water Absorption:* Whenever the golem is subjected to cold or water damage, it takes no damage and instead regains a number of hit points equal to the damage dealt.

**Actions:**
- *Multiattack:* The golem makes two slam attacks.
- *Slam:* Melee Weapon Attack: +7 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. *Hit:* 13 (2d8 + 4) bludgeoning damage plus 5 (1d10) slashing damage.

- *Slam:* Melee Weapon Attack: +8 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. *Hit:* 14 (2d8 + 5) bludgeoning damage plus 10 (3d6) force damage.
- *Lightning Strike (Recharge 5-6):* The elemental hurls a magical lightning bolt at a target within 120 feet. The target must make a DC 16 Dexterity saving throw, taking 33 (6d10) lightning damage on a failed save, or half on a success.

*Gargantuan monstrosity (Titan), unaligned*

A swallowed creature is blinded and restrained, it has total cover against attacks and other effects outside the Leviathan, and it takes 42 (12d6) acid damage at the start of each of the Leviathan's turns.

**Legendary Actions (3/Turn):**

# Chapter 37: Bestiary - Legendary NPCs

This chapter provides high-level boss statblocks for the most powerful figures in the Shattered Expanse. These entities possess Legendary Actions, Lair Actions, and unique mechanics suited for campaign climaxes.

---

### High Admiral Vane (Crimson Dominion)
*Medium humanoid (human), lawful evil*
- **Armor Class:** 21 (+3 Plate Armor)
- **Hit Points:** 190 (20d8 + 100)
- **Speed:** 30 ft.
- **Challenge:** 18 (20,000 XP)

**STR:** 20 (+5) | **DEX:** 14 (+2) | **CON:** 20 (+5) | **INT:** 18 (+4) | **WIS:** 16 (+3) | **CHA:** 20 (+5)
- **Saving Throws:** Str +11, Con +11, Cha +11
- **Damage Immunities:** force

**Traits:**
- *Legendary Resistance (3/Day):* If Vane fails a saving throw, he can choose to succeed instead.
- *Aether-Core Heart:* Vane is sustained by a piece of raw Aether implanted in his chest. Any spell that targets him specifically has a 25% chance to be absorbed by his heart, healing him for 1d10 HP per level of the spell instead.

**Actions:**
- *Multiattack:* Vane makes three attacks with his *Aether-Blade*.
- *Aether-Blade (Longsword):* Melee Weapon Attack: +13 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. *Hit:* 11 (1d8 + 7) slashing damage plus 14 (4d6) force damage.
- *Iron Dictate (Recharge 5-6):* Vane issues a crushing command. Each enemy within 60 feet must make a DC 19 Charisma saving throw. On a failure, they are stunned until the end of their next turn and drop whatever they are holding.

**Legendary Actions (3/Turn):**
- *Attack:* Vane makes one Aether-Blade attack.
- *Tactical Reposition:* Vane commands an ally he can see to immediately move up to their speed without provoking opportunity attacks.
- *Aether Vent (Costs 2 Actions):* Vane expels magical energy. Every creature within 15 feet must succeed on a DC 19 Dexterity saving throw or take 21 (6d6) force damage and be pushed 10 feet away.

---

### Hurricane Jax (Free Captains Alliance)
*Medium humanoid (half-elf), chaotic neutral*
- **Armor Class:** 19 (Enchanted Duster Coat)
- **Hit Points:** 165 (22d8 + 66)
- **Speed:** 45 ft.
- **Challenge:** 17 (18,000 XP)

**STR:** 14 (+2) | **DEX:** 22 (+6) | **CON:** 16 (+3) | **INT:** 14 (+2) | **WIS:** 14 (+2) | **CHA:** 22 (+6)
- **Saving Throws:** Dex +12, Cha +12
- **Damage Immunities:** lightning, thunder

**Traits:**
- *Legendary Resistance (3/Day).*
- *Storm-Step:* Jax does not trigger attacks of opportunity when he moves out of a hostile creature's reach.
- *Evasion:* If Jax is subjected to an effect that allows a Dexterity saving throw for half damage, he takes no damage on a success, and half damage on a failure.

**Actions:**
- *Multiattack:* Jax makes four attacks with his *Storm-Sabers*.
- *Storm-Saber:* Melee Weapon Attack: +12 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. *Hit:* 10 (1d8 + 6) slashing damage plus 10 (3d6) lightning damage.
- *Lightning Tether (Recharge 4-6):* Jax hurls a lasso of pure electricity at a target within 60 feet. The target must make a DC 20 Dexterity saving throw. On a failure, they take 45 (10d8) lightning damage and are pulled up to 30 feet directly toward Jax.

**Legendary Actions (3/Turn):**
- *Dash:* Jax moves up to his speed.
- *Saber Attack:* Jax makes one Storm-Saber attack.
- *Thunderous Mockery (Costs 2 Actions):* Jax hurls an insult infused with sonic magic. One creature within 60 feet must make a DC 20 Wisdom save. On a fail, they take 16 (3d10) psychic damage and have disadvantage on all attacks until the end of their next turn.

---

### Matron Viconia (Drowned Silk Houses)
*Medium humanoid (drow), neutral evil*
- **Armor Class:** 17 (Flesh-crafted Armor)
- **Hit Points:** 153 (18d8 + 72)
- **Speed:** 30 ft.
- **Challenge:** 16 (15,000 XP)

**STR:** 10 (+0) | **DEX:** 16 (+3) | **CON:** 18 (+4) | **INT:** 20 (+5) | **WIS:** 18 (+4) | **CHA:** 16 (+3)
- **Saving Throws:** Con +9, Int +10, Wis +9

**Traits:**
- *Legendary Resistance (3/Day).*
- *Master Flesh-Crafter:* Any undead or monstrosity allied with Viconia within 60 feet has advantage on all attack rolls and saving throws.

**Actions:**
- *Multiattack:* Viconia casts a spell, then uses *Warp Flesh*.
- *Warp Flesh:* Ranged Spell Attack: +10 to hit, range 60 ft., one target. *Hit:* 27 (6d8) necrotic damage. If the target is a creature, it must succeed on a DC 18 Constitution save or have one of its limbs twisted, suffering disadvantage on attack rolls and ability checks for 1 minute.
- *Spellcasting:* Viconia casts one of the following spells (save DC 18, +10 to hit):
  - At will: *ray of sickness, chill touch*
  - 3/day each: *blight, animate dead (cast as an action)*
  - 1/day each: *finger of death, cloudkill*

**Legendary Actions (3/Turn):**
- *Cast Cantrip:* Viconia casts a cantrip.
- *Warp Flesh:* Viconia uses her Warp Flesh attack.
- *Command Thralls (Costs 2 Actions):* All allied undead and monstrosities within 60 feet can immediately use their reaction to move up to their speed and make one melee attack.

---

### High Elder Naia (Spirit Keepers)
*Medium elemental, neutral*
- **Armor Class:** 16 (Barkskin)
- **Hit Points:** 170 (20d8 + 80)
- **Speed:** 30 ft., swim 60 ft.
- **Challenge:** 16 (15,000 XP)

**STR:** 14 (+2) | **DEX:** 16 (+3) | **CON:** 18 (+4) | **INT:** 16 (+3) | **WIS:** 22 (+6) | **CHA:** 18 (+4)
- **Saving Throws:** Con +9, Wis +11
- **Damage Resistances:** bludgeoning, piercing, and slashing from nonmagical attacks

**Traits:**
- *Legendary Resistance (3/Day).*
- *One with the Expanse:* Naia constantly projects a 30-foot aura. The area is considered difficult terrain for enemies, and allies within the aura regenerate 10 hit points at the start of their turns.

**Actions:**
- *Multiattack:* Naia makes two attacks with *Tidal Surge*.
- *Tidal Surge:* Ranged Spell Attack: +11 to hit, range 120 ft., one target. *Hit:* 22 (4d10) bludgeoning damage.
- *Nature's Wrath (Recharge 5-6):* Naia causes massive coral spikes to erupt from the ground/deck in a 30-foot radius around her. Each enemy must make a DC 19 Dexterity saving throw, taking 42 (12d6) piercing damage on a failure, or half as much on a success.

**Legendary Actions (3/Turn):**
- *Tidal Surge:* Naia uses her Tidal Surge attack.
- *Symbiotic Healing:* Naia touches one creature. They heal for 20 hit points.
- *Elemental Shift (Costs 2 Actions):* Naia instantly teleports to any body of water or living plant within 120 feet.

---

### The Vance Brothers (Grand Merchant Consortium)
*Medium humanoids (human), lawful neutral*
- **Armor Class:** 18 (Enchanted Suits)
- **Hit Points:** 135 each (18d8 + 54)
- **Speed:** 30 ft.
- **Challenge:** 15 (13,000 XP) - *Fought together as a pair*

**STR:** 12 (+1) | **DEX:** 14 (+2) | **CON:** 16 (+3) | **INT:** 20 (+5) | **WIS:** 16 (+3) | **CHA:** 18 (+4)
- **Saving Throws:** Int +10, Cha +9

**Traits:**
- *Legendary Resistance (3/Day) - Shared between them.*
- *Twin Abjuration:* As long as both brothers are within 30 feet of each other, they both have resistance to all magical damage.
- *Shared Lifeblood:* If one brother is reduced to 0 hit points, the other brother instantly enters a rage, gaining advantage on all attacks and dealing double damage.

**Actions (Each Brother rolls initiative separately):**
- *Multiattack:* The brother makes two attacks with their *Golden Canes*.
- *Golden Cane:* Melee Weapon Attack: +7 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. *Hit:* 6 (1d8 + 2) bludgeoning damage plus 14 (4d6) psychic damage.
- *Hostile Takeover (Recharge 5-6):* The brother targets one creature within 60 feet. The target must succeed on a DC 18 Charisma saving throw. On a failure, the brother steals one of the target's prepared spells (level 5 or lower) or a magic item they are wearing (it teleports to the brother's hand).

**Legendary Actions (3/Turn shared between the brothers):**
- *Cane Strike:* One brother makes a Golden Cane attack.
- *Bribe (Costs 2 Actions):* One brother throws a handful of cursed gold at a creature within 30 feet. The creature must succeed on a DC 18 Wisdom saving throw or be charmed by the brothers until the end of its next turn.

---

### Sergeant Kael (Ally / Defector)
*Medium humanoid (human), neutral good*
- **Armor Class:** 18 (Scavenged Dominion Plate)
- **Hit Points:** 112 (15d8 + 45)
- **Speed:** 30 ft.
- **Challenge:** 9 (5,000 XP)

**STR:** 18 (+4) | **DEX:** 14 (+2) | **CON:** 16 (+3) | **INT:** 12 (+1) | **WIS:** 14 (+2) | **CHA:** 10 (+0)
- **Skills:** Athletics +8, Survival +6

**Traits:**
- *Veteran Instincts:* Kael has advantage on initiative rolls and cannot be surprised.
- *Protect the Crew:* When an ally within 5 feet of Kael is hit by an attack, Kael can use his reaction to swap places with the ally, taking the damage instead.

**Actions:**
- *Multiattack:* Kael makes three attacks with his *Boarding Axe* or *Heavy Repeating Crossbow*.
- *Boarding Axe:* Melee Weapon Attack: +8 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. *Hit:* 9 (1d10 + 4) slashing damage.
- *Heavy Repeating Crossbow:* Ranged Weapon Attack: +6 to hit, range 100/400 ft., one target. *Hit:* 7 (1d10 + 2) piercing damage.
- *Grapeshot Grenade (3/Day):* Kael throws an explosive 30 feet. Each creature in a 15-foot radius must make a DC 15 Dexterity save, taking 21 (6d6) fire damage on a failure, or half on a success.
# Chapter 37: Naval Loot & Ship Capture Tables

When a Wayfinder Corsair crew successfully captures an enemy vessel, the Game Master can use the following tables to generate the contents of the cargo hold and the captain's private coffers. 

To use these tables, first determine the **Class of the Captured Ship**, which dictates how many rolls the GM makes on each table.

## 1. Ship Class Loot Multipliers

| Ship Class | Examples | Bulk Cargo Rolls | Munitions Rolls | Contraband Rolls | Captain's Coffer Rolls |
| :--- | :--- | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: |
| **Light** | Skiff, Sloop, Interceptor | 1 | 1 | 0 | 1 |
| **Medium** | Cutter, Corsair, Brig | 2 | 2 | 1 | 1 |
| **Heavy Cargo** | Merchant Galleon, Cog | 5 | 1 | 2 | 2 |
| **Warship** | Heavy Cruiser, Frigate | 2 | 4 | 1 | 2 |
| **Flagship** | Ironclad, Dreadnought | 3 | 5 | 3 | 3 |

---

## 2. Table A: Bulk Cargo & Trade Goods (d100)
*These goods are bulky and require a ship's cargo hold to transport. Values listed are total market fence value for the lot.*

| d100 | Cargo Contents | Fence Value |
| :---: | :--- | :--- |
| **01-15** | 1d4 Weeks of hardtack, salted fish, and stale water | 10 GP |
| **16-30** | 10 Barrels of cheap Harbor Rum and cheap ale | 50 GP |
| **31-45** | 5 Crates of raw iron ore, sail canvas, and hemp rope | 100 GP |
| **46-60** | 20 Sacks of exotic spices (saffron, sea-salt, deep-kelp) | 250 GP |
| **61-75** | 10 Crates of imported Myrish Silk and fine textiles | 500 GP |
| **76-85** | 5 Sealed barrels of medicinal herbs and alchemical base | 750 GP |
| **86-95** | 1d4 Blocks of refined Platinum or Gold Trade Bars | 1,000 GP |
| **96-99** | A captive exotic beast (e.g., juvenile Sea Drake or Griffon) | 1,500 GP |
| **100** | A fully intact, disassembled smaller vessel (e.g., a racing sloop) | 2,500 GP |

---

## 3. Table B: Armaments & Munitions (d100)
*Military supplies used to supply the crew or reload the ship's siege engines.*

| d100 | Munitions Contents | Utility / Value |
| :---: | :--- | :--- |
| **01-15** | Rusted, low-quality cutlasses and boarding pikes | 20 GP (Scrap) |
| **16-30** | 20 Standard Ballista Bolts and 5 Barrels of Black Powder | Resupply Fleet |
| **31-45** | 10 Intact Flintlock Pistols and Musket ammunition | 250 GP |
| **46-60** | 1d4 Masterwork Boarding Weapons (Axes, Cutlasses) | 150 GP each |
| **61-75** | 1d4 Crates of Aether-Slag (Unrefined precursor material) | Ship Crafting |
| **76-85** | 10 Heavy Siege-Breaker Mortar Shells (Explosive) | 500 GP / Ammo |
| **86-95** | 1d4 Suits of intact Masterwork Naval Plate Armor | 300 GP each |
| **96-99** | A sealed crate of 1d4 Experimental Aether-Rifles | 1,000 GP each |
| **100** | A boxed, unassembled Precursor Naval Cannon | Fleet Upgrade |

---

## 4. Table C: High-Value Contraband & Relics (d100)
*Rare, highly illegal, or magical items typically hidden beneath the floorboards.*

| d100 | Contraband Contents | Utility / Value |
| :---: | :--- | :--- |
| **01-15** | A sealed ledger of blackmail on a local governor | Leverage |
| **16-30** | 1d4 Intact Precursor Power Cells | 50 GP each |
| **31-45** | 1d4 Vials of Concentrated Aquatic Elixir | Healing/Water Breathing |
| **46-60** | A glowing, unmapped Ley-Line Chart (Grants +2 Navigation) | Quest Item |
| **61-75** | Minor Wondrous Item (e.g., Bag of Holding, Feather Token) | Magical |
| **76-85** | 1d4 Crystalline Blueprint Slates | Crafting Lore |
| **86-95** | A Medium Wondrous Item (e.g., Ring of Protection +2) | Magical |
| **96-99** | A dormant, damaged Precursor Guardian Construct | Base Defense |
| **100** | A Major Artifact (e.g., A Violet Aether-Core or First Captain Relic) | Campaign Altering |

---

## 5. Table D: The Captain's Coffer (d100)
*The personal wealth stored in the captain's quarters.*

| d100 | Coffer Contents | Value |
| :---: | :--- | :--- |
| **01-15** | Loose silver and copper in a lockbox | 1d10 x 10 GP |
| **16-30** | A velvet pouch of Dominion minted gold | 1d6 x 50 GP |
| **31-45** | A heavy iron chest of platinum trade tokens | 1d6 x 100 GP |
| **46-60** | A collection of fine jewelry and pearls | 1d4 x 250 GP |
| **61-75** | The Ship's Payroll Chest (Massive influx of gold) | 1d6 x 500 GP |
| **76-85** | A Masterwork, gold-inlaid spyglass and navigation compass | 750 GP |
| **86-95** | 1d4 Gemstones (Rubies, Emeralds, Sapphires) | 1,000 GP each |
| **96-99** | The deed to a safehouse or tavern in a major port | Real Estate |
| **100** | A King's Ransom: A chest overflowing with flawless diamonds | 5,000 GP |
# Chapter 38: Magic Items of the Expanse

The Shattered Expanse is a world where raw magic bleeds into the oceans. The magic items here reflect the brutal, pragmatic, and highly specialized nature of the factions that forge them.

---

## 1. Dominion Aether-Tech
Forged by the Crimson Dominion, these items utilize raw, unstable Aether crystals to produce powerful, albeit dangerous, martial effects.

### Aether-Core Grenade
*Wondrous item, uncommon*
- **Caster Level (CL):** 5th
- **Aura:** Moderate magic
- **Market Price:** 5,000 gp
- **Construction Requirements:** Craft Wondrous Item
*Encased in heavy, rune-stamped iron, this fist-sized sphere houses a violently vibrating shard of raw blue Aether. The Dominion issues these to boarding parties, where the volatile blast can clear a deck in seconds.*
- As an action, you can throw the grenade up to 60 feet. It explodes on impact. Each creature within a 15-foot radius must make a DC 13 Dexterity saving throw, taking 14 (4d6) force damage on a failed save, or half as much on a successful one.

### Inquisitor's Lens
*Wondrous item, uncommon*
- **Caster Level (CL):** 5th
- **Aura:** Moderate magic
- **Market Price:** 5,000 gp
- **Construction Requirements:** Craft Wondrous Item
*A thick glass monocle set in a tarnished brass and iron frame. Used by the Crimson Inquisitors to root out unsanctioned magic users, the lens seems to catch and bend the ambient light of the Expanse.*
- While wearing it, you have advantage on Intelligence (Investigation) checks to find hidden magical traps, glyphs, or illusions.
- You can cast *Detect Magic* at will, without requiring any components.

### Aether-Charged Cutlass
*Weapon (longsword or scimitar), rare *
- **Caster Level (CL):** 5th
- **Aura:** Moderate magic
- **Market Price:** 5,000 gp
- **Construction Requirements:** Craft Wondrous Item
*This heavy iron blade hums with contained energy. The fuller of the blade contains a thin, glowing blue line of liquid Aether that flares when drawn.*
- You gain a +1 bonus to attack and damage rolls made with this magic weapon.
- **Overload:** As a bonus action, you can strike the hilt to overload the Aether core for 1 minute. During this time, the weapon deals an extra 1d6 force damage on a hit. However, if you roll a 1 on an attack roll while overloaded, the weapon vents raw magic, dealing 1d6 force damage to you.

### Grappling Gauntlet
*Wondrous item, rare *
- **Caster Level (CL):** 5th
- **Aura:** Moderate magic
- **Market Price:** 5,000 gp
- **Construction Requirements:** Craft Wondrous Item
*A heavy mechanical bracer fitted with a spool of high-tensile mythril wire and a jagged, barbed spike. It smells of grease and ozone.*
- As an action, you can fire the spike at a solid surface or creature within 60 feet (using your Dexterity modifier + proficiency bonus for the attack roll). 
- If you hit a surface, you can use a bonus action to instantly winch yourself to that point. 
- If you hit a creature (Large or smaller), it takes 1d6 piercing damage and is grappled (escape DC 15). You can use a bonus action to winch the creature 15 feet closer to you, or winch yourself 15 feet closer to it.

### Inquisitor's Manacles
*Wondrous item, rare*
- **Caster Level (CL):** 5th
- **Aura:** Moderate magic
- **Market Price:** 5,000 gp
- **Construction Requirements:** Craft Wondrous Item
*Heavy iron shackles deeply etched with anti-magic runes. When locked around a prisoner's wrists, the metal feels ice-cold, actively draining the magical spark from their blood.*
- A creature bound by these manacles cannot cast spells that require somatic or material components.
- The creature has disadvantage on Constitution saving throws made to maintain concentration on a spell.
- Escaping requires a successful DC 20 Dexterity (Sleight of Hand) or DC 20 Strength (Athletics) check.

### Ironclad Breaching Ram
*Weapon (maul), rare *
- **Caster Level (CL):** 5th
- **Aura:** Moderate magic
- **Market Price:** 5,000 gp
- **Construction Requirements:** Craft Wondrous Item
*A massive, piston-driven hammer designed to shatter ship hulls and fortification doors. The head of the maul is a solid block of Dominion steel powered by an Aether combustion engine.*
- You gain a +1 bonus to attack and damage rolls. 
- **Siege Weapon:** This weapon deals double damage to objects and structures.
- **Earthshatter:** Once per day, you can strike the ground to cast *Earthquake* with a radius of only 15 feet, centered on the point of impact.

### Aether-Thruster Boots
*Wondrous item, rare *
- **Caster Level (CL):** 5th
- **Aura:** Moderate magic
- **Market Price:** 5,000 gp
- **Construction Requirements:** Craft Wondrous Item
*Heavy, iron-shod boots equipped with glowing aether-exhaust ports on the heels and soles. They are loud, clunky, and highly effective for ship-to-ship boarding.*
- You can use a bonus action to activate the boots, granting yourself a flying speed of 30 feet until the end of your turn. 
- If you do not land on a solid surface at the end of your turn, you fall.

### Dominion Shield-Generator
*Wondrous item, very rare*
- **Caster Level (CL):** 5th
- **Aura:** Moderate magic
- **Market Price:** 5,000 gp
- **Construction Requirements:** Craft Wondrous Item
*A heavy metal tripod topped with a spinning, multifaceted aether crystal. When activated, it emits a low hum that vibrates in the teeth of anyone nearby.*
- As an action, you can deploy the tripod on a solid surface. It projects a 15-foot-radius dome of shimmering blue force for 1 minute.
- The dome blocks line of effect and provides total cover to anyone inside it against attacks originating from outside.
- The dome has 100 hit points and is immune to non-magical physical damage. Once used, it cannot be used again until the next dawn.

### Vane's Iron Decree
*Wondrous item, legendary *
- **Caster Level (CL):** 5th
- **Aura:** Moderate magic
- **Market Price:** 5,000 gp
- **Construction Requirements:** Craft Wondrous Item
*A heavy iron speaking trumpet used by high-ranking admirals of the Dominion fleet. The metal is etched with runes of absolute authority.*
- While holding it, your voice can be amplified to be heard clearly up to 1 mile away. 
- **Mass Command:** Once per day, you can shout a one-word command through the trumpet. This acts as a 5th-level *Command* spell (Save DC 17), affecting up to 6 creatures of your choice within 120 feet.

---

## 2. Drow & Keeper Biomancy
Created through flesh-warping or druidic symbiosis, these items are living organisms that merge with the user.

### Blood-Leech Bandage
*Wondrous item, common*
- **Caster Level (CL):** 5th
- **Aura:** Moderate magic
- **Market Price:** 5,000 gp
- **Construction Requirements:** Craft Wondrous Item
*A large, pale, sterile-looking slug kept in a jar of brine. When placed on a wound, it rapidly spins a silk-like webbing to stitch flesh together while consuming tainted blood.*
- As an action, you can apply the slug to a wound. You can expend one of your own Hit Dice. Roll the die, add your Constitution modifier, and you regain that many hit points. The slug then dies, turning to dust.

### Symbiotic Gills
*Wondrous item, uncommon *
- **Caster Level (CL):** 5th
- **Aura:** Moderate magic
- **Market Price:** 5,000 gp
- **Construction Requirements:** Craft Wondrous Item
*A pulsating, fleshy collar resembling a shark's gills. To attune to this item, you must press it against your neck, where it painfully burrows into your flesh and fuses with your windpipe.*
- You can breathe underwater indefinitely.
- You gain a swimming speed equal to your walking speed.
- **Curse of the Deep:** If you remain out of water for more than 24 hours, you begin to suffocate and gain one level of exhaustion every hour until submerged.

### Kelp-Strand Whip
*Weapon (whip), uncommon *
- **Caster Level (CL):** 5th
- **Aura:** Moderate magic
- **Market Price:** 5,000 gp
- **Construction Requirements:** Craft Wondrous Item
*A living, slimy vine harvested from the deepest trenches. It writhes slightly when held, as if seeking prey to constrict.*
- You gain a +1 bonus to attack and damage rolls made with this magic weapon. 
- **Constrict:** When you hit a creature with this weapon, you can attempt to grapple them (escape DC 13) instead of dealing damage. You cannot attack with the whip while grappling a creature in this way.

### Reef-Walker's Ring
*Ring, uncommon *
- **Caster Level (CL):** 5th
- **Aura:** Moderate magic
- **Market Price:** 5,000 gp
- **Construction Requirements:** Craft Wondrous Item
*A ring made of living, vibrant pink coral. It stays warm to the touch and occasionally shifts its shape around your finger.*
- You ignore difficult terrain caused by aquatic environments (such as deep mud, sharp coral, or thick seaweed).
- You have advantage on saving throws against being moved or knocked prone while underwater or on a shifting ship deck.

### Coral-Weave Armor
*Armor (scale mail), rare *
- **Caster Level (CL):** 5th
- **Aura:** Moderate magic
- **Market Price:** 5,000 gp
- **Construction Requirements:** Craft Wondrous Item
*Living, bioluminescent coral grown meticulously over a flexible mesh of woven kelp. The armor breathes with the wearer, hardening upon impact.*
- You have a +1 bonus to AC. 
- **Living Defense:** As a reaction when hit by a melee attack, you can cause razor-sharp coral to sprout outward, dealing 1d10 piercing damage to the attacker. Once used, it cannot be used again until the next dawn, as the coral requires time to regrow.

### The Eye of the Deep
*Wondrous item, rare *
- **Caster Level (CL):** 5th
- **Aura:** Moderate magic
- **Market Price:** 5,000 gp
- **Construction Requirements:** Craft Wondrous Item
*A preserved, oversized eye of a deep-sea leviathan, mounted on a bone chain. The pupil constantly dilates and contracts, tracking movement in the dark.*
- You gain darkvision out to a range of 120 feet. If you already have darkvision, its range increases by 60 feet.
- You can see through magical darkness as if it were dim light.

### Chitinous Tower Shield
*Armor (shield), rare *
- **Caster Level (CL):** 5th
- **Aura:** Moderate magic
- **Market Price:** 5,000 gp
- **Construction Requirements:** Craft Wondrous Item
*A massive, jagged shield made from the hollowed carapace of a gargantuan deep-sea crab. It is surprisingly light despite its immense size.*
- While holding this shield, you have a +3 bonus to AC instead of the normal +2. 
- **Carapace Plating:** As a reaction when you take bludgeoning, piercing, or slashing damage, you can reduce the damage by 1d10 + your Constitution modifier.

### Matron's Flesh-Borer
*Weapon (dagger), very rare *
- **Caster Level (CL):** 5th
- **Aura:** Moderate magic
- **Market Price:** 5,000 gp
- **Construction Requirements:** Craft Wondrous Item
*A grotesque, writhing bone knife that seems to be made of a single, petrified tooth. The hilt is wrapped in drow spider-silk.*
- You gain a +2 bonus to attack and damage rolls. 
- **Flesh-Warp:** When you hit a creature with this dagger, they must succeed on a DC 16 Constitution saving throw or have their Constitution score reduced by 1d4. The target dies if this reduces its Constitution to 0. The reduction lasts until the target finishes a long rest.

### Staff of the Deep Mother
*Weapon (quarterstaff), legendary *
- **Caster Level (CL):** 5th
- **Aura:** Moderate magic
- **Market Price:** 5,000 gp
- **Construction Requirements:** Craft Wondrous Item
*A staff formed of black, weeping coral, topped with a glowing, bioluminescent lure resembling an anglerfish. It constantly drips saltwater.*
- You gain a +2 bonus to attack and damage rolls made with this magic weapon, and a +2 bonus to spell attack rolls and your spell save DC.
- **Summon the Deep:** Once per week, you can cast *Conjure Elemental* to summon a Water Elemental, or cast a modified *Conjure Animals* to summon a CR 5 or lower aquatic beast/monstrosity. The creature obeys you perfectly and remains for 1 hour without requiring concentration.

---

## 3. Consortium Luxury
The Grand Merchant Consortium produces items designed to protect wealth, ensure safe travel, and project opulence.

### Coin of Safe Passage
*Wondrous item, common*
- **Caster Level (CL):** 5th
- **Aura:** Moderate magic
- **Market Price:** 5,000 gp
- **Construction Requirements:** Craft Wondrous Item
*A beautifully minted gold coin, slightly heavier than a standard piece. The Consortium gives these to their most valued VIPs.*
- If you snap the coin in half (requiring an action), it emits a silent magical beacon. Any Consortium ship within 50 miles is instantly aware of the beacon's exact location and will move to retrieve you. Note: the Consortium will charge an exorbitant "rescue fee" once you are aboard.

### Silk-Weave Cloak
*Wondrous item, uncommon *
- **Caster Level (CL):** 5th
- **Aura:** Moderate magic
- **Market Price:** 5,000 gp
- **Construction Requirements:** Craft Wondrous Item
*An incredibly stylish, water-resistant cloak woven from abyssal spider-silk. It shifts colors subtly to match the current high-fashion trends of the floating cities.*
- You can cast *Mage Armor* on yourself at will. 
- You have advantage on Charisma (Persuasion) checks made against nobility, merchants, or politicians.

### Vault-Lock Box
*Wondrous item, rare*
- **Caster Level (CL):** 5th
- **Aura:** Moderate magic
- **Market Price:** 5,000 gp
- **Construction Requirements:** Craft Wondrous Item
*A small, heavily reinforced iron and mythril lockbox containing 1 cubic foot of space. It has no keyhole, only a smooth ruby set into the lid.*
- To open or close the box, a specific individual (chosen when the box is enchanted) must press a drop of their blood to the ruby. 
- The box is completely immune to the *Knock* spell, lockpicks, and all mundane attempts to force it open.

### Ledger-Mage's Quill
*Wondrous item, rare*
- **Caster Level (CL):** 5th
- **Aura:** Moderate magic
- **Market Price:** 5,000 gp
- **Construction Requirements:** Craft Wondrous Item
*A golden quill that never runs out of ink and never snaps its nib. The feather belonged to a celestial bird of trade.*
- It grants you advantage on Intelligence (Investigation) checks to detect forgeries or altered documents. 
- You can use the quill to cast *Illusory Script* at will, without requiring material components.

### Abjuration Signet Ring
*Ring, rare *
- **Caster Level (CL):** 5th
- **Aura:** Moderate magic
- **Market Price:** 5,000 gp
- **Construction Requirements:** Craft Wondrous Item
*A heavy gold ring bearing the crest of a Merchant Lord. It hums with latent, protective magic designed to stop assassins.*
- This ring has 3 charges. It regains 1d3 expended charges daily at dawn.
- As a reaction when you are hit by an attack, you can expend 1 charge to cast *Shield*. 
- As an action, you can expend 2 charges to cast *Counterspell* (3rd level).

### Golden Contract
*Wondrous item, very rare*
- **Caster Level (CL):** 5th
- **Aura:** Moderate magic
- **Market Price:** 5,000 gp
- **Construction Requirements:** Craft Wondrous Item
*A glowing piece of enchanted parchment with a pre-written, highly dense legal agreement. The ink is mixed with crushed diamond dust.*
- If two or more willing creatures sign their true names to a bargain written on this parchment, they are subjected to a 9th-level *Geas* spell to fulfill the terms of the bargain. Only a *Wish* spell can break the contract before the terms are met.

### The Dividend Shield
*Armor (shield), legendary *
- **Caster Level (CL):** 5th
- **Aura:** Moderate magic
- **Market Price:** 5,000 gp
- **Construction Requirements:** Craft Wondrous Item
*A shield made of solid, enchanted gold. Despite its material, it is perfectly balanced and practically indestructible.*
- You gain a +2 bonus to AC in addition to the shield's normal bonus. 
- **Compound Interest:** When you are hit by a melee attack, you can use your reaction to absorb the kinetic energy. You reduce the damage by 1d10 + your Dexterity modifier. You then gain temporary hit points equal to the damage reduced. You can use this ability 3 times per day.

### Ledger of Infinite Coin
*Wondrous item, legendary *
- **Caster Level (CL):** 5th
- **Aura:** Moderate magic
- **Market Price:** 5,000 gp
- **Construction Requirements:** Craft Wondrous Item
*A heavy, gold-leafed book that feels slightly warm to the touch. The pages are blank, but smell of freshly minted currency.*
- Once per day at dawn, the book produces 100 gold pieces that spill endlessly from its pages.
- **The Catch:** The gold is magically marked. Any Consortium agent or ledger-mage can instantly track the exact location of any coin produced by this book, no matter what plane it is on.

---

## 4. Pirate Gear
Dirty, reliable, and deeply illegal. The Free Captains forge items for boarding actions, mutinies, and quick escapes.

### Rust-Eaten Mutineer's Dagger
*Weapon (dagger), uncommon*
- **Caster Level (CL):** 5th
- **Aura:** Moderate magic
- **Market Price:** 5,000 gp
- **Construction Requirements:** Craft Wondrous Item
*A rusted, barnacle-encrusted blade with a handle wrapped in flayed skin. It perpetually drips a foul-smelling black ichor, yearning for the blood of a friend.*
- You gain a +1 bonus to attack and damage rolls made with this magic weapon. 
- **Betrayal's Rot:** If you make an attack against a creature that considers you an ally, the attack automatically becomes a critical hit if it connects, and the wound immediately rots, dealing an extra 2d6 necrotic damage.

### Smuggler's Pocket
*Wondrous item, uncommon*
- **Caster Level (CL):** 5th
- **Aura:** Moderate magic
- **Market Price:** 5,000 gp
- **Construction Requirements:** Craft Wondrous Item
*A mundane-looking patch of dark leather that can be hastily sewn into the inside of a coat or trousers.*
- It functions exactly like a *Bag of Holding*, but is completely undetectable by visual inspection unless the coat is thoroughly searched (Investigation DC 16).

### Tricorne of the Zephyr
*Wondrous item, uncommon *
- **Caster Level (CL):** 5th
- **Aura:** Moderate magic
- **Market Price:** 5,000 gp
- **Construction Requirements:** Craft Wondrous Item
*A faded leather pirate hat with a brightly colored, magical parrot feather tucked into the brim. It always smells of sea salt and fresh wind.*
- While wearing this hat, you have advantage on initiative rolls.
- You can cast *Gust of Wind* once per day, regaining the ability to do so at dawn, allowing you to quickly fill your ship's sails in a dead calm.

### Compass of the Drowned
*Wondrous item, rare*
- **Caster Level (CL):** 5th
- **Aura:** Moderate magic
- **Market Price:** 5,000 gp
- **Construction Requirements:** Craft Wondrous Item
*A rusted brass compass containing murky seawater instead of glass. The needle is carved from human bone.*
- The needle does not point North. Instead, it points to the nearest location where a creature has died within the last 24 hours.
- If no creature has died within 10 miles in the last 24 hours, the needle spins aimlessly.

### Boarding-Axe of the Zephyr
*Weapon (handaxe), rare *
- **Caster Level (CL):** 5th
- **Aura:** Moderate magic
- **Market Price:** 5,000 gp
- **Construction Requirements:** Craft Wondrous Item
*A perfectly balanced throwing axe with a wickedly curved blade. The handle is wrapped in storm-cloud-grey linen.*
- You gain a +1 bonus to attack and damage rolls made with this magic weapon. 
- **Boomerang Strike:** When you throw the axe, it immediately returns to your hand after the attack resolves. If the attack hits, it deals an extra 1d6 thunder damage.

### False-Flag Pennant
*Wondrous item, rare *
- **Caster Level (CL):** 5th
- **Aura:** Moderate magic
- **Market Price:** 5,000 gp
- **Construction Requirements:** Craft Wondrous Item
*A magical flag, torn and stained, that must be flown from the highest mast of a vessel.*
- As an action, the captain can speak a command word to cast a massive, ship-wide *Mirage Arcane* (or *Seeming*). This illusion can alter the ship's colors, flag, and overall silhouette to look like any other mundane ship of the same size class. The illusion lasts for 8 hours.

### Powder-Mage's Flintlock
*Weapon (pistol), rare *
- **Caster Level (CL):** 5th
- **Aura:** Moderate magic
- **Market Price:** 5,000 gp
- **Construction Requirements:** Craft Wondrous Item
*A masterfully crafted, heavily scarred flintlock pistol. The barrel glows cherry-red when fired.*
- You gain a +1 bonus to attack and damage rolls made with this magic weapon.
- This weapon does not require black powder or physical ammunition. It magically conjures its own explosive force, dealing force damage instead of piercing.
- **Dragon's Breath:** Once per short rest, instead of making a normal attack, you can fire a blast of fire in a 15-foot cone. Each creature in that area must make a DC 14 Dexterity saving throw, taking 3d6 fire damage on a failed save, or half as much on a successful one.
# Chapter 39: Cursed Artifacts & Relics

Not all treasure in the Shattered Expanse is worth taking. Many ancient ruins and deep-sea trenches hold relics of immense power that have been corrupted by the Leviathans, the unstable Aether of the deep, or the sheer greed of dead pirate lords. 

DMs are encouraged to use these artifacts as major plot devices. They offer characters massive mechanical benefits but carry debilitating, often horrific, narrative and mechanical costs.

---

### Lantern of the Sunken Souls
*Wondrous item, rare *
- **Caster Level (CL):** 5th
- **Aura:** Moderate magic
- **Market Price:** 5,000 gp
- **Construction Requirements:** Craft Wondrous Item
*A barnacle-encrusted brass lantern that burns with a cold, pale-blue light. It smells faintly of rotting kelp and cold grave dirt. The light it casts casts long, unnatural shadows that seem to move on their own.*
- **The Boon:** The lantern casts bright light for 30 feet and dim light for an additional 30 feet. While holding it, you have *Truesight* out to 30 feet, allowing you to see invisible creatures and peer through illusions.
- **The Curse (The Beacon):** The light of the lantern acts as a beacon to the dead. Every hour the lantern is lit outside of a magically warded area, there is a cumulative 10% chance that a hostile undead encounter (e.g., Specters, Wraiths, or a Ghost Ship) is drawn to your exact location. They will prioritize targeting the lantern bearer above all others.

### The Drowned Captain's Log
*Wondrous item, rare *
- **Caster Level (CL):** 5th
- **Aura:** Moderate magic
- **Market Price:** 5,000 gp
- **Construction Requirements:** Craft Wondrous Item
*A water-logged, bloated leather journal that never dries out. The ink inside glows a faint, sickly green. The pages contain the frantic scrawlings of captains who sought legendary treasures, only to find their graves.*
- **The Boon:** The book knows the location of every sunken ship in the Shattered Expanse. By spending 1 hour reading the log, you can name a specific lost ship or ask for the nearest wreck. The log will magically sketch a highly accurate map to the wreck's location.
- **The Curse (The Drowned Crew):** Every time you use the book to locate a wreck, you must make a DC 16 Wisdom saving throw. On a failure, you are haunted by horrific visions of drowning in absolute darkness. You suffer one level of exhaustion and gain an extreme phobia of deep water. While on a ship out of sight of land, you have disadvantage on all attack rolls and ability checks.

### Spyglass of Paranoia
*Wondrous item, very rare *
- **Caster Level (CL):** 5th
- **Aura:** Moderate magic
- **Market Price:** 5,000 gp
- **Construction Requirements:** Craft Wondrous Item
*A collapsible brass spyglass, heavily tarnished. The glass lens looks eerily like a bloodshot human eye. When you peer through it, you feel a cold breath on the back of your neck.*
- **The Boon:** You can use the spyglass to look at any location within 10 miles that you are familiar with, functioning as the *Scrying* spell.
- **The Curse (The Watching Eye):** You become intensely, cripplingly paranoid. You constantly see shadows moving in the corners of your vision and hear whispers of mutiny from your crew. You suffer disadvantage on all saving throws against the frightened condition, and you can no longer gain the benefits of a short or long rest if another creature is within 10 feet of you.

### The Blood-Drinker Cutlass
*Weapon (longsword), very rare *
- **Caster Level (CL):** 5th
- **Aura:** Moderate magic
- **Market Price:** 5,000 gp
- **Construction Requirements:** Craft Wondrous Item
*A jagged, crimson-stained blade that hums with sentient, malevolent hunger. The hilt is wrapped in human skin, and the blade is warm to the touch. It desperately wants to feed.*
- **The Boon:** You gain a +2 bonus to attack and damage rolls. When you hit a creature with this sword, you heal hit points equal to half the damage dealt.
- **The Curse (Starving Blade):** The sword demands blood. If you go 24 hours without killing a creature of CR 1 or higher with this weapon, the sword begins feeding on you. Your hit point maximum is reduced by 1d10 each dawn. This reduction cannot be reversed until the sword kills a creature.

### The Black Spot Doubloon
*Wondrous item, legendary *
- **Caster Level (CL):** 5th
- **Aura:** Moderate magic
- **Market Price:** 5,000 gp
- **Construction Requirements:** Craft Wondrous Item
*A heavy, impossibly cold coin minted from obsidian. It bears the jagged emblem of a forgotten pirate king on one side, and a screaming skull on the other.*
- **The Boon:** While this coin is on your person, you cannot be killed by mundane means. If an attack or effect would reduce you to 0 hit points, you drop to 1 hit point instead. You can use this ability a number of times equal to your Constitution modifier (minimum 1). You regain all expended uses at midnight.
- **The Curse (The Black Spot):** Attuning to the coin permanently sears a black, necrotic mark onto your palm. This curse cannot be removed by any spell short of a *Wish*. Every time you use the coin's boon to survive dropping to 0 HP, a powerful, hostile undead creature from the Expanse is immediately drawn to your exact location to claim your soul. If you ever willingly give the coin away or lose it, you immediately suffer 10d10 necrotic damage, and the Black Spot begins to rot your flesh, giving you disadvantage on all Charisma checks until you reclaim the coin or die.

### The Aether-Core Heart
*Wondrous item (implant), legendary *
- **Caster Level (CL):** 5th
- **Aura:** Moderate magic
- **Market Price:** 5,000 gp
- **Construction Requirements:** Craft Wondrous Item
*A fist-sized crystal of pure, glowing red Aether, encased in a crude mechanical pump. It was designed by a mad Dominion scientist to replace a failing biological heart with endless magical energy.*
- **The Boon:** To attune to this item, it must be surgically implanted into your chest, replacing your actual heart (a process requiring a DC 20 Medicine check and 8 hours of surgery). Once implanted, your Constitution score becomes 22. You become immune to the exhausted and poisoned conditions, and you no longer need to eat, sleep, or breathe.
- **The Curse (Meltdown):** The raw magic inside your chest is highly volatile. Every time you cast a spell of 1st level or higher, or take more than 20 points of damage in a single hit, the core surges. Roll a d20. On a 1, the core vents raw magic. You and every creature within 10 feet of you take 4d10 force damage. If you die while the core is implanted, it detonates in a catastrophic explosion. Every creature and object within a 60-foot radius takes 10d10 force damage and 10d10 fire damage.

### Ring of the Leviathan's Bargain
*Ring, legendary *
- **Caster Level (CL):** 5th
- **Aura:** Moderate magic
- **Market Price:** 5,000 gp
- **Construction Requirements:** Craft Wondrous Item
*A massive, heavy ring made of blackened gold, depicting a kraken dragging a galleon down into the abyss. It whispers promises of ultimate power in the dark.*
- **The Boon:** You can cast the *Wish* spell once. 
- **The Curse (The Debt Collector):** Immediately after you cast *Wish*, the ring dissolves into seawater and vanishes. Within 1d4 days, an ancient Leviathan (or Kraken) is summoned within 1 mile of your location. The beast has only one goal: to utterly destroy you and everyone you love. It magically knows your exact location at all times, no matter where you flee, until it is slain.

### The Crimson Anchor
*Weapon (maul), legendary *
- **Caster Level (CL):** 5th
- **Aura:** Moderate magic
- **Market Price:** 5,000 gp
- **Construction Requirements:** Craft Wondrous Item
*A massive, two-handed ship's anchor that drips with fresh blood, regardless of how long it has been out of the water. It requires immense strength just to lift, let alone swing.*
- **The Boon:** You gain a +3 bonus to attack and damage rolls. When you hit a creature, they must succeed on a DC 18 Strength saving throw or be knocked prone and paralyzed until the end of their next turn, crushed by the phantom weight of the anchor.
- **The Curse (Dead Weight):** Your physical weight increases by 500 pounds. Your walking speed is reduced by 10 feet. If you ever fall into water, you sink like a stone at a rate of 60 feet per round, and you automatically fail all Athletics checks to swim. You cannot be buoyant.

### Crown of the Deep Mother
*Wondrous item, artifact *
- **Caster Level (CL):** 5th
- **Aura:** Moderate magic
- **Market Price:** 5,000 gp
- **Construction Requirements:** Craft Wondrous Item
*A crown formed of interlaced, razor-sharp black coral. It constantly drips saltwater and smells of the deepest, lightless ocean trenches.*
- **The Boon:** While wearing the crown, you gain telepathic control over the lesser monsters of the deep. You can cast *Dominate Monster* (DC 18 Wisdom save) at will, but it only affects beasts or monstrosities with a swim speed. You also gain resistance to cold damage and a swim speed of 60 feet.
- **The Curse (Deep-Sea Symbiosis):** The crown is alive. Upon attunement, it fuses with your skull (taking 4d10 piercing damage). It cannot be removed without killing the wearer. Over the course of 1d4 weeks, the wearer begins to mutate. First, their skin turns pale and clammy (disadvantage on Charisma checks). Second, they grow gills and lose the ability to breathe air. Finally, they begin hearing the whispers of the Deep Mother. Every time they finish a long rest, they must make a DC 15 Wisdom saving throw. On a failure, their alignment shifts one step closer to Chaotic Evil, and they become obsessed with returning to the abyss.

### Sextant of the Void
*Wondrous item, artifact *
- **Caster Level (CL):** 5th
- **Aura:** Moderate magic
- **Market Price:** 5,000 gp
- **Construction Requirements:** Craft Wondrous Item
*An impossibly complex navigational tool made of dark, unearthly metal that feels cold to the touch. The lenses are ground from meteorites.*
- **The Boon:** When navigating a ship using this sextant, the ship can never be lost. It can perfectly navigate through magical storms, impenetrable fog, and spatial anomalies. Additionally, once per week, you can use the sextant to cast *Teleport* on the entire ship and its crew, moving it to any ocean location in the world instantly.
- **The Curse (Gaze of the Abyss):** When looking through the sextant, you look directly into the terrifying vastness of the Void. Every time you use the item, you must succeed on a DC 18 Intelligence saving throw. On a failure, you suffer short-term madness for 1d10 minutes. If you fail this saving throw three times total over your lifetime, you gain a permanent, incurable long-term madness.

### The Gorgon's Figurehead
*Wondrous item, artifact *
- **Caster Level (CL):** 5th
- **Aura:** Moderate magic
- **Market Price:** 5,000 gp
- **Construction Requirements:** Craft Wondrous Item
*A beautifully carved, serpentine figurehead of a medusa-like woman with glowing, venomous green eyes. It must be mounted on the prow of a ship to function.*
- **The Boon:** When mounted to the prow of a ship, the captain can use an action to unleash the Gorgon's Gaze. Every creature and enemy vessel within a 120-foot cone must make a DC 18 Constitution saving throw. On a failure, creatures are petrified, and enemy ships instantly have their rigging, sails, and hulls turn to solid stone, rendering them immobile and causing them to rapidly sink.
- **The Curse (The Heavy Toll):** The terrible magic bleeds backward into your own ship. Every time you use the Gorgon's Gaze, 5% of your own ship's hull permanently turns to stone. This reduces your ship's maximum speed by 5 feet each time it is used. If the ship's speed reaches 0, the entire ship fully petrifies and sinks like a stone, taking the captain and crew with it. The petrification of the hull cannot be reversed by any means.

### The Drowned King's Crown
*Wondrous item, artifact *
- **Caster Level (CL):** 5th
- **Aura:** Moderate magic
- **Market Price:** 5,000 gp
- **Construction Requirements:** Craft Wondrous Item
*An ancient, rusted iron crown set with massive pearls. It constantly leaks seawater down the wearer's face, making them look perpetually sorrowful.*
- **The Boon:** You exude an aura of terrifying, regal majesty. Any creature that serves on your crew is magically bound to your will. They cannot mutiny, they cannot lie to you, and they have advantage on saving throws against being charmed or frightened by anyone other than you. You can cast *Geas* at will on any member of your crew without components.
- **The Curse (Bound to the Sea):** The crown permanently binds you to the ocean, the only domain you are now allowed to rule. You can never step foot on dry land again. If you step onto land (or an island, continent, or non-floating city), you take 10d10 necrotic damage every minute you remain there. Your body will rapidly begin to dry out and turn to dust if you leave the water.
# Chapter 40: Plunder & Cargo Tables

When the smoke clears and the enemy ship begins to sink, the victor takes the spoils. Use these d100 tables to quickly generate randomized cargo, loot, and contraband whenever players successfully board and salvage an enemy vessel.

To determine what a ship is carrying, roll 1d4 times on Table A, 1d2 times on Table B, and 1 time on Table C.

---

## Table A: Generic Cargo Hold
This table represents the standard trade goods, supplies, and mundane cargo found in the main holds of most merchant and faction vessels.

| d100 | Cargo Discovered | Value |
| :---: | :--- | :--- |
| 01-10 | **Spoiled Rations:** Barrels of hardtack and salted pork that have been tainted by seawater. | 0 gp |
| 11-20 | **Iron-Wood Timber:** 10 bundles of hardened timber used for repairing ships. | 50 gp |
| 21-30 | **Cheap Rum:** 5 large barrels of harsh, burning pirate liquor. | 25 gp |
| 31-40 | **Whale Oil:** 20 sealed jars of highly flammable blubber oil. | 100 gp |
| 41-50 | **Basic Munitions:** 50 iron cannonballs and 3 kegs of standard black powder. | 150 gp |
| 51-60 | **Spices:** A small, locked crate containing rare saffron and cinnamon. | 300 gp |
| 61-70 | **Fine Silks:** 5 bolts of high-quality, water-resistant spider-silk. | 400 gp |
| 71-80 | **Medical Supplies:** Healer's kits, bandages, and a dozen vials of basic antitoxin. | 100 gp |
| 81-90 | **Raw Ore:** Crates filled with unrefined iron and copper ore. | 200 gp |
| 91-99 | **Precious Metals:** A locked strongbox containing silver and gold trade bars. | 800 gp |
| 100 | **The Motherlode:** A perfectly preserved, massive pearl harvested from a Leviathan. | 2,500 gp |

---

## Table B: The Smuggler's Hold
Found hidden beneath false floors or in lead-lined compartments. This represents the highly illegal, dangerous, or heavily taxed goods that Free Captains and Consortium smugglers transport.

| d100 | Contraband Discovered |
| :---: | :--- |
| 01-15 | **Volatile Aether Dust:** 3 pouches of glowing blue powder. Can be snorted for a temporary +2 to spell save DCs, but the user takes 2d6 necrotic damage. Highly illegal in Dominion waters. |
| 16-30 | **Stolen Masterworks:** 1d4 crates of plundered, high-society artwork. Fencing these requires passing a DC 16 Charisma (Deception) check, or drawing the ire of the Consortium. (Value: 500 gp each). |
| 31-45 | **Unstable Aether Core:** A crackling, football-sized energy core. Can be used to power a massive ship weapon once, or sold on the black market for 1,000 gp. Highly explosive. |
| 46-60 | **Drow Slumber-Poison:** 5 vials of a thick purple liquid. A creature hit by a weapon coated in this poison must succeed on a DC 15 Constitution save or fall asleep for 1 hour. |
| 61-75 | **Forged Letters of Marque:** Blank, officially sealed documents that grant the bearer legal permission to act as privateers for the Crimson Dominion. Highly valuable to pirates. |
| 76-90 | **Precursor Tech Fragments:** Rusted, incomprehensible clockwork pieces from a lost civilization. Tinkers and artificers will pay handsomely (800 gp) for them. |
| 91-99 | **A Captured Monster:** A locked iron cage containing a highly aggressive, starved monstrosity (e.g., a young Arachna-Drider or Kelp-Hydra). |
| 100 | **A High-Value Hostage:** A bound and gagged noble or high-ranking faction officer. Ransoming them could yield 5,000 gp, but will undoubtedly start a war. |

---

## Table C: The Captain's Quarters
The personal stash, lockboxes, and secrets kept by the commander of the defeated vessel.

| d100 | Personal Loot |
| :---: | :--- |
| 01-15 | **The Ship's Ledger:** A detailed book containing the routes, codes, and passwords for several faction strongholds. (Grants advantage on navigation and infiltration checks). |
| 16-30 | **A Potion Stash:** 1d4 *Potions of Greater Healing* and 1 *Potion of Water Breathing*. |
| 31-45 | **Captain's Fine Clothes:** An incredibly gaudy, heavily enchanted coat. (*+1 Studded Leather Armor*). |
| 46-60 | **A Treasure Map:** A cryptic, blood-stained map pointing to an uncharted island in the Shattered Expanse where a legendary Pirate Lord buried their loot. |
| 61-75 | **The Magic Compass:** A *Compass of the Drowned* (See Chapter 38). |
| 76-90 | **Aether-Charged Weapon:** The captain's personal sidearm (A *+1 Aether-Charged Cutlass* or *Powder-Mage's Flintlock*). |
| 91-99 | **A Ring of Keys:** A keyring that opens every lock on the ship, plus a strange, glowing bone-key that radiates necromantic magic (Purpose unknown). |
| 100 | **A Cursed Relic:** The captain was hiding a terrifying artifact of immense power. Roll 1d4. (1: *The Black Spot Doubloon*, 2: *Crown of the Deep Mother*, 3: *Aether-Core Heart*, 4: *Drowned Captain's Log*). |
# Chapter 41: Bastion (The Crimson Capital)

*The impenetrable heart of the Crimson Dominion, built atop the jagged, smoking caldera of an active volcano.*

Bastion is not a city of leisure; it is a fortress of industry and war. Black smoke constantly chokes the sky above the island, venting from the massive Aether-forges that power the Dominion's ironclad fleet. The city is tiered, with the lowest levels reserved for laborers and the slave-pits, and the upper echelons housing the High Admiralty and the Inquisition.

### Physical Description
The island itself is sheer, black volcanic rock. There are no natural beaches; ships must enter through heavily fortified sea-gates into an artificial, cavernous harbor carved directly into the caldera. The architecture is brutalistâ€”heavy iron, dark stone, and jagged spikes designed to repel boarding actions even on land.

### Key NPCs
- **High Admiral Vane:** The supreme commander of the Dominion forces. He rarely leaves the High Admiralty Spire unless personally leading a fleet. (See Chapter 37 for statblock).
- **Grand Inquisitor Thorne:** A fanatic who oversees the Aether-Processing facilities and ensures that no unsanctioned magic users exist within Bastion's walls.

### Local Laws & Dangers
- **Absolute Prohibition on Unsanctioned Magic:** Casting a spell in Bastion without an official Dominion writ is a capital offense. Inquisitor patrols use *Inquisitor's Lenses* to constantly scan the crowds.
- **The Ash-Sickness:** The air in the lower tiers is highly toxic. Creatures without a filter-mask or immunity to poison who spend more than 24 hours in the lower city must succeed on a DC 15 Fortitude save or become fatigued.

### Points of Interest

#### 1. The Aether-Forges
Located deep within the volcano's caldera, these massive foundries harness geothermal heat and raw Aether to smelt Dominion steel. It is deafeningly loud and incredibly dangerous. Slaves and political prisoners are worked to death here. *Adventure Hook: The players must infiltrate the forges to sabotage a prototype Dreadnought engine before it can be installed.*

#### 2. The High Admiralty Spire
The tallest structure in Bastion, a jagged needle of black iron piercing the smog. The upper floors contain the strategic war rooms where the Dominion plans its conquest of the Expanse. It is heavily guarded by Aegis Automatons and elite Marines.

#### 3. The Drowned Oubliette
The Dominion's prison is built into the sea-wall. At high tide, the lower cells completely flood. High-value prisoners are kept in suspended cages just above the water line, exposed to the freezing spray and the psychological torture of the rising tides.
# Chapter 42: Shipwreck Cove (The Neutral Haven)

*A sprawling, chaotic, floating shantytown made entirely from the lashed-together hulls of hundreds of sunken and captured ships.*

Shipwreck Cove is the beating heart of the Free Captains Alliance and the only truly "free" port in the Shattered Expanse. It has no fixed location; it drifts on the ocean currents, hidden from the Dominion by powerful, ship-wide illusion magic (similar to the *False-Flag Pennant*). It is a city of vice, freedom, and extreme danger.
