### Physical Description
The city is a three-dimensional maze of rotting wood, canvas sails, and rope bridges. Galleons are stacked atop frigates, creating artificial "hills" of timber. The smell of cheap rum, black powder, and roasting fish is omnipresent. The city constantly groans and sways with the ocean swells.

### Key NPCs
- **Hurricane Jax:** The charismatic and deadly pirate king who acts as the unofficial mayor of the Cove. He mediates disputes between rival pirate crews. (See Chapter 37 for statblock).
- **"One-Eye" Pete:** The proprietor of the Powder-Keg Tavern and the best fence for stolen Consortium goods in the Expanse.

### Local Laws & Dangers
- **The Pirate's Code:** There is only one real lawâ€”do not betray the Cove to the Dominion or the Consortium. Those found guilty of treason are keelhauled beneath the city's massive, barnacle-encrusted underbelly.
- **Structural Instability:** The Cove is constantly shifting. During a fight, there is a 10% chance each round that the floor (a rotting deck or frayed rope bridge) collapses. Creatures must make a DC 15 Reflex save or fall 20 feet into the shark-infested waters below.

### Points of Interest

#### 1. The Powder-Keg Tavern
Built inside the overturned hull of a colossal Consortium Man-o-War, this is the largest tavern in the Expanse. It is a neutral ground where rival crews come to drink, gamble, and recruit. Violence is heavily discouraged here, enforced by Jax's elite bouncers. *Adventure Hook: A map to a legendary cursed artifact is being auctioned off in the back room tonight.*

#### 2. The Black Market Bazaars
A tangled web of rickety stalls selling everything from volatile Aether-dust to illegal biomancy implants and stolen Dominion weapons. If it's illegal anywhere else, it's sold here.

#### 3. The Corsair's Fighting Pits
Located in the flooded hold of a ruined galleon, pirates bet heavily on blood sports. Combatants fight on slippery, floating wooden platforms while captive sea monsters (like Abyssal Anglerfish) circle in the water below.
# Chapter 43: The Silk Chasm (Drow Metropolis)

*A terrifyingly beautiful city built directly into the vertical walls of a lightless, abyssal trench, illuminated only by bioluminescence and magic.*

The Silk Chasm is the ancestral home of the Drowned Silk Houses. Surface dwellers rarely see it and live to tell the tale. It is a three-dimensional metropolis that defies gravity, relying on underwater currents, magical levitation, and giant spider-silk zip-lines for traversal.

### Physical Description
The city is carved into the black rock of the trench walls. Massive, glowing webs woven by giant aquatic spiders span the chasm, acting as both structural support and sensory nets that detect approaching vessels. The architecture is elegant but sinister, favoring sharp spires and organic, fluid curves that mimic deep-sea flora.

### Key NPCs
- **Matron Viconia:** The absolute ruler of the Silk Chasm. A master flesh-crafter who views all non-drow as raw biological material. (See Chapter 37 for statblock).
- **Arch-Mage Pharaun:** The head of the Abyssal magical academy, constantly seeking to bind Leviathan Spawn to the city's defense grid.

### Local Laws & Dangers
- **The Surface Tax:** Any non-drow permitted to enter the city must offer a tribute of fresh blood or living tissue upon entry. Failure to do so results in immediate enslavement in the Flesh-Warping Pits.
- **The Crushing Depths:** The Chasm is located so deep that the water pressure is lethal. Any creature not native to the deep (or lacking magical protection like *Symbiotic Gills*) takes 4d6 bludgeoning damage every minute they are outside a magically pressurized building or submarine.

### Points of Interest

#### 1. The Matron's Spire
A massive stalactite hanging from an overhang in the trench wall, completely encased in glowing purple silk. It houses the ruling council. The interior is a labyrinth of traps and magical wards. *Adventure Hook: The players must infiltrate the Spire to steal a biological key needed to cure a cursed crewmate.*

#### 2. The Flesh-Warping Pits
The industrial heart of the Drow war machine. Here, slaves and captured sea life are magically mutated into Abyssal Thralls and Arachna-Driders. The area smells strongly of ozone and burnt flesh, and the screams echo up the chasm.

#### 3. The Drowned Gardens
A bizarre, beautiful agricultural sector where the Drow cultivate poisonous anemones, hallucinogenic kelp, and bioluminescent fungi. The gardens are tended by blind, mind-controlled thralls and guarded by aggressive Kelp-Hydras.
# Chapter 44: The Golden Flotilla (Consortium Hub)

*Not a static island, but a massive fleet of opulent mega-ships chained together into a moving city of commerce.*

The Grand Merchant Consortium does not trust land. Land can be besieged, blockaded, or destroyed by earthquakes. Instead, their capital is the Golden Flotilla, a collection of dozens of massive dreadnoughts and galleons chained together by bridges of solid gold and iron. It is the richest moving target in the world, heavily guarded by mercenary fleets.

### Physical Description
The Flotilla is blindingly bright during the day, reflecting sunlight off gilded hulls and polished brass cannons. At night, it glows with magical lanterns, looking like a constellation fallen to the sea. The central ships are massive floating palaces, while the outer ring consists of heavily armed escort frigates.

### Key NPCs
- **The Vance Brothers:** A pair of ruthlessly efficient Merchant Lords who oversee the Great Exchange. They finish each other's sentences and share a magical bond. (See Chapter 37 for statblock).
- **Captain Alistair Vance:** The commander of the mercenary defense fleet. He cares only for coin and will turn a blind eye to piracy if the bribe is high enough.

### Local Laws & Dangers
- **The Iron Ledger:** Every transaction, conversation, and breath on the Flotilla is taxed. Failure to pay docking fees or tariffs results in immediate seizure of your vessel.
- **Mercenary Law:** The city is policed by Gold Cloaks. They are well-paid and completely incorruptible by small sums, but will absolutely look the other way for a king's ransom. Any act of violence disrupts trade, which is a capital offense.

### Points of Interest

#### 1. The Great Exchange
The largest ship in the fleet, functioning as a massive auction house. Here, entire islands, cursed artifacts, and mercenary armies are bought and sold. The bidding wars are cutthroat, sometimes literally. *Adventure Hook: The players must enter a high-stakes auction to win back a stolen piece of their ship's core.*

#### 2. The Vault-Ships
Specially designed, heavily armored barges that contain the Consortium's liquid assets. They sit low in the water, practically submarines, and are guarded by Abjuration wards and Ledger-Mages.

#### 3. The Velvet Lounge
A high-end casino on one of the pleasure yachts. High-stakes card games, magical roulette, and political backstabbing are the main attractions. It is neutral ground where rival faction leaders sometimes meet in secret to negotiate.
# Chapter 45: The Coral Citadel (Spirit Keepers Sanctuary)

*A sacred, living reef that breaches the surface of the water, cultivated over centuries by druidic magic.*

The Spirit Keepers do not build; they grow. The Coral Citadel is a massive atoll formed of vibrant, impossibly tall coral formations that reach hundreds of feet into the air. It is a sanctuary for aquatic life and a stronghold against the industrial expansion of the Dominion.

### Physical Description
The Citadel is breathtakingly beautiful. Waterfalls cascade down massive tiers of pink and blue coral. Giant glowing anemones provide light, and the "buildings" are naturally formed hollows within the reef. The air smells of salt, blooming sea-flowers, and ancient magic.

### Key NPCs
- **High Elder Naia:** The spiritual leader of the Keepers, deeply connected to the life-force of the Expanse. (See Chapter 37 for statblock).
- **Kaelen the Reef Warden:** A militant druid who commands the Citadel's defenses and believes the Dominion must be wiped out entirely to save the oceans.

### Local Laws & Dangers
- **The Sacred Edict:** No mechanical engines or Aether-tech are allowed near the Citadel. Any ship powered by an engine that approaches within 5 miles will be attacked by summoned sea life.
- **The Living Defenses:** The Citadel is alive. If the coral is damaged, it bleeds a highly acidic sap. Any creature dealing physical damage to the architecture takes 2d6 acid damage in return.

### Points of Interest

#### 1. The Elder Spawning Pools
Deep, warm pools at the center of the atoll where rare, magical aquatic beasts come to lay their eggs. It is a place of immense restorative magic. Any creature that takes a long rest here removes all exhaustion and curses. *Adventure Hook: The pools have been corrupted by a strange magical blight, and the players must dive deep to find the source.*

#### 2. The Whispering Kelp Forest
A dense underwater forest surrounding the base of the Citadel. The kelp is sentient and stores the memories of the Keepers' ancestors. DMs can use this area to provide lore drops or cryptic prophecies to the players.

#### 3. The Sunken Shrine of the Deep Mother
A terrifying, abyssal drop-off at the edge of the reef. The Keepers leave offerings here to appease the slumbering Leviathans. It is heavily guarded, and non-Keepers are strictly forbidden from approaching.
# Chapter 46: The Sunken Ruins of Aethelgard

*The treacherous, magic-warped ruins of the precursor civilization that caused the Shattered Expanse event.*

Before the world flooded, Aethelgard was a continent-spanning empire built on unchecked magical hubris. Their experiments tore the crust of the earth apart, sinking their cities beneath the waves. Today, these ruins are the most dangerousâ€”and lucrativeâ€”adventure sites in the Expanse.

### Physical Description
The ruins vary from half-submerged spires jutting out of the ocean like broken teeth, to massive, hermetically sealed biodomes resting in the abyssal trenches. The architecture is sleek, white stone mixed with veins of raw Aether crystal. The geometry is often broken, with stairs leading nowhere and gravity behaving erratically.

### Local Dangers
- **Aether Radiation:** The ambient magic here is highly unstable. Every hour spent exploring a major ruin, characters must make a DC 15 Fortitude save. On a failure, they suffer a random magical mutation (e.g., skin turns blue, hair falls out, gain vulnerability to force damage) that lasts until cured by *Greater Restoration*.
- **The Silent Guardians:** Aethelgard was defended by advanced constructs (use statblocks for Golems or Aegis Automatons) that are still active, relentlessly following millennium-old patrol routes.

### Points of Interest

#### 1. Unstable Aether Vents
Geysers of raw magical energy that erupt from the sea floor near the ruins. They deal massive force damage but are surrounded by highly valuable, crystallized Aether shards. Harvesting them is incredibly dangerous. *Adventure Hook: A Consortium merchant has hired the crew to harvest shards during an active eruption.*

#### 2. Leviathan Breeding Grounds
The lingering magical radiation of the ruins attracts Leviathan Spawn. The massive beasts use the hollowed-out skyscrapers of the old world as lairs and nests. Exploring these ruins often means playing a deadly game of cat-and-mouse with a monstrosity.

#### 3. The Trapped Vaults
Aethelgard's greatest treasures (and most terrible cursed artifacts) are locked in sealed vaults. These vaults are protected by complex magical puzzles, anti-magic fields, and lethal traps. Opening one often requires finding key-runes scattered across the Expanse.
# Chapter 47: Running a Campaign in the Expanse

Running a game in the Shattered Expanse is fundamentally different from a standard high-fantasy dungeon crawl. The environment is actively hostile, the sea is an ever-present danger, and the factions are locked in a ruthless cold war. This chapter provides guidance for Dungeon Masters to set the tone and structure their campaign.

## Session Zero: Setting Expectations
Before the dice roll, DMs must establish the tone of the campaign with the players. The Shattered Expanse is a world of nautical horror, magic-punk industry, and piracy.

- **Morality:** The Expanse has very few purely "good" factions. Are the players acting as ruthless pirates out for themselves, honorable corsairs fighting the oppressive Dominion, or greedy Consortium mercenaries?
- **Naval Mechanics:** Ensure the players understand that they will be managing a ship. They must agree on a chain of command (who is the Captain, the Quartermaster, the Helmsman?) to avoid arguing during naval combat.
- **Lethality:** The sea is unforgiving. If a ship sinks in the deep ocean, it is often a total party kill (TPK). Players must understand that fleeing from a superior Dreadnought is often the only smart option.

## The Party's Origin: Starting Hooks
Why are the players on a ship together? Choose or roll on the following table to establish the party's starting bond:

| d6 | Origin Hook |
| :---: | :--- |
| **1** | **Mutineers:** The players were all press-ganged into serving on a Dominion frigate. They staged a bloody mutiny, took the ship, and are now on the run from Inquisitors. |
| **2** | **Consortium Debtors:** The players owe a massive, unpayable debt to the Vance Brothers. They have been given a junker ship and told to run highly illegal, dangerous errands until the debt is paid. |
| **3** | **Treasure Hunters:** A dying pirate gave the party half of a map to a sunken Aethelgard ruin. They pooled their money to buy a skiff and are setting out to strike it rich. |
| **4** | **Shipwreck Survivors:** The party's previous ship was destroyed by a Leviathan. They are the only survivors, washing up on the docks of Shipwreck Cove with nothing but the clothes on their backs. |
| **5** | **Keeper Envoys:** The party was hired (or commanded) by High Elder Naia to map the shifting magical currents and report back on Dominion fleet movements. |
| **6** | **The Inheritors:** One of the players inherited a heavily modified, but cursed, ship from a famous Free Captain relative. The ship comes with loyal crewmen and a lot of enemies. |

## Structuring the Campaign
A naval campaign usually follows a "Hub and Spoke" model. 
1. **The Hub:** The players start at a safe port (like Shipwreck Cove or the Golden Flotilla) where they rest, upgrade their ship, and gather rumors.
2. **The Voyage:** The travel is part of the adventure. Roll for random weather (storms, dead calms) and random encounters (Dominion patrols, sea monsters).
3. **The Spoke (The Location):** The players arrive at their destination (a precursor ruin, an enemy ship, or a faction island), complete the objective, and load up the plunder.
4. **The Return:** Surviving the journey back to port with a hull full of heavy, valuable loot is often the hardest part of the quest.
# Chapter 48: The Aether-Smuggler's Run (Levels 1-3)

*An introductory adventure designed to teach players the basics of Naval Combat, ship management, and the ruthless politics of the Expanse.*

## The Premise
The players begin in the chaotic shantytown of **Shipwreck Cove**. They are flat broke, but they possess a small, dilapidated sloop (Size 1: The Bramble Skiff). A desperate, sweaty Consortium merchant named **Silas Vance** approaches them in the Powder-Keg Tavern with a highly illegal job.

### The Job
Silas has acquired an **Unstable Aether Core** stolen from a Dominion forge. It is highly volatile and highly illegal. He needs it transported immediately to a buyer waiting on a small, uncharted atoll called **Razor-Reef**, a two-day sail away. He promises 500 gp upon delivery.

## Part 1: The Voyage and The Patrol
The players set sail. The DM should use this time to let players test their ship roles (rolling Navigation to plot a course, having the Quartermaster manage rations).

**The Encounter:** On the second day, a **Crimson Dominion Patrol Skiff** (Size 1) spots them. 
- The Dominion ship will fire a warning shot and demand to board and inspect the cargo.
- If the players allow the boarding, a **Dominion Marine** and an **Inquisitor** (See Chapter 35) will search the hold. They will find the core unless the players pass a DC 15 Bluff or Stealth check.
- If combat breaks out, the DM runs a tutorial Naval Combat encounter. The Dominion Skiff has 50 Hull Points and two basic cannons. The goal is to either sink the patrol or damage their sails to escape.

## Part 2: Razor-Reef
The players arrive at the atoll. It is desolate, surrounded by sharp coral. The buyer's ship, a sleek black schooner, is anchored in the lagoon. 

**The Betrayal:** The buyer is not a merchant; it's a **Consortium Repo-Man** (See Chapter 35) named **Kargen**. Silas owed the Consortium money, and Kargen was sent to take the Aether Core and kill the smugglers to tie up loose ends.
- Kargen demands the core and refuses to pay. 
- If the players fight, Kargen signals his crew. 
- **The Fight:** The players must fight Kargen (CR 1) and two **Corsair Thugs** (CR 1/8) on the sandy beach of the atoll. 

## Part 3: The Volatile Core
During the fight (perhaps on Initiative count 0 of the second round), the wooden crate holding the Aether Core is damaged. The core begins to glow bright red and emit a high-pitched whine. 

- The players have 3 rounds to secure the core before it explodes. 
- A player adjacent to the core can use a standard action to make a DC 15 Knowledge (arcana) or Sleight of Hand check to magically stabilize or physically realign the crystal matrix.
- If it explodes, it deals 3d6 force damage to everything in a 20-foot radius (DC 15 Reflex save for half). 

## Conclusion and Rewards
If the players survive and defeat Kargen, they can loot his body (finding a *Smuggler's Pocket* and 100 gp). If they saved the core, they can keep it. An Unstable Aether Core can be sold at a black market (like Shipwreck Cove) for 1,000 gp, or kept to power a future ship upgrade. 

The players have officially made an enemy of the Grand Merchant Consortium, but they have their first real taste of plunder. They should reach Level 2 (or 3, if milestones are used).
# Chapter 49: The Sunken Vault of Aethelgard (Levels 4-6)

*A deep-sea dungeon crawl that challenges the players with environmental hazards, magical puzzles, and ruthless competitors.*

## The Premise
The players come into possession of a *Drowned Captain's Log* or a treasure map that points to an uncharted coordinate in the deepest part of the ocean. The map promises a sealed vault belonging to the precursor civilization of Aethelgard. 

To reach it, the players must either acquire a submarine, cast *Water Breathing* on the entire party, or procure a magical diving bell.

## The Dungeon: Aethelgard Bio-Spire Sigma
The ruin is a massive, cracked glass-and-white-stone biodome resting on the ocean floor in utter darkness. It was once a precursor research facility. 

### Environmental Hazards
- **Aether Radiation:** The facility is leaking. Every 2 hours spent inside, players must make a DC 15 Fortitude save or suffer a random minor mutation (e.g., hair turns to kelp, eyes glow in the dark).
- **Gravity Inversions:** Certain hallways have broken gravity plating. Walking into these zones causes players to "fall" upward to the ceiling, taking 2d6 bludgeoning damage. Movement in these areas is treated as difficult terrain unless the player has a fly speed.

## Key Encounters

### Zone 1: The Pressurized Antechamber
The outer doors are breached, but the inner doors are magically sealed. The doors are guarded by a **Coral Golem** (CR 6, Chapter 36) that has grown over the millennia. The Golem will attack anything that does not present an Aethelgard insignia.

### Zone 2: The Cryo-Genesis Labs
Rows of shattered glass tubes. The water here is murky and cold. Two **Aether-Burned Scavengers** (CR 2, Chapter 36) have made this room their nest. If the players search the debris (DC 15 Perception), they find an *Inquisitor's Lens* and a rusted Aethelgard keycard.

### Zone 3: The Aether-Vault & The Ambush
The final door is a massive vault locked by a complex arcane puzzle. However, the players are not alone. A raiding party of **Drow** from the Silk Houses tracked the players' ship.
- As the players attempt to solve the puzzle, **Matron Viconia's** lieutenant, a **Deep Weaver** (CR 5) and two **Abyssal Thralls** (CR 3), ambush the party. 
- The Drow want the vault's contents for flesh-warping experiments. The players must fight a pitched battle in a zero-gravity, flooded chamber.

## The Vault Contents
If the players defeat the Drow and open the vault, they find the intact research cache of the Aethelgard scientists.
- Roll twice on Plunder Table B (The Smuggler's Hold).
- The centerpiece of the vault is a legendary artifact resting on a pedestal. The DM can choose to place either the **Crown of the Deep Mother** or the **Sextant of the Void** here, setting up a major plot hook for the next arc of the campaign.
# Chapter 50: The Leviathan's Wake (Levels 7+ Campaign Arc)

*This chapter provides a high-level campaign framework rather than a room-by-room module. It is designed to act as the climax to a Shattered Expanse campaign.*

## The Premise
The delicate balance of the Shattered Expanse is broken. Deep within the abyssal trenches, a **Slumbering Leviathan** (CR 25 Titan, Chapter 36) has awoken. The massive creature is slowly swimming toward the surface, leaving a wake of destruction, tsunamis, and displaced sea monsters in its path. Its target: a major capital city (choose either Bastion, Shipwreck Cove, or the Golden Flotilla based on the players' alliances).

The players are the only ones with a ship fast enough, and the reputation necessary, to do something about it.

## Phase 1: The Warning
The campaign arc begins with unnatural phenomena. 
- Tides rise unpredictably, swallowing small islands. 
- Ships are found completely crushed, not by cannons, but by sheer physical force. 
- The players' ship is attacked by a panicked **Kraken** (CR 21) that is fleeing from the Leviathan's territory. The players don't have to kill the Kraken, just survive long enough to realize it is running *from* something bigger.

## Phase 2: Uniting (or Plundering) the Factions
To fight a CR 25 Titan, a single party of adventurers is not enough. They need a fleet.
The players must travel to the faction capitals to secure alliances.
- **The Crimson Dominion:** High Admiral Vane will only commit his Dreadnoughts if the players assassinate a rival Pirate Lord who is blocking their supply lines.
- **The Spirit Keepers:** High Elder Naia believes the Leviathan is a natural purge. The players must pass a difficult Skill Challenge (Diplomacy, Knowledge [religion], Knowledge [nature]) to convince her the beast is corrupted by Aether and must be stopped.
- **The Consortium:** The Vance Brothers will fund the war effort, but only if the players sign a *Golden Contract* promising them exclusive salvage rights to the Leviathan's corpse.

*Alternative Evil Path:* If the players are ruthless pirates, they can use the Leviathan's approach as a distraction. While the Dominion fleet sails out to fight the beast, the players can infiltrate Bastion and rob the Aether-Forges blind.

## Phase 3: The Weapon
Conventional cannons will only annoy the Leviathan. The players must seek out a mythical precursor weapon capable of stunning or killing the beast. 
- They must delve into a highly dangerous, uncharted Aethelgard ruin to retrieve **The Gorgon's Figurehead** (Chapter 39). 
- To retrieve it, they must bypass its guardian: an ancient **Ghost Ship Apparition** (CR 8) crewed by the cursed precursors who originally built the weapon.

## The Climax: The Battle of the Wake
The Leviathan breaches the surface near the targeted capital city. The sky turns black with aether-storms, and the water boils.
- The players command the flagship of the united fleet. 
- The DM runs a massive, multi-stage combat encounter.
- **Stage 1 (Naval):** The players must maneuver their ship to get within range of the Leviathan while avoiding its 300-foot *Tidal Wave* attacks. Allied faction ships provide covering fire, taking out the *Leviathan Spawn* that swarm the waters.
- **Stage 2 (The Figurehead):** The players must successfully activate the *Gorgon's Figurehead*, petrifying the beast's outer shell to make it vulnerable, taking the curse's penalty to their own ship.
- **Stage 3 (The Boarding Action):** The players must physically board the petrified-but-still-alive Leviathan. They fight their way across its back to shatter the glowing Aether-node on its spine, dealing the killing blow in traditional party-vs-boss combat.

## Epilogue
If the Leviathan is defeated, the players are hailed as the saviors (or the new undisputed rulers) of the Shattered Expanse. Their ship becomes legendary, and the factions must rebuild in the wake of the destruction, setting the stage for a new era on the high seas.
